# Internal IPC contracts

Status: draft v0.3 (2026-11-20), revised after two independent security reviews ("approve with changes" each; every required change is applied, and §22 records the decisions taken on the open questions of v0.1). v0.3 replaces the MAC oracle of v0.2 with failure-capped verification (§5.3, §6.15). Written **before any implementation** (execution-phase.md, "Contracts before consumers"); §21 records what exists in code today and where the code differs. The installer parts (v0.4, 1E: §2 paths, §5.4, §6.4, §6.12, §6.13) were written before the installer code. Part 3 (v0.5, 2026-11-26: §23–§36, the repository link of the Phase 2 backup worker) was written before any Phase 2 code.

Three contracts between hash-xcreen's own server-side processes:

1. **`hx-privd` command set** (Part 1, §3–§9): what the unprivileged `hx-gatewayd` (and the root CLI and installer) may ask the root `hx-privd` to do, over a unix socket.
2. **Session link** (Part 2, §10–§22): how `hx-gatewayd` talks to `hx-session` inside the desktop container, over a unix socket exposed into the container.
3. **Repository link** (Part 3, §23–§36): how the Phase 2 backup worker reaches the backup repository on Google Drive through `hx-privd` and the Drive helper, and how a backup or restore job is run and supervised.

The client wire protocol is a third contract and lives in [protocol.md](protocol.md); this file only refers to it. Design background is in [architecture.md](architecture.md) §2, §4, §5, §8 and §9; the library that `hx-privd` drives is `crates/hx-provision` (its README, "Caller obligations for `hx-privd`", is binding for everything below).

Conventions: "MUST", "SHOULD" and "MAY" are used in the RFC 2119 sense. Integers outside protobuf are little-endian. Sizes are in bytes unless a unit is given. A "validation rule" is enforced by the receiver before it acts or allocates.

## 1. Trust model

### 1.1 Processes and principals

```
 client ⇄ (QUIC / WebSocket, TLS) ⇄ hx-gatewayd ──────────────┐
                                      │  privd.sock            │ Incus proxy device
 hx-agent CLI (root) ─── privd.sock ──┤  (§3)                  │ (Part 2, §11)
 installer    (root) ─── privd.sock ──┤                        ▼
                                      ▼                  hx-session (desktop user,
                                  hx-privd (root) ──► Incus      inside the container)
                                      │    unix socket
                                      ├─► update fetcher (sandboxed child)
                                      ├─► backup worker (helper container, no network)  ┐ Part 3:
                                      └─► Drive helper (systemd unit; holds the token,  │ every repository
                                          the only process that speaks HTTPS to Google) ◄┘ request passes privd
```

| Principal | Identity on the privd socket | Trust |
|---|---|---|
| `Gateway` | uid of the `hx-gatewayd` system user | Low. Internet-facing; assume it can be compromised through a parser bug. |
| `Admin` | uid 0 (the `hx-agent` CLI, the installer, root's own tools) | Full. Root on the host is outside the threat model (architecture §8.1). |
| anyone else | any other uid | Refused at connect; no response is sent. |

`hx-session` is not a privd principal at all. It talks only to `hx-gatewayd`, over the session link, and is treated as **hostile input** there (malware in the desktop runs as the same user).

### 1.2 What a compromised gatewayd must not achieve

| Goal of the attacker | Why it fails |
|---|---|
| Run code as root | No command executes caller-supplied programs, paths, arguments, environment, recipes or Incus configuration. Every target (instance, pool, device, file, URL) is derived by privd from constants and its own state (§7). |
| Reach other containers or the host's services | Only one hash-xcreen desktop exists per server in v1, named by privd. The only device privd ever attaches to a desktop is the link proxy device, whose host path privd computes (§7, rule 2); the few devices on a backup helper instance are a separate closed set (§7, rule 4, §24). The host firewall, ACL and sysctl are generated by privd and cannot be influenced by the caller. |
| Read the Drive token, the backup key or the recovery key | The Drive token is stored root-only and loaded only by the Drive helper; the backup key is stored root-only and loaded only by privd, which hands it to the worker for the length of one job; the recovery key is not stored at all, only its key id (§2, §34). Where a client must hand the Drive token or a recovery key to the server, it travels sealed to a key only privd holds (§5.2). The claim is deliberately limited to *reading*: whether gatewayd could *replace* a credential is closed separately by authenticating sealed secrets with an owner key that gatewayd cannot hold (§5.2, rule 3). |
| Exhaust the host | Every command is bounded: argument limits, per-command rate limits, a global concurrency cap, per-desktop locks, deadlines, disk guards (§8). |
| Switch off isolation silently | The only commands that reduce isolation are `EnableDocker` and `allow_private_networks`. `EnableDocker` is open to the Gateway role only while the setup window is open (the wizard's `--docker` path; explicit acknowledgement, roll back on failure, fail closed); afterwards it is Admin-only. `allow_private_networks` is Admin-only. Both are logged. |
| Persist as root | Updates are accepted only if signed by an embedded minisign key, newer than the running version, and read from a root-owned copy (§6.12). privd and root tools never open paths inside a directory gatewayd can write (§7, rule 15). |

**Residual power of a compromised gatewayd** (accepted, because gatewayd is the relay by design): it sees and can drive the desktop's screen, keyboard, clipboard and files; it can read and rewrite the device registry; it can start, stop and restart the desktop, change its limits within the bounds of §6.8, change the desktop user's password (outside the setup window only with the current password, §6.9), create a few manual snapshots, disable Docker, and trigger an update to an already-verified newer release; during an open setup window it can also enable Docker (after the acknowledgement flag). That ceiling is the contract of the `Gateway` command set; any new command must justify its place in it.

**Residual risk, stated plainly.** A gatewayd compromise that stays resident in memory can add its own device to the registry and so persist across restarts as a legitimate paired device, and can capture the PIN (and, in Phase 1, the Linux password) that users type. What limits it: PIN and password verifiers are peppered with a key only privd holds, so a stolen registry cannot be attacked offline, and online guessing goes through failure-capped verification in privd (§5.3); every registry change is recorded in privd's audit log through `RecordEvent` (§6.15) and shown to all paired devices; and recovery is to rotate the identity key and revoke every device (§2.1). This is not prevented by the privd command set, because the registry is gatewayd's by design (§2).

## 2. Where state and secrets live

Decision (architecture §8.6 made concrete). Reviewed and confirmed (§22, decision 1).

| Item | Location | Owner and mode | Who reads it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Device registry**: paired devices (public keys, names, created/last-seen), revocation state, the **peppered** PIN and Linux-password verifiers (§5.3) and failure counters, pairing-token state | `/var/lib/hx-gatewayd/registry` (the unit's `StateDirectory`) | `hx-gatewayd`, 0700 | `hx-gatewayd` only. The CLI reaches it through gatewayd's admin socket (§9), not by file. |
| Agent identity key and ACME account key | `/etc/hx/creds/*.cred`, encrypted with `systemd-creds` (TPM-backed where available); `identity.cred` is created by the installer's `credentials` stage (architecture §5.7) | root, 0600 | Delivered to gatewayd with `LoadCredentialEncrypted=` (tmpfs, visible to that unit only). gatewayd needs both: it terminates TLS and runs ACME. |
| **Drive refresh token** (Phase 2) | `/etc/hx/creds/drive-token.cred`, sealed as above | root, 0600 | Written by `hx-privd` (`SetBackupCredentials`; how privd seals it is open, §36 Q-H1); loaded only by `hx-drive.service` with `LoadCredentialEncrypted=drive-token:` (§24). privd never loads it and the backup worker never sees it. **Never** loaded into gatewayd. |
| **Backup repository key** (`BACKUP_KEY`, Phase 2): the host's password for the rustic repository | `/etc/hx/creds/backup-key.cred`, sealed as above. Generated on the server by the installer's `credentials` stage (the code path of the pepper, architecture §5.7); never transmitted | root, 0600 | `hx-privd` (`LoadCredentialEncrypted=backup-key:...` on `hx-privd.service`), and the backup worker for the length of one job, over the repository link (§34). **Never** loaded into gatewayd. |
| **Recovery key** (Phase 2): a second rustic key file for the same repository master key | Not stored. privd keeps only its rustic key id, in the backup state below | n/a | privd generates it, adds it to the repository and seals it to the owner key for the client (§6.14, §34). |
| Backup state: the server id, the job record per desktop (§35), the persisted "daily limit hit at T" time, the recovery key id | `/var/lib/hx/backup/` (`server-id`, `<desktop_id>/job.json`) | root, 0700; files 0600, written atomically | `hx-privd` only. |
| Backup runtime: one socket per active job, and the Drive helper's socket | `/run/hx/backup/<job>.sock` (directory root, 0700, from `systemd-tmpfiles`); `/run/hx/drive.sock` (`hx-drive.socket`, root, 0600) | root | `hx-privd`; the Drive socket also `hx-drive.service`, by socket activation (§24). Swept at privd start (§35). |
| privd's sealing key (§5.2): private half | `/etc/hx/creds/privd-recipient.cred` | root, 0600 | `hx-privd` only. |
| Pepper key for PIN and password verifiers (§5.3) | `/etc/hx/creds/pepper.cred`, created by the installer's `credentials` stage, which also migrates a plaintext `/var/lib/hx/pepper.key` (architecture §5.7). A release build (`release-build`) refuses to start without `$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY/pepper`: only development and test builds fall back to a self-generated `pepper.key`, and a stale one is logged as a warning | root, 0600 | `hx-privd` only; gatewayd gets MACs, never the key. |
| Enrolled owner public key (§5.2; Phase 2) | `/var/lib/hx/owner-key` | root, 0644 | `hx-privd`. Written only by `EnrolOwnerKey` (Admin). |
| Persisted rate-limit buckets (§8) | `/var/lib/hx/ratelimit` | root, 0600 | `hx-privd` only. |
| Update staging written by the fetcher | `/var/lib/hx/updates/`, a 300 MiB, 64-inode tmpfs (`var-lib-hx-updates.mount`, `nosuid,nodev,noexec`) | root, mode 1733 (the fetcher's dynamic user reaches it only through its unit's bind mount, `/var/lib/hx-update`; its uid is not known to privd and privd has no `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, so the files it creates are 0644) | The fetcher writes; privd treats the content as hostile and sweeps it before every fetch (§6.12). |
| privd's verified copies of artifacts | `/var/lib/hx/staging/` | root, 0700 | `hx-privd` only: the staged manifest and signature, and the artifact copy as an unnamed `O_TMPFILE`. |
| Update bookkeeping | `/var/lib/hx/updates-state` | root, 0600 | `hx-privd` only: highest manifest timestamp, the pending update, the last rollback (§6.12). |
| Image staging written by the image fetcher | `/var/lib/hx/imagein/`, an 80 MiB, 16-inode tmpfs (`var-lib-hx-imagein.mount`, `size=80M,nr_inodes=16,mode=1733,nosuid,nodev,noexec`) | root, mode 1733, reached by the fetcher only through its unit's bind mount `/var/lib/hx-image` (same reasoning as the update staging row) | The image fetcher writes; privd treats the content as hostile, copies it chunk by chunk and sweeps it before and after every job (§6.12). Separate from `updates/` so that the update path's host lock and `CreateDesktop`'s desktop lock never share a directory. |
| Offline image import | `/var/lib/hx/import/` | root, 0700, created by privd or the installer | `hx-privd`: the offline image file, `images-manifest-x86_64.json` and its `.minisig`, written by the installer (Admin only); neither gatewayd nor a fetcher can write there. Unlinked after a successful import (§6.5). |
| Image bookkeeping | `/var/lib/hx/images-state` | root, 0600, atomic | `hx-privd` only: highest image-manifest timestamp seen online, and the imported `hx-img-*` images (fingerprint, recipe, build id, import time) (§6.5, §6.12). |
| Image public keys | embedded in `hx-agent` from `apps/hx-agent/image-keys/{current,next}.pub` (`UNPROVISIONED` until the maintainer supplies them) | the binary | `hx-privd` (`privd::image::keys`) and the installer. A **separate** pair from the release keys (§6.12, architecture §13). |
| Setup window mirror and binding | `/var/lib/hx-gatewayd/setup` | `hx-gatewayd`, 0600, written atomically like the verifier store | `hx-gatewayd` only: `{window_opened, window_expires, setup_token_id, setup_device}` (§9, §5.5). Persisted so that a gatewayd restart does not forget an open window. |
| Installed releases | `/usr/lib/hx/<version>/`, with the `current` and `previous` symlinks | root, 0755 | Written only by `hx-privd` (`ApplyUpdate`, `RollbackUpdate`) and the installer; the units run `/usr/lib/hx/current/<binary>`. |
| privd policy (max desktops, host reserve, update channel, takeover limits) | `/etc/hx/privd.toml` | root, 0644 | `hx-privd` at start. Never writable or influenceable through the socket (the channel is changed by `Admin` through the CLI or the installer's `--channel`, which edit this file). |
| Desktop metadata (state, username, recipe id, Docker acknowledgement, created) | Incus instance config keys `user.hx.*` | Incus | privd. After creation privd reads the username and recipe from here and never trusts the caller to repeat them. |
| Linux password | Never stored by hash-xcreen. Set inside the desktop; only the shadow hash exists, in the container. gatewayd keeps a **peppered verifier** of it in the registry (§5.3), never the password. | n/a | n/a |
| Installer state: stages, normalized options (no secrets), what the installer created (architecture §5.7) | `/var/lib/hx/install-state.json` | root, 0600, written atomically | The installer and uninstall only. privd never reads or writes it. |
| Uninstall resume file | `/var/lib/hx-uninstall.json` | root, 0600 | `hx-agent uninstall`. Outside `/var/lib/hx` because uninstall removes that directory. |
| Install and uninstall lock | `/run/hx-install.lock` | root, 0600 | One `flock` shared by `install.sh`, `hx-agent install` and `hx-agent uninstall` (architecture §5.6). |
| Installer log | `/var/log/hx-install.log` | root, 0600 | Tool argv and exit codes, never secrets. |
| Rescue helper | `/usr/lib/hx-rescue/hx-privd-rescue` | root, 0755 | Run by `hx-privd-rescue.service` (§6.12). Outside `/usr/lib/hx` so that pruning never touches it. |
| The `hx-agent` command | `/usr/local/sbin/hx-agent`, a symlink to `/usr/lib/hx/current/hx-agent` | root | The operator (`sudo hx-agent …`). Created by the installer's `system-files` stage only if the name is free; removed by uninstall only while it is still that symlink (architecture §5.7). |
| Units installed by the installer | `/usr/local/lib/systemd/system/` (drop-ins in `/etc/systemd/system/<unit>.d/`) | root | systemd. Outside dpkg's namespace; embedded in `hx-agent` (architecture §5.7). |
| Link sockets | `/run/hx/link/` | root:`hx-gatewayd`, 0750 (§11) | gatewayd connects; it cannot create or replace entries. |
| privd socket | `/run/hx/privd.sock` | root:`hx-gatewayd`, 0660 | §3 |

Both hashes in the registry (PIN and Linux password verifier) are `HMAC(pepper, Argon2id(secret))` with the HMAC computed by privd (§5.3). Why gatewayd owns the registry: it is needed on the pre-authentication path of the internet-facing process and holds nothing that grants root (public keys and an Argon2id hash); putting it in privd would route pre-auth data through the root daemon and add a command surface for no isolation gain, because a compromised gatewayd already controls who gets in. Revocation also has to close live sessions, which only gatewayd can do. Consequences: the PIN is verified and its hash stored by gatewayd, so there is **no privd command for the PIN**; "store the PIN hash" (architecture §5.3, stage `verifiers`) is a gatewayd-local step.

### 2.1 Identity-key compromise and rotation

The agent identity key (Ed25519, architecture §8.2) is held by gatewayd because gatewayd terminates TLS. A gatewayd compromise therefore exposes it, and an attacker holding it can impersonate the server to any client that pins it. Procedure, to be implemented as `hx-agent rotate-identity` (Admin):

1. privd generates a new identity key, seals it to `/etc/hx/creds/identity.cred` (the old one is kept as `identity.prev` until step 4) and restarts gatewayd.
2. **Planned rotation** (key not believed compromised): gatewayd presents the new key signed by the old one for 7 days; clients that connect verify the signature, re-pin the new key and drop the old.
3. **After compromise**: no trust in the old key can be carried over. Every device is revoked (the registry is wiped by the CLI under the §7 rule 15 procedure) and each client re-pairs with a fresh token or the password verifier (§5.3, §9) and pins the new key. The client UI shows "server key changed" as a hard stop, never a click-through, unless the change is signed by the old key.
4. `identity.prev` is deleted after the overlap, or immediately after a compromise.

**Target design (deferred)**: a privd-backed signing oracle, so that the identity key never enters gatewayd's memory. gatewayd would implement a rustls custom `SigningKey` that sends each handshake signature request to privd over the IPC socket (rate limited, a few per second). That removes impersonation from the compromise ceiling at the cost of a new privd command and a latency step per connection; it is recorded in the Deferred list (§22), not specified here.

---

# Part 1: the `hx-privd` command set

## 3. Transport and peer authentication

- **Socket**: `/run/hx/privd.sock`, a unix stream socket created by systemd socket activation (`hx-privd.socket`: `ListenStream=/run/hx/privd.sock`, `SocketUser=root`, `SocketGroup=hx-gatewayd`, `SocketMode=0660`, `Accept=no`). privd inherits the fd, so it needs no filesystem permission to create it and the socket survives a privd restart (an update re-execs privd without dropping gatewayd's connection queue).
- **Runtime directories**: `/run/hx` (root:root 0755) and `/run/hx/link` (root:`hx-gatewayd` 0750, Part 2) come from `systemd-tmpfiles` (`d /run/hx 0755 root root -`, `d /run/hx/link 0750 root hx-gatewayd -`, and, for Phase 2, `d /run/hx/backup 0700 root root -`) so that they exist before any unit that needs them; the socket unit orders itself after `systemd-tmpfiles-setup.service`.
- **Peer authentication**: on every accept privd calls `getsockopt(SO_PEERCRED)`. The group bits on the socket are only a first filter. The allowlist is `{0, uid(hx-gatewayd)}`; the gateway uid is resolved by privd at start from the fixed user name compiled into the binary, never from a configuration value or from anything received over the socket. Any other uid is closed without a reply and logged. Where `SO_PEERPIDFD` exists the pid is recorded for the audit log; it is not used for decisions.
- **Self-checks at start** (privd refuses to start otherwise): the gateway user is in none of the groups `incus`, `incus-admin`, `docker`, `disk`, `sudo`, `wheel`, `adm`, `systemd-journal`, `lxd`, `shadow` or `kvm`, and has no supplementary group other than its own; no other user is a member of the `hx-gatewayd` group (membership would grant access to the privd socket and the link directory); the Incus socket is not readable by the gateway uid; `/run/hx/privd.sock` has the mode and owner above. The same checks re-run daily and after every update, and a failure sets `needs_attention=HOST_ACCOUNT_POLICY`.
- **No descriptor passing**: privd never uses received file descriptors. It reads every frame with `recvmsg` and closes the connection if the call returns any control message or `MSG_CTRUNC` (so a peer cannot make the kernel install a descriptor in privd silently, and the check is not defeated by a plain `recv`).
- **Units** (the installer installs them, architecture §5.7): `hx-privd.service` carries `OnFailure=hx-privd-rescue.service` and `StartLimitIntervalSec=300` with `StartLimitBurst=5`, which is at least `MAX_ATTEMPTS` + 1 (4) so that a failing update can reach its own rollback before systemd gives up (§6.12).
- **Roles**: the peer uid selects the role (`Gateway` or `Admin`) for the life of the connection. The role is never a field in a message. A command not allowed to the role is `PERMISSION_DENIED`.

## 4. Framing, messages and errors

**Framing.** A deliberate variation of `hx-proto`'s reliable-stream framing (`u32` little-endian length, then a protobuf message): the same length prefix and the same rule that a zero or oversized length, a truncated frame or a message that fails to decode closes the connection, plus one **kind** byte so that secrets can bypass the protobuf decoder (§5.1):

```
+----------------+---------+------------------------+
| length (u32 LE)| kind u8 | body (length - 1 bytes)|
+----------------+---------+------------------------+
```

`length` counts the kind byte and the body and is checked **before** anything is allocated.

| Kind | Body | Maximum `length` |
|---|---|---|
| `1` Message | one protobuf `Frame` (below) | 65 536 |
| `2` Secret | `request_id` (u64) + `slot` (u8) + raw secret bytes (§5.1) | 8 192 |

Protobuf (`prost`) is used because the rest of the project already uses it, it versions by field number, and the request/response messages are small. The messages are specified here by table; the schema is generated into the shared `hx-ipc` crate (§22, decision 9). Fields that only `Admin` may set are declared proto3 `optional`, so privd sees whether the caller *set* them: a `Gateway` request with such a field present, even at its default value, is `PERMISSION_DENIED`. Unknown fields are ignored; field numbers are never reused.

**`Frame`** (a `oneof`; exactly one variant, else invalid):

| Variant | Direction | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `Hello` {`major`, `minor`, `software` (≤ 64)} | caller → privd, first | Version handshake. privd requires the same `major` and answers `HelloAck`; a different major is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` and a close. Components ship from one release, so a mismatch means an update is half done and gatewayd restarts. |
| `HelloAck` {`major`, `minor`, `privd_version`, `phase` (§5.1), `role`} | privd → caller | `role` echoes the role privd derived, for diagnostics. |
| `Request` {`request_id` u64, `deadline_ms` u32, `secret_slots` (repeated `SecretSlot`), `command` (`oneof`, §6)} | caller → privd | `request_id` is chosen by the caller, non-zero, unique on the connection (a repeat is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`). `deadline_ms` may only shorten the command's maximum deadline (§8); 0 means the maximum. |
| `Cancel` {`request_id`} | caller → privd | Cancel the in-flight request; an unknown id is ignored. |
| `Ping` {`nonce` u64} / `Pong` {`nonce`} | both | Connection-level liveness (this replaces a `Ping` command). Answered at once, even while a request is running; at most 10 per second. |
| `Progress` {`request_id`, `stage`, `index`, `total`, `step` (≤ 128, sanitized), `percent` (0–100 or 255 for unknown), `ok`; reserved additive fields `bytes_done`, `bytes_total`, `transfer_bytes` (u64, 0 = unknown), set only for `BACKUP` and `RESTORE`, §33} | privd → caller | Zero or more per request, before its `Response`. |
| `Response` {`request_id`, `result` (`oneof`: a per-command result or `Error`)} | privd → caller | Exactly one per request. |

**Connection rules.** `Hello` within 5 s of accept, else close. The body of a frame must arrive within 5 s of its header. A connection idle for 10 minutes is closed (callers reconnect). privd allows **one request in flight per connection**; a second `Request` before the `Response` is `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`. `Cancel`, `Ping` and `Pong` are the only frames accepted while a request runs. Parallelism comes from several connections (§8). Requests are processed in arrival order per connection.

**Cancellation.** `Cancel`, a closed connection and an expired deadline cancel the running command at its next *safe point*:

- **Secret-carrying sections are shielded.** A section that handles a secret (`run_post_stage` with the password, `SetUserPassword`, the current-password check, `PepperVerify`, `PepperEnrol`) runs to completion, bounded by its own timeouts, and its work directory is swept; only then does privd answer `CANCELLED` or `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`. A cancel never lands between writing the secret file and removing it.
- **Everything else is cancellable, and cancel is explicit.** Dropping an `hx-provision` future does not stop an Incus background operation. On cancel privd therefore deletes the Incus operation it started (`DELETE /1.0/operations/<id>`) and **waits for it to end** (at most 30 s) before it runs the command's cleanup (§6.5 for `CreateDesktop`) and answers. If the operation will not end, privd answers `INTERNAL` and sets `needs_attention=STUCK_OPERATION` rather than start new work on the same desktop. *Verified on Incus 6.0.0:* a command started through the exec API (every step of the build and post stages) answers `DELETE /1.0/operations/<id>` with `400 This operation can't be cancelled`, so for those `Client::cancel_in_flight` reports the operation stuck. What ends it is a forced stop of the instance, which privd does for `CreateDesktop` anyway (it destroys the unfinished instance); the in-container `timeout` around each command bounds it otherwise. Image import and instance creation do cancel.
- A cancelled command never leaves state it did not own; the "converge" semantics below make a retry safe.

*hx-provision follow-up (1D):* a cancel-safe secret path (the post stage as one shielded unit) and an API that exposes the Incus operation id so that it can be cancelled (§21).

**Progress streaming.** Long commands (`SetupHost`, `CreateDesktop`, `EnableDocker`, `ApplyUpdate`, Phase 2 backups) emit `Progress` frames. They map `hx_provision::Progress` (`Started`/`Finished` with `stage`, `index`, `total`, `step`, `elapsed`, `ok`) one to one and add coarse stages not in that type:

`stage` ∈ `PREFLIGHT`, `HOST`, `IMAGE_FETCH`, `IMAGE_IMPORT`, `BUILD`, `LAUNCH`, `POST`, `DOCKER`, `SESSION`, `UPDATE`, `UNINSTALL`, `BACKUP`, `RESTORE`, `DONE`.

The Phase 2 jobs add three **reserved, additive** fields to `Progress`, `bytes_done`, `bytes_total` and `transfer_bytes` (§33), so that the restore screen can show bytes (architecture §9.8, item 6); they are absent (0) for every other stage, and older callers ignore them like any unknown field. The job kinds `CHECK`, `PRUNE` and `INIT` report under the stage `BACKUP`.

Progress is lossy and bounded: at most 10 per second per request, at most 256 buffered per connection; when the peer reads slowly privd keeps the newest and drops the rest. A step description is `hx-provision`'s own text (recipe-defined, never container output), still passed through `sanitize`. The `Response` is never dropped; if the peer will not read it within 10 s privd closes the connection (the command's effect stands).

**Error model.** `Error` {`code`, `detail` (command-specific enum, optional), `message` (≤ 256 bytes, sanitized, never contains secrets or unsanitized container output), `retryable` (bool), `retry_after_ms`, `stage` (optional: the failing stage of a long command, §6.5)}.

| `code` | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | A validation rule failed. `message` names the field. |
| `PERMISSION_DENIED` | The role may not call this command, or the command may not take this argument from this role. |
| `FAILED_PRECONDITION` | Wrong state: setup window closed, desktop not ready, `needs_attention` set, an uninstall in progress (`detail` `UNINSTALLING`, §6.13). `detail` says which. |
| `NOT_FOUND` | Unknown desktop or snapshot id. |
| `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` | Rate limit, concurrency cap, host headroom, pool space, desktop cap. Retryable with `retry_after_ms` where known. |
| `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` / `CANCELLED` | Deadline hit or `Cancel` received. |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | Incus (or another dependency) cannot be reached. Retryable. |
| `UNIMPLEMENTED` | Command reserved or not available in this release. |
| `INTERNAL` | A bug or unexpected dependency failure; the detail is in the journal, not in the message. For a failed recipe step the message holds the stage, the step number and kind and the exit status plus `details: journalctl -u hx-privd`, never the step's output (the container may have run user code); the output is in the journal. |

A *domain outcome* that the caller must act on is a successful `Response`, not an `Error` (for example Docker's `SelfTestFailed`, §6.9). Container-originated text is sanitized by `hx-provision` (control and ANSI characters removed, truncated) and is still untrusted: callers escape it for any UI.

## 5. Authorization and secrets

### 5.1 Command classes

privd authorizes by role and by **phase** (kept in `/var/lib/hx/privd-state`, root-only, one of `UNINITIALIZED`, `SETUP_OPEN`, `READY`; plus independent `needs_attention` flags per desktop, §6.9).

| Class | Gateway | Admin |
|---|---|---|
| `R` read-only | always | always |
| `A` routine (desktop operations) | when `READY`, or during `SETUP_OPEN` | always |
| `S` setup | only while `SETUP_OPEN`, with the restricted argument set | always, full argument set |
| `X` privileged | never | always |

`SetupHost`, `CreateDesktop` and **`EnableDocker`** are class `S`: after the window closes they are effectively class `X` (Admin only, for Docker through `hx-agent docker enable`; a later design may accept an owner-key-signed request from the app, which needs the owner-key enrolment of §5.2). `DisableDocker` stays class `A` because it only reduces exposure.

`SETUP_OPEN` is opened only by `Admin` (`BeginSetup`, called by the installer after it has verified and installed the agent), lasts **2 hours by default**, and can be extended only by `Admin` (`ExtendSetup`, up to 24 hours in total) or closed by `FinishSetup` (callable by Gateway or Admin once a desktop is `ready`). The window exists for the one-liner path, where the app drives the install over the paired channel (architecture §5.3, the stages from `desktop` onward) without gatewayd keeping standing power to create desktops or rebuild the host afterwards. **In the SSH install path the window is not needed for secrets**: the app sends the password and the Drive token over SSH to the root installer, which calls privd as `Admin` and writes the verifiers through the gatewayd admin socket (§9), so gatewayd never sees them. **How the window behaves, as implemented.** It is persisted with the phase, so a privd restart neither opens nor closes it; expiry is applied lazily, on the next request, and returns to the phase it was opened from (`UNINITIALIZED` or `READY`). The window is checked when a request is admitted, not while it runs: a class `S` command, or a password change without the current password, admitted just before expiry may wait up to about 10 s for the desktop lock and then complete after the window has closed. This is accepted, because admission is what the window controls. `BeginSetup` while a window is open restarts it for the requested time (Admin only; the 24-hour total of `ExtendSetup` counts from that restart), so Admin, not Gateway, decides how long setup power lasts. `FinishSetup` in `READY` is a no-op that succeeds. A Gateway class `A` request while `UNINITIALIZED` is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (`SETUP_WINDOW_CLOSED`), and a Gateway class `S` request outside the window is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` except `EnableDocker`, which is `PERMISSION_DENIED` (§7 rule 5). The window limits the damage from a gatewayd compromised *after* setup; it does not protect a compromise *during* setup, which is why the setup commands take only bounded, validated arguments anyway.

**What gatewayd calls during the window (the post-pairing setup, 1G; [protocol.md](protocol.md) §5.10, §5.5 below).** Nothing in privd's contract widens: gatewayd's setup coordinator calls only commands the Gateway role already has, with the arguments of the SSH path:

| Command | Class | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `GetHostInfo` | R | the window phase (admission of a setup connection) and the host facts shown in the app |
| `GetDesktop` | R | whether a usable desktop already exists (the `EXISTING` path) |
| `CreateDesktop` | S | the build; Docker inline when requested |
| `StartDesktop` | A | a stopped existing desktop |
| `SetUserPassword` | A | the existing-desktop path, without `CURRENT_PASSWORD`, only in the window (§6.9) |
| `PepperEnrol` (PIN, PASSWORD) | A | the verifier MACs (§5.3) |
| `FinishSetup` (without `force`) | A | the end of a successful job |
| `RecordEvent` | A | `PIN_CHANGED`, `PASSWORD_VERIFIER_CHANGED` |

Not exposed to the app: `SetupHost` (the `--pair` installer already ran it as Admin), a standalone `EnableDocker`, `pool_device`, `allow_private_networks`, `extra_host_addresses`, `ipv4`, `image_file` and `force`; `desktop_id` comes from gatewayd's configuration (`main`). **The job finishes setup itself** (`FinishSetup` at the end of a successful job), so the window and gatewayd's setup power close as early as possible and an app that crashes after success never leaves a window open. **A job is admitted only if at least 50 minutes remain in the window** (the 45-minute `CreateDesktop` deadline plus a margin; otherwise `WINDOW_TOO_SHORT` and the app shows `sudo hx-agent setup extend`); an admitted job runs to completion, because the window is checked at admission. **The expiry trap:** when the window expires before `FinishSetup`, privd returns to the phase it was opened from, which for a fresh install is `UNINITIALIZED`, where every Gateway class `A` command (`PepperVerify` included) is refused, so PIN logins fail. To close it, **`FinishSetup` called by Admin in `UNINITIALIZED` moves privd to `READY` when a ready desktop exists** (changed in 1G from "refused"; Gateway's `FinishSetup` in `UNINITIALIZED` stays refused). Without that, the Admin fix would have been `hx-agent setup begin` then `hx-agent setup finish`.

Stages the installer (root) performs itself, outside privd (architecture §5.3), because they change host packages, units and the host firewall policy, none of which privd's sandbox allows (`ProtectSystem=strict`; its `ReadWritePaths=` are `/etc/hx`, `/etc/sysctl.d`, `/run/xtables.lock` and `/usr/lib/hx`): installing or adopting Incus (the package manager; the pool, ruleset and sysctl file are privd's, §6.4), unpacking the first release, the units, their drop-ins and the unit files of the host firewall (`hx-host-firewall.service`, `hx-docker-user.service`), the sealed credentials, the rescue helper (§6.12) and the **agent port rule** in ufw or nftables (with, for those two backends, the rules that let the desktop's bridge reach the host's DHCP and DNS and forward, architecture §5.3). privd never opens a port and never writes a unit directory.

### 5.2 Secrets: how they travel and are held

Rules for every secret (Linux password, PIN-related MAC inputs, Phase 2 Drive token and recovery key; the backup key is generated on the server and never travels, §2): never in argv, environment, a file path, a log line, an error message, a `Progress` step or a protobuf message; never written to disk by privd except the declared swept work file inside the container (§6.9).

1. **Secret frames.** A request that carries secrets lists them in `secret_slots` and sends one **kind `2` frame** per slot straight after the `Request` and before processing begins. `SecretSlot` = {`slot` u8, `kind`, `sealed` bool, `length` u32, and fields **reserved now** for the Phase 2 sealed form: `upload_nonce` (16 bytes), `signer_key_id` (u32)}. privd reads these frames into `Zeroizing` buffers and excludes them from core dumps (`PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0` for the whole process, `RLIMIT_CORE=0`), without passing them through `prost`, so the protobuf decoder makes no copy of them. **Swap is prevented process-wide, not per buffer**: privd and gatewayd both call `mlockall(MCL_CURRENT | MCL_FUTURE)` at start (the unit sets `LimitMEMLOCK=infinity`; a failure to lock is fatal), because per-buffer `mlock` cannot cover the frame buffer the decoder allocates. Both **must read the privd and admin sockets with `Decoder::read_from` over a `NoCmsgReader`** (no intermediate user buffer). The copies of a secret that then exist, and nothing else: (1) the kernel's socket buffer, until `recvmsg` returns (outside the process, discarded by the kernel); (2) the decoder's frame buffer, filled directly by `recvmsg`, zeroized by `SecretFrame::from_body` right after the copy below, and zeroized on drop or poison if the frame is cut short; (3) the `Secret` (`Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>`, not `Clone`, redacting `Debug`), zeroized on drop. `hx-ipc`'s `tests/alloc.rs` asserts that decoding a kind-2 frame makes exactly the two allocations of (2) and (3). On the sending side `encode_secret` and `SecretFrame::encode_body` return `Zeroizing` buffers. What a caller does with `Secret::expose()` afterwards (the stdin file of `chpasswd`, the Argon2 input) is outside this crate and is swept by privd. (The kernel's socket buffers and the read buffer still hold the bytes briefly; the read buffer is zeroized after use, and the point of the design is that no *long-lived or pooled* heap copy exists, not that no copy ever exists.) Missing, extra, oversized or late (5 s) secret frames reject the request. The `Request` struct has no field that can hold secret bytes, and secret types have a redacting `Debug`.
2. **Where each secret goes.**

| `kind` | Accepted forms | Length | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `USER_PASSWORD` | plain, or sealed | 1–1 024 bytes, no `\n`, `\r` or NUL (the `hx_provision::Secrets::with_user_password` rules) | stdin of `chpasswd` via `hx_provision`'s 0600 file in the 0700 work directory (no argv, no environment), then swept |
| `CURRENT_PASSWORD` | plain | same | Checked against the desktop user's shadow hash before `SetUserPassword` outside the setup window (§6.9), through the same stdin-file path, then swept |
| `MAC_INPUT` | plain | exactly 32 bytes | The Argon2id output that `PepperVerify` compares and `PepperEnrol` MACs (§5.3) |
| `DRIVE_TOKEN` (Phase 2) | **sealed only** | at most 8 182 (the kind-2 limit of 8 192 minus the kind byte and the 9-byte `request_id` and `slot` header) | Stored as the sealed credential `drive-token.cred` (§2) and loaded only by the Drive helper; it never reaches the backup worker (§23, architecture §9.4) |
| `RECOVERY_KEY` (Phase 2) | **sealed only** | at most 8 182 | The user's recovery key, supplied when restoring onto a new server. Held by privd only for the job and handed to the worker as repository slot 0 (§34); never stored |
| `BACKUP_KEY` | **withdrawn**: no longer a client-supplied kind | n/a | The backup key is generated on the server (§2). The kind number stays **reserved** and is never reused; a request that carries it, sealed or plain, is `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (§7, rule 9) |

3. **Sealed secrets (Phase 2; the shape is fixed now so that nothing changes later).** The Drive token and the recovery key (`RECOVERY_KEY`) are sealed by the client to privd's recipient key and **authenticated by an owner key**, so that gatewayd can neither read them (confidentiality) nor substitute its own (integrity). The same rules apply to both kinds: owner-authenticated, bound to the kind and the desktop, with a nonce issued by privd. Format: HPKE (RFC 9180) **Auth mode** (`mode_auth`) with DHKEM(X25519, HKDF-SHA256), HKDF-SHA256 and ChaCha20-Poly1305; the sender's static key is the **owner key**; `info` = `hx-privd-secret/v2`; `aad` = `len(desktop_id)` (u8) ‖ `desktop_id` ‖ `kind` (u8) ‖ `upload_nonce` (16 bytes); plaintext = `not_after_unix_s` (u64) ‖ value. (A signature by the owner key over the same fields plus the ciphertext is an acceptable alternative construction; the requirement is the properties, below.)
   - The **owner key** is a keypair held by the user's client. Its public half is enrolled in privd **outside gatewayd's control**: by `EnrolOwnerKey` (Admin) called by the installer over SSH during setup, or by `hx-agent owner enrol` (not implemented yet; planned with the Phase 2 sealed-secret work) on the server. privd refuses a sealed secret that is not authenticated by the enrolled owner key. Without an enrolled key, `SetBackupCredentials` is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` and the Drive token must be set through the CLI. **Enrolment is also a prerequisite for the recovery key**: privd seals the recovery key it generates to the owner key (§6.14, §34), so without an enrolled owner key the app cannot show it, and a restore onto a new server needs the owner key enrolled there for `RECOVERY_KEY` to be accepted.
   - The **`upload_nonce`** is issued by privd (`BeginSecretUpload` {`kind`, `desktop_id`} returns 16 random bytes; one-time, bound to kind and desktop, expires in 10 minutes, consumed on first use). It makes replay of a captured box useless, and the length-prefixed `desktop_id` stops ambiguity between ids.
   - privd rejects a box whose `not_after` has passed.
   - **Delivery of privd's recipient public key** (`GetRecipientKey`, §6.2): over SSH where the SSH install path is used (authentic, because the SSH host key was confirmed); otherwise through an authenticated hook in the pairing exchange, which belongs to 1B and is the remaining dependency for the one-liner path.
   In Phase 1 only the Linux password exists. It may travel plain: gatewayd then sees it in memory in transit in the one-liner path (decision Q2, §22) and never in the SSH path.
4. **Gateway-side obligations.** `hx-gatewayd` holds a password or PIN only between receiving it from the client and the privd response, in `Zeroizing` memory, with no copy into logs, metrics or panics (a panic hook redacts). gatewayd runs with `PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0` and `LimitCORE=0` (and `MemoryDenyWriteExecute`, `ProtectProc=invisible` where compatible), so an unprivileged process cannot ptrace it or read its memory, and a crash writes no core. If gatewayd restarts mid-install the app resends the password, and only to start a new job; nothing is stored for resuming. Precise hold times in the one-liner setup (§5.5): the **PIN** is held from its receipt on the setup connection until `PepperEnrol` answers; the **password** is first used for Argon2id and `PepperEnrol(PASSWORD)` (the record is kept in memory, not stored), then handed to privd as `CreateDesktop` secret slot 0 (or the `SetUserPassword` slot) and wiped as soon as the secret frame has been written, which needs a privd-client variant that takes ownership of the secret (if that variant cannot be built, holding it until privd answers, up to 45 minutes, still meets this rule). **Nothing is cached** across a disconnect, a job end or a restart: watching or resuming a running job needs no secret, and a new job needs a new `start` that carries the password.
5. **Process hygiene.** privd and the workers it spawns are started with a cleared environment and fixed argv from constants (never through a shell); nothing derived from a secret appears in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`, `environ`, a unit file or the journal. This is what Gate 1's "no secret in any command line, log or process list" checks. **One-liner token (decision 14, §22; owner 1E and the wizard):** the one-liner carries **no token** and runs the installer with `--pair`. The installer prompts for it on `/dev/tty` with hidden input and hands it to gatewayd as the `CreatePairingToken` secret frame (§9). `--token` in argv remains only for unattended use and is documented as weaker: `sudo` writes the argv to the auth log and the journal, and the shell keeps it in its history; the token is single-use and expires after 15 minutes, which bounds but does not remove that exposure. Gate 1 applies to the installer's own argv, so the interactive prompt is the default the wizard produces. The installer's own secrets in the SSH path arrive on its stdin, in the framing of §5.4, not in argv.

### 5.3 Verifier hashes and the pepper

Decision (§22, decision 1). gatewayd keeps **two verifiers** in the registry, both of the form `HMAC-SHA256(pepper_purpose, Argon2id(secret, salt))`:

- the **PIN verifier**, used on every connection (architecture §8.3);
- the **Linux-password verifier**, set whenever the password is set through hash-xcreen (`CreateDesktop`, `SetUserPassword`, the installer in the SSH path), used for re-pairing after a lockout (architecture §8.3: "re-pairing needs the password") and for the web client's first login (Phase 3). A password changed inside the desktop with `passwd` makes the verifier stale: re-pairing by password then fails, and the user resets it with `hx-agent desktop password --verifier-only` (Admin; it recomputes the verifier).

The Argon2id step runs in gatewayd (it has the CPU budget and the parameters live in `hx-crypto`). The pepper step runs in privd and is split in two commands, so that **no command returns a MAC to a caller that could use it as a guessing oracle**:

- **`PepperVerify`** (§6.15) answers only *yes or no* for a given input and expected verifier, and **counts failures globally per purpose**. This is the only operation gatewayd performs on a login path.
- **`PepperEnrol`** (§6.15) returns the MAC, only so that a verifier can be set or changed; it is limited to a few calls a day for Gateway after setup.

A stolen registry (or a memory dump of a past gatewayd) therefore cannot be attacked offline without privd's pepper, and an *online* attack, even from a fully compromised gatewayd holding the registry, is limited to the failure caps below: a 6-digit PIN or a dictionary password cannot be guessed at any useful rate. The pepper is per purpose (`pin`, `password`), derived with HKDF from `/etc/hx/creds/pepper.cred`.

**When gatewayd may call `PepperVerify`.** For the PIN, only after the device has authenticated with its pinned key in the TLS handshake (so only the holder of a paired device key can cause a PIN check, and gatewayd's own per-device lockout of 5 failures applies first, architecture §8.3). For the password, only inside a re-pair or web-login flow that gatewayd already rate limits per source address. Successful verifications are free; they do not count against any cap. **Keeping the last attempt from locking the purpose.** gatewayd is privd's only PIN caller, so it counts in memory the `PepperVerify(PIN)` calls privd answered *no* to in the last hour (a gatewayd restart empties the count, which only weakens this guard and never locks anyone). Attempts 1 to 4 of a device's lockout cycle are always sent. The 5th, which revokes the device, is sent too unless `charged_in_last_hour + 1 >= the hourly cap` (the cap is `privd::limiter::failure_caps(Pin).0`, 10, the same constant privd enforces); then it is judged wrong locally without calling privd (protocol §11.4, rule 4a), and still revokes. Two stolen device keys without the PIN thus charge at most 9 of the 10, and the purpose is not locked; three or more can lock it with their earlier attempts, which is accepted and which `Admin` lifts with `ResetLockout`. An owner with four typos is not affected.

**Failure caps (global per purpose, persisted across restarts, §8):** `PIN` 10 failures per hour and 50 per day; `PASSWORD` 5 per hour and 20 per day. A `CURRENT_PASSWORD` check (§6.9) has its own cap of 20 checks per day and charges every failure to the same `PASSWORD` budget. Past a cap that purpose is **locked**: every `PepperVerify` for it answers `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` (detail `LOCKED`), `needs_attention` gets `PIN_LOCKED` or `PASSWORD_LOCKED`, the event is audited, and only `Admin` can lift it (`ResetLockout`, §6.13).

**Trade-offs, stated.**
- The `PASSWORD` failure cap is a lockout lever that an internet attacker can pull once web login exists (Phase 3): enough wrong password attempts through the web login lock the password re-pair and web-login path until an Admin resets it. Only that path is locked (device-key PIN login and existing sessions continue to work), and the cost is accepted in exchange for making password cracking impractical. The PIN cap cannot be pulled by an unauthenticated attacker, because `PepperVerify` for the PIN is only reached after device-key authentication.
- PIN checks depend on privd's availability: while privd re-execs during an update, a login can fail briefly (`UNAVAILABLE`, retry); gatewayd tells the client to retry and does not count it as a wrong PIN.

**Enrolment from the setup connection (1G, one-liner path).** gatewayd produces both verifiers itself, with exactly the parameters, purposes, record format (`hx-pv1$m=..,t=..,p=..$salt$mac`) and `all` target of the SSH path (`PinParams::DEFAULT`; PIN: 6 to 16 ASCII digits, `hx_crypto::Pin::from_bytes`; password: 1 to 511 bytes of UTF-8 with no NUL, CR or LF, checked by the one shared `hx_proto::message::password_problem`):
- **PIN:** Argon2id in gatewayd, `PepperEnrol(PIN)`, `VerifierStore::set_pin(None /* all */)`, then `RecordEvent PIN_CHANGED`. It succeeds once: a PIN can be set only while none exists.
- **Password, inside the setup job (§5.5):** (1) compute the verifier record first (Argon2id, `PepperEnrol(PASSWORD)`) and hold it in memory; (2) send the password to privd as `CreateDesktop` slot 0, or as the `SetUserPassword` slot on the existing-desktop path; (3) store the record only after the desktop step succeeded, then `RecordEvent PASSWORD_VERIFIER_CHANGED`. A password that was never accepted by the desktop therefore never leaves a verifier behind.
The difference from the SSH path, stated: gatewayd sees the password in memory while it passes through (decision Q2, §22). It is `mlockall`ed, non-dumpable, never logged and held only in `Zeroizing` buffers.

**Regression tests required (1C):** (1) a loop of wrong `PepperVerify` guesses hits `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` and then the `LOCKED` state within the cap, and the lock **survives a privd restart**; (2) correct verifications never consume the failure budget; (3) `PepperEnrol` from Gateway after setup is refused past its daily cap, allowed more often during `SETUP_OPEN`, and never limited for Admin; (4) no command returns a MAC except `PepperEnrol`.

### 5.4 Installer secret input (`--ssh`)

Contract between the installer (`hx-agent install --ssh`, architecture §5.2) and the app's SSH driver; the secrets never appear in argv, environment or a file. Stdin carries:

```
"HXI1" | { kind u8 | len u16 LE | bytes[len] }* | kind 0, len 0
1 USER_PASSWORD     1..=511 bytes, no \r \n NUL                  required
2 PIN               6..=16 ASCII digits                         required
3 PAIRING_TOKEN     hxt1-…, at most 1024 bytes                  optional: the app's own token, used right after the install
4 OWNER_PUBLIC_KEY  32 bytes                                    optional: EnrolOwnerKey (Phase 2 shape)
5 DRIVE_TOKEN       reserved: exit 2 in Phase 1
```

- The password cap of 511 bytes is libxcrypt's limit (`hx-pwcheck` and `crypt_rn` refuse longer input), so a longer password could never be checked later.
- At most 8192 bytes in total, each kind at most once, the whole input within 30 s. EOF before the end record, or any byte after it, is exit 2.
- Bytes are read straight into `Zeroizing` buffers, never into a `String`; afterwards `/dev/null` is duplicated onto fd 0. Stdin must not be a tty, and `--json` is implied.
- Where each goes: the password to `CreateDesktop` (slot 0), through Argon2id and `PepperEnrol(PASSWORD)` to `SetPasswordVerifier`; the PIN through Argon2id and `PepperEnrol(PIN)` to `SetPinVerifier{all}`; the token to `CreatePairingToken` (a secret frame) as the **last** stage, so its 15-minute life starts after the desktop build; the owner key to `EnrolOwnerKey`.
- `install.sh` must be run from a file in this mode (not `curl | sh`) and never reads stdin itself. If `sudo -S` needs a password, the app sends it before the `HXI1` magic and sudo consumes it; that is the app's concern (1G).

### 5.5 Gateway setup coordinator (the one-liner path, 1G)

The client side of this is [protocol.md](protocol.md) §5.10; this section is what gatewayd does behind it. The decisions are recorded in §22 (decision 20).

**Components** (`apps/hx-agent/src/gateway/setup/`): `SetupCoordinator` (implements the authenticator's `SetupGate`; `on_window_open/extend/close`, `on_token_installed(id)`, `on_paired(device, PairedWith)`, `on_revoked(device)`, `serve(conn, device, pin_verified)`, `start`, `cancel`, `finish`) and `SetupStore` (`/var/lib/hx-gatewayd/setup`, §2).

**Window and binding** (persisted):
- `Closed` becomes `Open{expires, token_id: None, device: None}` when Admin opens the window (§9 `SetupWindow`). The mirror lives in gatewayd's memory today and is lost on a restart; the store fixes that.
- When `CreatePairingToken` arrives on the admin socket **while the window is open**, gatewayd records the token's public 8-byte id as `setup_token_id`. A pairing **code** never binds (40 bits of entropy; codes belong to the interactive path).
- When a device pairs with `Token{id}` equal to `setup_token_id` while the window is open, it becomes `setup_device` and `setup_token_id` is cleared. A previously bound device loses the binding and its live setup connection is closed with `SETUP_CLOSED`; Admin decides by issuing tokens. Any other device that pairs is ordinary: it cannot log in until a PIN exists, then logs in normally (the PIN is `all`), and it can never open `INTENT_SETUP`.
- The binding is cleared on `FinishSetup`, when the window closes or expires, and when the bound device is revoked (a revocation also cancels a running job).
- To recover (a lost or revoked app device), rerun the one-liner; its `pairing-token` stage is never skipped.

**Admission** of `INTENT_SETUP` ([protocol.md](protocol.md) §5.10): the device must be the bound setup device, gatewayd's persisted mirror must say the window is open, and privd's `GetHostInfo.phase` must be `SETUP_OPEN` (privd is the authority; unreachable is `Refuse{BUSY}`). The gate and the registry are checked again immediately before the connection is authorized.

**One job, on its own privd connection.** The job is owned by the coordinator, not by the client connection, and runs on a **dedicated privd connection outside the pool** (the pool holds 2 connections, the dedicated lane 1: 3 of privd's 4 Gateway connections; if `max_connections` ever becomes configurable the lane must count against one shared gateway budget). **The lane is shared**: server management's `change_password` (§5.6) uses the same lane, one call at a time, instead of a second one (which would take the fourth and last Gateway connection, leaving no slack for a restart overlap or a reconnect race); a password change that finds the lane busy with a setup job waits at most its own 90 s bound and then answers `UNAVAILABLE`. The lane's `call_streaming(command, secrets, deadline, progress)` forwards `Progress` frames (today's client discards them) and wipes the secrets once their frames are written; `cancel(request_id)` sends a privd `Cancel` frame on the call's connection, and the call then resolves with privd's `CANCELLED` answer (implemented as `PrivdLane::call_streaming`, which returns a `LaneCall` carrying the request id, and `PrivdLane::cancel`; dropping the call, or stopping gatewayd, closes the connection, which privd treats as a cancel). A client disconnect does not affect the job. Only one job runs at a time (a second `start` is `BUSY`); `start` after an ended job is allowed unless setup is finished.

**Job steps.**
1. `PREFLIGHT`: compute the password verifier record (§5.3), kept in memory.
2. `GetDesktop(main)`. **Usable** (ready, running, stopped or starting): the username, distro and preset must match the request, else the job fails with `DESKTOP_EXISTS`; a stopped desktop is started; then `SetUserPassword` with slot 0 and no current password (the window allows it, §6.9). The result has `path_taken = EXISTING`, and Docker status comes from `Desktop.docker` (`NOT_ENABLED` if it was requested but is off). **Otherwise** (`NOT_FOUND`, creating or failed): `CreateDesktop` on the dedicated lane with the full 45-minute deadline and progress forwarded. `GetDesktop` is what tells "registered and usable" from the rest, because a resumed `CreateDesktop` on a ready, registered desktop returns success **without** setting the password (§6.5).
3. `VERIFIERS`: store the password verifier, then `RecordEvent`.
4. `FINISH`: `FinishSetup`, retried up to 3 times on `UNAVAILABLE` (2 s apart). On success the mirror is closed and persisted and the binding cleared. On `SETUP_WINDOW_CLOSED` the result is `SUCCEEDED` with `finished = false` and a warning naming `sudo hx-agent setup finish`, which is enough because Admin `FinishSetup` in `UNINITIALIZED` moves privd to `READY` once a ready desktop exists (§5.1).
5. Publish `SetupResult`. On failure the in-memory record and the password are dropped.

**Cancel** takes effect only while a privd command is running (privd destroys the unfinished instance and answers `CANCELLED`); later it is acknowledged `OK` and the result shows what actually happened.

**gatewayd restarts or crashes mid-build.** The dedicated connection closes, so privd cancels at a safe point and destroys and unregisters the instance (the post stage is shielded). Recovery is **reconnect and converge**: the restarted gatewayd reloads `SetupStore` (job `IDLE`); on reconnect the state is `{IDLE, desktop NONE}`; the app asks for the password again and a new job starts. If the crash came after `CreateDesktop` succeeded, the new job takes the `EXISTING` path (`SetUserPassword`, verifier, finish). The rejected alternative, a job owned by the connection, would destroy up to 20 minutes of build on every network blip or laptop sleep and burn privd's `CreateDesktop` budget of 3 per hour.

**Limits at the gateway.** At most 2 requests per second per connection (burst 8), else close `BUSY`; `start` at most 6 per hour per server (privd's persisted 3 `CreateDesktop` per hour is the hard stop); `set_pin` succeeds once, with at most 10 attempts per hour if `PepperEnrol` is unavailable; one live setup connection; control writes bounded to 5 s, a client that does not read is closed `BUSY` and the job continues; an idle setup connection with no running job closes after 10 minutes. Request fields are validated against static caps (cpu at most 1024, memory at most 16 777 216 MiB, disk at most 65 536 GiB; host bounds are privd's, §6.8); Docker without the isolation acknowledgement is refused.

**Progress mapping.** privd `ProgressStage` maps 1:1 to `SetupStage` for `PREFLIGHT`, `IMAGE_FETCH`, `IMAGE_IMPORT`, `BUILD`, `LAUNCH`, `POST`, `SESSION`, `DOCKER` and `DONE`; `VERIFIERS` and `FINISH` are gatewayd's own stages. `step` is sanitized again. Updates are coalesced (latest wins, at most 4 per second); a stage change is sent at once.

**Error mapping** (privd code and detail to `SetupErrorCode`):

| privd | `SetupErrorCode` |
|---|---|
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | `INVALID_ARGUMENT` |
| `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, detail `DESKTOP_EXISTS` | `DESKTOP_EXISTS` |
| `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, detail `POOL` | `INSUFFICIENT_SPACE` |
| `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, detail `SETUP_WINDOW_CLOSED` | `WINDOW_CLOSED` |
| `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, detail `ISOLATION_NOT_RESTORED` | `ISOLATION_NOT_RESTORED` |
| `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` | `RATE_LIMITED`, or `LOCKED` when the detail is `LOCKED` |
| `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` | `TIMED_OUT` |
| `CANCELLED` | `CANCELLED` |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `UNAVAILABLE` (retryable) |
| `UNIMPLEMENTED` | `UNSUPPORTED` |
| anything else | `INTERNAL` |

privd's `output_tail` is **not** forwarded to the app; Docker results carry a status, versions, the found and required Incus versions, whether isolation was restored and a hint of at most 256 bytes. A retry of Docker after `SELF_TEST_FAILED` during setup is possible only afterwards, through the Admin CLI (`hx-agent docker enable`), because the job finishes setup itself.

### 5.6 Server management (gatewayd, 1G)

The client side is [protocol.md](protocol.md) §5.11 (`ServerMgmt`, feature `SERVER_MANAGEMENT`); this section is what gatewayd does behind it (`apps/hx-agent/src/gateway/server_mgmt.rs`). **Nothing in privd's contract widens**: every command used is class `A` or `R` and already allowed to the Gateway role with exactly these arguments.

| Request | gatewayd calls | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `get_overview` | `GetHostInfo` (R), `GetDesktop` (R) | bounded; cached update status; no network |
| `change_pin` | verifier check (Argon2id here, `PepperVerify(PIN)`), then `PepperEnrol(PIN)` (A, 5 a day for Gateway after setup), then `SetPinVerifier` locally: `{device}` if the caller has a PIN of its own, else `{all}` | the current PIN is checked by the authenticator's own PIN path (`check_pin_for`: per-device guard, counted first, shared backoff, 5th wrong revokes before the answer); no server-wide lock, so one device's check never waits for another's. Only `change_password` is serialized server-wide (one shared secret, one global privd budget) |
| `change_password` | `SetUserPassword` (A) with slots `USER_PASSWORD` and `CURRENT_PASSWORD` (the second is what makes it Gateway-allowed outside the window), then `PepperEnrol(PASSWORD)` and the verifier store | privd's PASSWORD budget applies; privd goes first so that a mistyped current password cannot use up `PepperEnrol`'s 5 a day; if the verifier cannot be refreshed afterwards the answer is `OK` with a warning `message` (the password did change) |
| `restart_desktop` | `RestartDesktop` (A) | |
| `set_docker` off | `DisableDocker` (A) | |
| `set_docker` on | **nothing**: `ADMIN_ONLY` | `EnableDocker` is class `S` (§5.1, §7 rule 5) |
| `set_limits` | `SetLimits` (A) | bounds are privd's (§6.8) |
| `check_update` | `CheckUpdate` (A) | result cached for the overview |
| `apply_update` | `ApplyUpdate` (A) | privd's own rules: no downgrade, 2 started a day (Gateway only), a rolled-back release not re-applied by Gateway for 24 h |
| every mutation | `RecordEvent` (A): `PIN_CHANGED`, `PASSWORD_CHANGED`, `PASSWORD_VERIFIER_CHANGED`, `DESKTOP_RESTARTED`, `DOCKER_DISABLED`, `LIMITS_CHANGED`, `UPDATE_APPLIED` | the requesting device's id, never a secret; the last five are additive `AuditEvent` values (8 and up in `privd.proto`) |

**Secrets.** A PIN or password is copied into `Zeroizing` memory when the request is read and the message is dropped; the current PIN lives until the verifier answers, the new PIN until `PepperEnrol` answers, the new password until `SetUserPassword`'s secret frame is written (the owned-secret path of the dedicated lane is used so the plaintext is wiped as soon as the frame is on the socket), the current password likewise. Nothing is cached or logged and no error carries a value.

**The PIN check is shared with the login.** `ChangePin` uses the registry's per-device counter (`begin_pin_attempt`, `record_pin_success`, `refund_pin_attempt`) and the same `PrivdPinVerifier`; a 5th wrong PIN is revoked through `Authenticator::revoke` (reason `TooManyPinFailures`, by `Lockout`), so the sessions close and the audit and `DeviceEvent` follow the login's path. The wait after a wrong PIN is `backoff_after(failures)` kept in memory by gatewayd for this request family (the login's own wait is private to the authenticator); a request that arrives sooner is answered `BACKOFF` and not counted.

**Limits.** Per device over all connections: overview 30 a minute, every other request 10 a minute. privd's limits (SetLimits 6 an hour, `RestartDesktop` 6 a minute per desktop, `CheckUpdate` 6 an hour, `ApplyUpdate` for Gateway: 2 started a day and 10 refused an hour, none for Admin, `SetUserPassword` 10 an hour) are mapped to `RATE_LIMITED` with `retry_after_s`.

**Error mapping** (privd to `ServerStatus`): `INVALID_ARGUMENT` to `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; `PERMISSION_DENIED` on `SetUserPassword` to `WRONG_PASSWORD`, on `ApplyUpdate` to `UPDATE_REFUSED`, otherwise `ADMIN_ONLY`; `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` with detail `LOCKED` to `LOCKED`, otherwise `RATE_LIMITED`; `FAILED_PRECONDITION` with detail `DESKTOP_NOT_READY` to `DESKTOP_NOT_READY`, with `SETUP_WINDOW_CLOSED` to `UNAVAILABLE`, otherwise `FAILED` (for `ApplyUpdate` `UPDATE_REFUSED`); `UNAVAILABLE` and timeouts to `UNAVAILABLE`; `UNIMPLEMENTED` to `UNSUPPORTED`; anything else to `FAILED`.

## 6. Commands

Common to every command: arguments are parsed into typed values at the boundary (parse, do not re-validate later; the typed value is what is used) and an argument not listed is ignored. **Converge** means the command drives the system to the described state and succeeds if it is already there. All mutating commands are written to the audit log (§8) with role, command, desktop id and outcome, never with argument values that could be secret.

Shared types:

- `DesktopId`: `^[a-z][a-z0-9]{0,11}$`. v1 allows exactly one desktop and the installer uses `main`. privd maps it to the instance name `hx-<id>`; the caller never supplies an instance name (hx-provision README, "Names and paths stay inside privd").
- `Limits` {`cpu` u32, `memory_mib` u64, `disk_gib` u64}; bounds in §6.8. `limits.processes` is not settable (always `DEFAULT_PROCESSES` = 8192, never unlimited).
- `Distro` ∈ `ubuntu-24.04`, `ubuntu-26.04`, `debian-12`, `debian-13`; `Desktop` ∈ `xfce`; `Preset` ∈ `basic`, `developer` (the `hx_provision::{Distro, Desktop, Preset}` enums; unknown values are `INVALID_ARGUMENT`).
- `Username`: `hx_provision::Username::new` (1–32 characters of `^[a-z_][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$`, reserved names refused).

### 6.1 Liveness (frames `Ping` / `Pong`, not a command)

Handled at the connection level (§4); no request, no class, no role check beyond being a served peer.

### 6.2 `GetHostInfo` (R, both)

Returns the facts the app and the installer's preflight show: `privd_version`, `phase`, OS id and version, arch, CPU count, total and available memory (MiB), pool {exists, driver, total and free GiB}, Incus version (`Client::server_info`), `docker_on_host`, `host_firewall_loaded` (table `inet hx` present), `sysctl_ok`, `needs_attention` list, and whether a desktop exists. Read-only; deadline 10 s. Failure: `UNAVAILABLE` if Incus is down (the other fields are still returned in the success body with `incus` marked absent).

**`GetRecipientKey`** (class R, both) {} returns `{key_id, public_key (32 bytes, X25519), owner_key_enrolled}`: the key to which sealed secrets are encrypted (§5.2). It involves no secret; the client must obtain it through an authenticated path (SSH, or the pairing hook), not trust the copy gatewayd relays. **Until sealed secrets exist (Phase 2) there is no recipient key and it answers `UNIMPLEMENTED`.**

### 6.3 `BeginSetup` / `ExtendSetup` / `FinishSetup`

- `BeginSetup` (class X) {`expires_in_s` 60 to 86 400, default **7 200 (2 hours)**}: Admin only; `UNINITIALIZED` or `READY` to `SETUP_OPEN`.
- `ExtendSetup` (X) {`extend_by_s` 60 to 86 400}: Admin only; the total lifetime of one window never exceeds 24 hours.
- `FinishSetup` (A) {}: Gateway or Admin; `SETUP_OPEN` to `READY`; requires that at least one desktop in state `ready` exists unless called by Admin with `force`. **Admin's `FinishSetup` in `UNINITIALIZED`** (the window expired before setup finished, §5.1) moves privd to `READY` when a ready desktop exists; Gateway's stays refused.

All converge. The window state is also mirrored into gatewayd through the admin socket (§9) so that gatewayd itself refuses setup traffic from clients when no window is open (defence in depth; privd is the authority).

### 6.4 `SetupHost` (class S)

Purpose: what architecture §4.2 and §5.3 (stage `host`) require on the host, except the steps that belong to the root installer (installing Incus, the units and the agent port rule, §5.1): `ensure_host` (pool, bridge, ACL, profiles), the sysctl file, the nftables host ruleset and the Docker `DOCKER-USER` rules.

| Argument | Type and validation | Who |
|---|---|---|
| `pool_size_gib` | u32, 12–200 (`MIN_POOL_GIB`/`MAX_POOL_GIB`); default `default_loop_size_gib(free)`; also at most 60% of the free space of the Incus storage directory's filesystem at call time. Maps to `PoolSource::LoopFile`. | Gateway, Admin |
| `pool_device` | A block-device path under `/dev/`. privd itself verifies, as root, what the README demands of the installer: it is a block device (not a symlink target of a non-device), a whole disk, not mounted, no mounted or in-use partitions, no filesystem or RAID/LVM signature (`blkid -p`), not the device holding `/`. Maps to `PoolSource::Device`. | **Admin only** |
| `allow_private_networks` | bool, default false. Maps to `HostSpec::allow_private_networks`; reducing isolation, so installer option only. | **Admin only** |
| `extra_host_addresses` | up to 16 IPs. | **Admin only** |
| `ipv4` | `auto` or `a.b.c.d/nn` (`/16`–`/29`, a private range). Default `auto`. | **Admin only** |

privd does:

1. `HostSpec`: `Naming::production()` (never a caller prefix), `host_addresses` = **every** address of every interface except our own bridge, enumerated by privd over netlink (public, private, Docker bridge gateways), plus `extra_host_addresses`; `HostSpec::validate()`.
2. `hx_provision::ensure_host`. Idempotent; nothing is deleted.
3. Take `HostReport::host_firewall` (privd calls `nft_host_protection` itself; the text is never accepted from a caller), check it with `nft -c -f -`, load it with `nft -f -` (the ruleset on stdin), and **write** it to `/etc/hx/host-firewall.nft`. The unit that loads that file at boot, `hx-host-firewall.service` (a oneshot, ordered before `incus.service`), is **installed and enabled by the installer**: privd cannot write unit directories under `ProtectSystem=strict`. Done when `nft list table inet hx` shows the table.
4. Write `/etc/sysctl.d/90-hx-incus.conf` from a constant template (`fs.inotify.max_queued_events`, `max_user_instances`, `max_user_watches` = 1048576; `vm.max_map_count` = 262144) and apply it with `sysctl -p` on that file (architecture §5.2 and §5.3, stage `host`; not `sysctl --system`, which would apply every other sysctl file of the host); verify by reading the values back.
5. If a host Docker is present (the `DOCKER-USER` chain exists): ensure the two accept rules for our bridge (`iptables -C` then `-I`, idempotent). `hx-docker-user.service`, ordered after `docker.service`, re-applies them at boot; like the firewall unit it is installed by the installer, not by privd. If the rules cannot be applied, the response says so (`docker_user_rules: FAILED`) and `needs_attention` gets `HOST_DOCKER_FORWARDING`; the command still succeeds, because the desktop works without internet and the installer decides.

privd also re-runs steps 1 to 3 on its own at start and, after a 30 s debounce, whenever a host address appears or disappears (the README requires `ensure_host` to be re-run when `host_addresses` change). That is internal behaviour, not a command.

Output: `{pool, network, acl, desktop_profile, compat_profile` (each `Ensured`: created/existed/updated)`, gateway_ip, firewall_loaded, sysctl_applied, docker_user_rules}`. Idempotent (converge). Deadline 5 minutes. Progress stage `HOST`. Failure: each step is independently idempotent, so a failed `SetupHost` is simply called again; the error names the step (`detail` ∈ `POOL`, `NETWORK`, `NFT`, `SYSCTL`, `DOCKER_USER`). Incus down is `UNAVAILABLE`.

### 6.5 `CreateDesktop` (class S)

Purpose: create the desktop container, from a prebuilt image or by building on the server (architecture §4.3), through to `ready`.

| Argument | Type and validation |
|---|---|
| `desktop_id` | `DesktopId`. Refused if a desktop already exists (v1 cap, from `privd.toml`, default 1) unless this is a retry of the same id still in state `creating`/`failed`. |
| `distro`, `desktop`, `preset` | enums as above. |
| `username` | `Username`. |
| `image_mode` | `AUTO` (default), `PREBUILT`, `BUILD`. `PREBUILT` fails instead of falling back (below). |
| `limits` | `Limits`, bounds in §6.8; omitted fields get privd defaults (all host CPUs; memory = host RAM minus the reserve; disk = 80% of the pool). |
| `docker` | bool, default false (the wizard's `--docker`). If true, `docker_isolation_acknowledged` MUST also be true and the Docker flow of §6.9 runs after the post stage. Like every `CreateDesktop` call from Gateway it is possible only while the setup window is open. |
| `settings` | `hostname` (RFC 1123 label), `timezone` (a name present in `/usr/share/zoneinfo`), `locale` (`^[a-z]{2,3}_[A-Z]{2}(\.UTF-8)?$` from an allowlist), `keyboard` (`^[a-z]{2}(\+[a-z0-9_-]{1,16})?$`). Implemented in `hx-provision` (`Settings`, `Catalog::resolve_with`; the recipe's post steps apply them, and an unset field does nothing). privd builds `Settings` from the request, checks the time zone with `Settings::with_timezone_in(.., "/usr/share/zoneinfo")`, and passes it to `resolve_with`. |
| `image_file` | **Admin only** (proto3 `optional`, §4): a **file name** matching `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$` inside the fixed directory `/var/lib/hx/import/` (root 0700), not a path; the signed manifest sits beside it as `images-manifest-x86_64.json` with its `.minisig`. Implies the offline prebuilt path, with no fallback. Gateway: `PERMISSION_DENIED`. |
| secret slot 0 | `USER_PASSWORD`, required (plain or sealed, §5.2). |

privd does:

1. Authorize (class S), take the `desktop:<id>` lock, check the phase and the desktop cap, check free pool space against a minimum (≥ 8 GiB free and ≥ 15% free).
2. Load the recipes that are **embedded in the privd binary** (`Catalog::embedded()`, an `hx-provision` follow-up, §21; recipes are then covered by the agent's signature and no directory is read at all); **never** `Catalog::from_dir`. `Catalog::resolve_for(RecipeId, addons, Some(&username))`.
3. Choose the image path with `privd::image::choose` (architecture §4.3 has the rationale; each outcome has a `reason_code`):
   1. `BUILD`: build, `BUILD_REQUESTED`. `image_file` given: the offline path below; any failure is an error with no fallback (`IMAGE_FILE`).
   2. No image key embedded: `AUTO` builds (`NO_IMAGE_KEY`), `PREBUILT` fails `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (detail `IMAGE`).
   3. The `images-manifest` fetch job fails: `AUTO` builds (`LIST_UNAVAILABLE`), `PREBUILT` fails `UNAVAILABLE` (retryable).
   4. Signature, trusted comment, schema or timestamp rules fail (§6.12 "Images manifest"): `AUTO` builds (`LIST_UNVERIFIED`, with an audit notice), `PREBUILT` fails `FAILED_PRECONDITION`. The highest timestamp is persisted only after full acceptance.
   5. No entry matches (recipe, the digest of the **embedded** recipe, `min_agent` at most this agent's version): `NO_MATCHING_IMAGE`. The entry is older than 30 days: `IMAGE_STALE`. Both build in `AUTO`; `PREBUILT` fails `FAILED_PRECONDITION`.
   6. A cached image matches (below): skip the download but still run the disk check of step 7.
   7. **Disk**, with S = `size` and U = `unpacked_size`, read with `statvfs`: free on the filesystem of `/var/lib/hx` at least S + 1 GiB (staging); free on the filesystem of `/var/lib/incus` at least S + 1 GiB (Incus's copy), or 2S + 2 GiB when both are one device; pool free at least U + 8 GiB, plus the 15% rule of step 1. Failure: `AUTO` builds (`DISK`), `PREBUILT` fails `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (detail `POOL`).
   8. **`AUTO` only, speed probe** (at most 30 s; a fetcher job, because privd has no IP networking, §6.12). With `r_rel` and `r_mir` in bytes per second: release probe failed, build, `PROBE_FAILED`; S / `r_rel` above 900 s, build, `DOWNLOAD_TOO_SLOW`; mirror probe succeeded and `r_rel` below 0.5 × `r_mir`, build, `SLOWER_THAN_MIRROR`; otherwise prebuilt, `PREBUILT_FASTER`. A failed mirror probe alone still allows prebuilt. `PREBUILT` skips the probe (`PREBUILT_REQUESTED`). The constant 0.5 comes from S3 (a build costs 1.5 to 3.5 extra CPU minutes for a download of about the same size) and is revisited against installer-CI timings.
   9. Prebuilt: `IMAGE_FETCH` (20 minute sub-deadline) then `IMAGE_IMPORT` (verified bytes through `Client::import_image`, below), `LAUNCH`, `POST` and a restart. In `AUTO`, a failure **before the post stage begins** destroys the instance and deletes the image if it was imported; if at least 25 of the 45 minutes remain, privd builds on the server (`FELL_BACK`, the reason names the failed stage). A failure during or after the post stage is an ordinary error, because a build would run the same post stage. `PREBUILT` never falls back; its error carries the stage.
   The base image for the build path is a constant remote alias (`ImageSource::Remote` with a constant server, never a caller value). The mirror used by the probe is the distro default of the base image, not the host's sources (`MIRROR_PROBE_HOSTS`, §6.12).
4. `DesktopSpec::for_plan(name = "hx-<id>", Naming::production(), &plan, limits)`, `compat_profile` from `needs_compat_profile(&plan)`, then `orchestrate::finish_prebuilt` (prebuilt, with `DesktopSpec::with_image(ImageSource::LocalFingerprint(fp))`) or `orchestrate::build_on_server`. **Both paths end with a restart and a wait for `running`** (`finish_prebuilt` gained it in 1D, because security updates replace libraries under running services and the account rename restarts the user manager). The post stage runs through `run_post_stage` (which sweeps `/run/hx-provision` before and after) with `Secrets::with_user_password`. The call must never be `apply_stage(.., Stage::Post, secrets, ..)` directly (README).
5. Write instance metadata: `user.hx.state=creating` first, then `user.hx.username`, `user.hx.recipe`, `user.hx.created`; `user.hx.state=ready` **last**, after steps 6 and 7 succeed.
6. Provision the session link (§6.11) and install `hx-session` (§6.11), so the version matches the agent. If the host has no `hx-session` package for the distro yet (a development install before the packaging of 1E and 1F), the install is skipped with an entry in `warnings` and the desktop still becomes `ready`; `InstallSession` installs it later.
7. Optional Docker (§6.9).

Idempotency and failure: a retry with the same `desktop_id` and arguments while the desktop is `creating` or `failed` destroys that unfinished instance (`destroy_instance`; it holds no user data yet) and starts again; a `ready` desktop with the same arguments that privd has registered returns success with its current report (`reason` "the desktop already exists with these arguments", nothing changes, the password is not set again); a `ready` desktop with the same arguments that privd has **not** registered is **adopted**: this is the desktop that `uninstall --keep-desktop` left behind, whose registry entry (`/var/lib/hx` is removed) and session link are gone. privd registers it again, sets the password given in this request in the desktop (the same shielded `change_user_password` as `SetUserPassword`, without a current password, so that the password verifier the installer writes next matches the Linux account), provisions the session link and installs the session again (the same warning when the host has no `hx-session` package), and answers with `reason` "adopted the existing desktop"; if a step fails it unregisters the desktop again so that a retry adopts it afresh; with different arguments, `FAILED_PRECONDITION`.
