All of that is already within its power (it controls the screen and receives the keystrokes anyway); the client treats remote clipboard and links as untrusted (architecture §8.7). |
| Send malformed or oversized data, or flood | Bounded by the §13 limits, the §19 validation and rate limits; a violation drops the link and does not affect gatewayd's other duties. |
| Send decodable-but-malicious video or cursor data | Reaches the client's decoder. Two layers: gatewayd checks the Annex B structure and the SPS dimensions of key frames (§15.1), and the client decodes in a sandbox with a `max_pixels` limit and is fuzzed (1G/1H; §22, decision 8). |
| Reach anything else through the socket | Nothing: the socket is a byte pipe to one gatewayd connection (§11); there is no descriptor passing, and the link carries no authority (no secrets, no path to privd). |

## 11. Transport and socket placement

**Decision: an Incus proxy device, with gatewayd dialling out and `hx-session` listening on a systemd-created socket in a root-owned directory.**

```
 host                                                     container (unprivileged, isolated idmap)
 hx-gatewayd ──connect──► /run/hx/link/main.sock  ⇄ forkproxy ⇄ /run/hx-session/link.sock ◄── hx-session.socket (systemd)
   (uid hx-gatewayd)       (listening socket made by the          (dir root:root 0755; socket owned by the desktop
                            proxy device, owner hx-gatewayd 0600)  uid, 0600)           └─ fd handed to hx-session.service
```

- **Container side**: a fixed path, `/run/hx-session/link.sock`, in a directory that is **root-owned (0755)**, so the desktop user, and therefore malware running as it, can neither rename, delete nor replace the socket path, nor swap the directory for a symlink. The socket is created by a systemd **socket unit** inside the container (`hx-session.socket`: `ListenStream=/run/hx-session/link.sock`, `SocketUser=<desktop uid>`, `SocketGroup=<desktop gid>`, `SocketMode=0600`, `DirectoryMode=0755`, `Accept=no`) and handed to `hx-session.service` (a system unit with `User=<desktop uid>`, which reads the descriptor with `sd_listen_fds`; a system unit is required because only system socket units support `SocketUser=`, and it also means no `loginctl enable-linger` is needed for the daemon). **`hx-session` must never launch user-facing programs.** The unit has `KillMode=control-group`, so anything it starts dies with it, and it runs outside the user's login session; if the daemon ever has to start an application it uses `systemd-run --user` or asks the X session, never `fork`/`exec` of its own. A side benefit: systemd holds the socket while `hx-session` restarts, so connections queue instead of failing, and the service starts on the first connection. No other account in the container can connect (root in the container can; root there is part of the container's trust domain).
- **Host side**: `hx-privd` adds one proxy device per desktop (`ProvisionSessionLink`, §6.11): `bind=host`, `listen=unix:/run/hx/link/<desktop_id>.sock`, `connect=unix:/run/hx-session/link.sock`, host-side socket owned by the gatewayd uid and gid, mode `0600`, **`security.uid` and `security.gid` set to the desktop user's ids (mandatory)**, so the container-side connect runs as that user and not as the container's root. **Both are required**: with only `security.uid` the connect arrives as gid 0, and with neither it arrives as uid 0 (spike 1C controls). A stopped container or a removed device leaves a stale socket file on the host whose connect is refused (`ECONNREFUSED`), and a connect that the proxy cannot complete on the container side is reset right after it is accepted, so gatewayd treats both as "session not available" and redials (§19.2). `/run/hx/link/` is root:`hx-gatewayd` 0750, so gatewayd can connect but cannot create, rename, replace or delete entries; no other host user can reach it.
- **No fallback to the container's root.** If `security.uid`/`security.gid` do not behave as specified on a supported Incus version, implementation stops and the design is reopened. Falling back to a root-identity connect would make the forkproxy a root-in-container actor that follows whatever the container presents at the path, and is rejected.
- **Direction**: gatewayd connects when it needs the desktop (first authorized client) and `hx-session` accepts. Reasons: (1) the gateway fully controls how many connections exist, which keeps its attack surface at a fixed number of connections per desktop instead of an accept loop that malware can hammer; (2) a gatewayd restart needs no cleanup in the container; (3) `hx-session` idles at about zero cost with no link, and streams only while one exists.
- **Why a proxy device, not a shared directory or a socket bind-mounted from the host.** The proxy copies bytes between two sockets it owns, so the container never obtains a kernel-level handle to a host-created socket: no `SCM_RIGHTS` descriptor passing into gatewayd, no peer-credential games, no dependency on how the isolated idmap shows host owners. A bind-mounted directory would also give the container a path on the host filesystem.
- **Peer identity.** There is none to authenticate on the container side: `SO_PEERCRED` of an accepted connection shows the proxy process, not the gateway, and anything running as the desktop user could connect too, which gives it nothing beyond what it already has (§10). gatewayd does not authenticate `hx-session` either: the host socket path proves "this is the proxy device privd created for this desktop", and the handshake (§12) proves nothing beyond the protocol version. Neither side's decisions rely on the identity of the other. gatewayd reads the link with `recvmsg` and treats any control message or `MSG_CTRUNC` as a violation (the same rule as privd's, §3).
- **Verified first in 1C implementation, on real Incus 6.0.0 with an isolated idmap** (decision 10, §22; evidence in [spikes/1c-proxy-device.md](spikes/1c-proxy-device.md): items 1 and 3 hold; items 2 and 4 hold except the sub-items listed next, which were not run). Not yet run, to repeat on the first real VPS: the host reboot and Incus daemon restart of item 2, and the unprivileged-user-namespace variant of item 4 (the spike host's `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1` forbids creating one). The checklist stays as the regression list: (1) `uid`, `gid`, `mode` of the host-side socket and `security.uid`/`security.gid` of the connect side behave as above; (2) connections work after the container is stopped and started and after a host reboot; (3) throughput at 20 Mbps with frames up to the configured cap; (4) **the symlink and replacement test**: as the desktop user inside the container, attempt to unlink, rename or replace `/run/hx-session/link.sock` and `/run/hx-session`, to place a symlink at the path or in front of it, to bind a different listener at the path while the socket unit runs, and to bind-mount over it (unprivileged user namespaces inside the container included); every attempt must either fail or leave the proxy connecting only to the systemd-created socket as the desktop user, and the test must also confirm that nothing the container does makes the proxy connect to a host path.
- **Rejected alternatives**: a TCP port on the bridge (the bridge's nftables rules and ACL deliberately forbid desktop-to-host traffic; opening a hole for this would weaken the isolation both layers exist to provide); `/dev/incus` or the guest API (`security.guestapi=false` on every desktop); vsock (containers have none; only VMs); a socket in the desktop user's `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (the user controls that path, the original v0.1 placement).

## 12. Connection lifecycle

### 12.1 Lanes

One link is **three** unix connections (lanes) to the same socket path, so that large or slow data never blocks small urgent data. Each lane is a separate byte stream through the proxy.

| Lane | Id | Direction of bulk traffic | Carries |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ctl` | 1 | both, small messages | handshake, control, input, mouse motion, cursor, frame feedback, cancels, statistics, events |
| `media` | 2 | session → gateway | video frames, audio (reserved) |
| `bulk` | 3 | both | clipboard and file transfers |

The gateway to session direction of `media` is unused; a byte received there is a violation.

### 12.2 Preamble and versioning

Each lane starts with an 8-byte preamble from the connecting side (the gateway), followed by a 16-byte `link_id` (random, chosen by the gateway per link, identical on the three lanes). The session answers with the same 8 bytes:

```
 0       4        5        6      7
+--------+--------+--------+------+------+----------------+
| "HXLK" | major  | minor  | lane | 0x00 | link_id (16 B) |   gateway → session
+--------+--------+--------+------+------+----------------+
```

The answer has the first 8 bytes only, with `minor` lowered to the session's if that is lower. Rules:

- The link version is `1.0`. Same major, different minor is accepted; the negotiated minor is the lower one, and a newer minor never sends a message to a peer whose negotiated minor predates it (the rule of protocol.md §4.2). A different major is refused by closing the connection; gatewayd then records `session_outdated` for the desktop and asks privd for `InstallSession` (an update replaced the agent but not the in-container binary).
- `hx-session` accepts at most **one link** (one `link_id`) at a time with at most one connection per lane. A new `link_id` on a `ctl` lane **supersedes** the old link: the old lanes are closed and the session behaves as for link loss (§12.4). A duplicate lane of the current link is closed.
- The gateway opens `ctl` first and waits for the session's `info` (§14.2); it opens `media` and `bulk` after that. If the session does not see both extra lanes within 5 s of `ctl` it drops the link. Preamble and `info` must complete within 5 s of connect, else the gateway closes.
- A malformed preamble, wrong magic, a `lane` outside 1 to 3 or a non-zero reserved byte closes the connection (on the gateway: a violation, §19.2).

### 12.3 Liveness

- The gateway sends `ping` {`nonce`} on `ctl` every 2 s while a link exists; the session answers `pong` within 1 s from its control thread (never from the media path). 10 s without a `pong` and no other `ctl` traffic: the gateway closes the link (a fault, §19.2).
- While streaming, the session also sends `stats` about once per second, which doubles as a heartbeat. An idle desktop produces no frames by design (architecture §6.1), so frame silence is not a liveness signal.
- The session closes the link and stops streaming if it receives nothing from the gateway for 15 s.

### 12.4 Link loss and restart

When the link closes for any reason the session, within 1 s: stops capture and encoding (idle cost about zero), injects **`release_all`** (every held key and button up), aborts transfers, discards queued output, and returns to listening for a new link. It never exits because the link ended. The gateway treats a lost link while a client is connected as `INTERNAL_ERROR`: it sends the client `Goodbye`, then redials with backoff, and the client reconnects to a fresh stream (video restarts from an IDR regardless).

## 13. Framing and limits

After the preamble every lane carries frames:

```
+----------------+---------+------------------------+
| length (u32 LE)| kind u8 | body (length - 1 bytes)|
+----------------+---------+------------------------+
```

The same shape as §4, so one reader implementation serves privd and the link. `length` counts the kind byte and body and is checked against the table **before** allocating. An unknown `kind`, a `kind` not valid on that lane or in that direction, a zero or oversize `length`, a frame truncated by a close, or a protobuf body that fails to decode is a **violation** (§19.2). An unknown *protobuf variant or field* inside a valid message is ignored and counted (forward compatibility).

| Lane | Kind | Direction | Body | Max `length` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ctl` | `0x01` `SessionToGateway` | S → G | protobuf (§14) | 327 685 (the 327 680-byte cursor limit of protocol.md §3.1: a 256 × 256 RGBA shape is 262 144 bytes plus protobuf overhead; plus the kind byte) |
| `ctl` | `0x02` `GatewayToSession` | G → S | protobuf (§14) | 16 384 |
| `media` | `0x10` Video | S → G | media header (20) + payload (§15) | `max_frame_bytes` + 21: 4 194 325 by default (4 MiB), configurable up to the protocol's 16 777 237 (16 MiB) |
| `media` | `0x11` Audio | S → G | `seq` u32, `timestamp` u32, Opus packet 1 to 1 100 | 1 110 (reserved, §15.4) |
| `bulk` | `0x20` TransferOpen | both | protobuf `TransferOpen` {`id` u32, `channel` (CLIPBOARD or FILE), `header` = protocol `TransferHeader`} | 4 200 |
| `bulk` | `0x21` TransferData | both | `id` u32 + raw bytes (1 to 65 536) | 65 541 |
| `bulk` | `0x22` TransferEnd | both | `id` u32 | 5 |
| `bulk` | `0x23` TransferAbort | both | `id` u32 + `code` u32 (protocol.md §8 transfer codes) | 9 |

Message-level limits (checked by the **gateway** for S → G and by the session for G → S):

Repeated fields are **counted on the wire before the message is decoded** (`info.codecs` at most 4, `frame_feedback.results` at most 64), so a 327 685-byte `ctl` body of empty elements cannot make the decoder allocate ten times its size; the remaining allocation per frame is bounded by the frame limit itself and measured in `hx-ipc` `tests/alloc.rs`.

| Item | Limit |
|---|---|
| Strings (`software`, event `message`, `goodbye` `message`) | 128, 256, 256 bytes; control and ANSI characters removed before display or logging |
| `stats` | 4 per second; every counter a bounded integer |
| Cursor shape | at most 256 × 256, `rgba.len == width * height * 4`, hotspot inside the image (protocol.md §5.4); at most 20 shapes per second and 4 MiB of shape data per second |
| Cursor position | coalesced by the gateway to at most one per 1 ms (latest wins); never queued unboundedly |
| `event` | 10 per second |
| Video frame payload | at most `max_frame_bytes` (default 4 MiB, configurable up to the protocol's 16 MiB; deliberately below the protocol limit, because the link carries one frame at a time from a hostile source and an IDR at 20 Mbps is far smaller; the session's encoder must stay under it); at most 2 × the negotiated `max_fps` frames in any one-second window (the excess is dropped); at most 4 IDR frames per second (the excess is dropped); more than 4 × the frame limit in attempts within one second is a flood and a violation, because dropping costs CPU per frame |
| Transfers | at most 4 concurrent per direction; one clipboard transfer per direction at a time (a new one aborts the previous); clipboard at most 64 MiB; file at most `max_file_bytes` (default 4 GiB, configurable lower, enforced in both directions; the protocol follow-up of §21 puts it in protocol.md); chunks at most 64 KiB; unread data at most 1 MiB per transfer and 4 MiB per link |
| Input G → S | `InputEvent` at most 1 024 (protocol.md §3.1); input queue 4 096 events |
| Memory per link on the gateway | video pool of `max(2 × max_frame_bytes, 12 MiB)` (**always at least one maximum-size frame, in fact two**), counting every queued or staged frame as its payload plus 256 bytes of overhead and shared by the consumer queue and the staging queue (§15.1); plus at most 8 MiB for `ctl` and `bulk` buffers (cursor shapes queued for a consumer that does not read are limited to 4 MiB of them, latest wins). **Worst case: pool + two frames in flight** (the one the media reader is assembling and the one the staging task holds while it waits for a `stream_format`, each at most `max_frame_bytes`) **+ 8 MiB**: 32 MiB at the default cap (12 + 2 × 4 + 8 + overhead), 72 MiB at the 16 MiB ceiling (32 + 2 × 16 + 8) |
| `stream_format` | at most 4 per second; `width` and `height` each 1 to 16 384, `width * height` at most 33 177 600 (7680 × 4320); `fps` 1 to 240; `codec` in the negotiated set; `first_frame` within 8 behind and 1 024 ahead of the newest frame number received (serial arithmetic); an out-of-range value drops the message and is counted |
| `cancel_frames` | at most 8 per second; `up_to_frame` must not be newer than the newest frame number the gateway has received and not more than 256 behind it; otherwise ignored and counted |
| `info` | accepted once per link, as the first `ctl` message; `display` 1 to 16 384 per side; `features` unknown bits ignored; a second `info` is a violation |
| `pong` | a `pong` with a nonce the gateway did not send, or a second one for the same `ping`, is ignored and counted; more than 10 per second is a violation |
| Byte budget per lane (sustained, burst = 2 s) | `ctl` S → G 6 MiB/s (shapes 4 MiB/s of it), `ctl` G → S 1 MiB/s, `media` S → G 16 MiB/s with an IDR burst allowance of `max_frame_bytes`, `bulk` 64 MiB/s per direction. **Beyond the budget the gateway stops reading that lane** (the kernel buffers fill and the session's writes block: backpressure) instead of reading and discarding it. A frame is read whole and charged afterwards, so the bucket goes **negative** by at most one frame and reading resumes only when refills have paid the debt (debt is never forgiven: a bucket that saturated at zero let frames larger than the bucket run at line speed). A `ctl` or `media` lane whose bucket stays empty (at or below zero, never recovering above a quarter of the burst) for 3 consecutive seconds while the peer still has data waiting (the socket is actually readable) is a violation (§19.2); a pause caused by downstream flow control (a slow client) is not. **`bulk` is backpressure only: an empty bucket stops the reader and the writer blocks, never a violation**, because an honest transfer from a fast disk legitimately offers more than 64 MiB/s. Both ends of `bulk` instead **pace their own sends** to 48 MiB/s (three quarters of the budget, `hx_ipc::link::budget::BULK_SEND_RATE`, one token bucket per lane shared by all transfers, 10 ms of burst), so the receiver's bucket normally never empties and a transfer takes the same time either way. The byte budget still bounds the receiver's work; the frame-count limit still applies. |
| Frame count per lane (bytes do not bound per-frame CPU) | Per second, all kinds together: `ctl` S → G 2 000 (cursor positions at most 1 000 plus about 50 of the capped classes, with headroom), `ctl` G → S 4 000 (input 2 000, motion 1 000, feedback and stats), `bulk` 2 048 per direction (a 64 MiB/s transfer is 1 024 chunks of 64 KiB), `media` audio 200 (reserved; 100 per second plus headroom). `ctl` frames that decode to no known variant: at most 10 per second (a negotiated peer sends none). **Exceeding any of these is a violation** (§19.2), unlike the message-class limits above, where only the excess is dropped, with one exception: frames are counted when they are *read*, and a reader that stalled for a moment (the session's `ctl` reader flushing XTest input) sees the gateway's queued frames as a burst. The **session's `ctl` G → S** limit is therefore a soft 4 000 per second (counted and logged, never closing the link) with a hard ceiling of 20 000 per second (`CTL_FRAMES_FROM_GATEWAY_HARD`, five times the legitimate peak, covering a stall of up to the gateway's 2 s write-stall bound). The gateway's own limits keep their meaning. Values are `hx_ipc::link::budget::limits`.

**The gateway stays inside the `ctl` G → S budget by construction.** A client must never be able to make the session see a violation (that would count as a session fault, §19.2, and ends in `RestartSession` and `session_unavailable`). In any sliding second one client can cause at most: input 2 000 sustained + 500 burst, motion 1 000 (the link's coalescer), `frame_feedback` 120 (one message per 10 ms, batched, plus the link layer's `DROPPED` reports, batched at most one message per 50 ms however many frames are dropped or keyframes requested), `reconfigure` 11 (a client's `ViewConfig`s are coalesced, latest wins, one per 100 ms), `transport_stats` 4, `client_stats` 4 (the gateway forwards at most 4 a second of what a client sends), `request_idr` 2, `ping` 1, and about 10 for `start`, `stop` and `goodbye`: 3 652 against the soft limit of 4 000. `hx_agent::gateway::relay::MAX_CTL_FRAMES_PER_SECOND` states the sum and a compile-time assertion keeps 300 frames of margin. The session coalesces `reconfigure` as well (latest wins) instead of closing the link when its engine queue is full. |

## 14. Control lane (`ctl`)

Messages are protobuf. They reuse the `hx.v1` types of protocol.md wherever the content is the same (`InputEvent`, `CursorEvent`, `ClientStats`, `TransferHeader`, `VideoCodec`), so field semantics are not defined twice. The new messages below belong to a package `hx.link.v1` (schema location: decision 9, §22).

### 14.1 Gateway to session: `GatewayToSession` (`oneof`)

| Variant | Fields | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `start` | `epoch` u32, `config` (`StreamConfig`) | Begin streaming for a client. The session creates or resumes the pipeline, replies `stream_format`, and its first frame is an IDR flagged `RESYNC`. `epoch` identifies the client (§18); the controller resets on `start`. |
| `stop` | `epoch` | Stop capturing and encoding within 1 s (the last client left). Keeps listening; clears held keys. |
| `reconfigure` | `config` (`StreamConfig`) | Apply new settings while streaming (client window resize, bitrate cap, frame rate, policies). Zero (unset) numeric fields keep their value, as `ViewConfig` does; the booleans `clipboard_enabled` and `file_transfer_enabled` cannot be "unset" and are applied as given, so the gateway always sends the device's full policy. |
| `request_idr` | `epoch`, `last_decoded_frame` u32 | The client lost sync (protocol `RequestKeyframe`) or the gateway dropped frames. The session's next frame is an IDR flagged `RESYNC`. The gateway sends at most 2 per second; the session coalesces repeats. |
| `input` | `event` (`hx.v1.InputEvent`) | One validated key, button, scroll or `release_all` event, in client order. |
| `mouse` | `seq` u32, `relative` bool, `x` sint32, `y` sint32 | Latest-wins pointer motion, already coalesced by the gateway (§17). Absolute values are clamped to the current display; relative deltas to ±32 768. |
| `frame_feedback` | `results` (repeated, at most 64) of `FrameResult` | Outcome per video frame (§15.3), sent as soon as known. |
| `transport_stats` | `rtt_us` u32, `delivery_rate_kbps` u32 | The gateway's view of the path to the client, about 4 per second while streaming; an optional extra input for the congestion controller. |
| `client_stats` | `hx.v1.ClientStats` | Forwarded client decode statistics (about 1 per second). |
| `ping` / `goodbye` | `nonce` u64 / `code` u32, `message` | Liveness; orderly end. `code` is a protocol.md §8 close code. |

`StreamConfig`:

| Field | Type | Validation (gateway before sending; session again on receipt) | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `codec` | `VideoCodec` | `H264` in v1; anything else is ignored and answered with `event` `CAPTURE_ERROR` | Codec to produce |
| `max_fps` | u32 | 0 (keep or default) or 1 to 240 | Frame ceiling (`Control::set_max_fps`, 60 in v1) |
| `bitrate_cap_kbps` | u32 | 0 = auto; otherwise 100 to 100 000 | Ceiling for the session's own controller (the ladder tops out at 20 000). The gateway has already applied the per-device bandwidth cap (architecture §7.4). On `start`, 0 means automatic; on `reconfigure`, 0 keeps the current cap (the rule below), so a client that wants "auto" again sends `start`, or a cap of 20 000 or more. |
| `width`, `height` | u32 | both 0 (keep) or each 1 to 16 384 with `width * height` at most 33 177 600 (the gateway sends 0 for a larger view; the session refuses it with `RESIZE_REFUSED`; the encoder takes 16 to 8 192 per side); the session rounds down to even | Desired remote size from the client window (`Control::request_resize`) |
| `scale_percent` | u32 | 0 (= 100) or 50 to 400 | HiDPI scale |
| `audio_enabled` | bool | requires the `AUDIO` feature | Reserved |
| `clipboard_enabled` | bool | | When false the session neither reads nor sends clipboard data |
| `file_transfer_enabled` | bool | | When false the session refuses file transfers |

### 14.2 Session to gateway: `SessionToGateway` (`oneof`)

| Variant | Fields | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `info` | `software` (at most 128), `features` u64, `codecs` (repeated `VideoCodec`, at most 4), `display` {`width`, `height`}, `max_fps` u32 | First message on `ctl`. `features` bits: 0 `AUDIO`, 1 `CLIPBOARD`, 2 `FILE_TRANSFER` (the protocol.md §4.3 bits), 8 `INPUT` (XTest available), 9 `RESIZE` (RandR resize available). The gateway intersects them with the client's capabilities and with policy. |
| `stream_format` | `epoch`, `codec`, `width`, `height`, `fps`, `first_frame` u32 | The format from `first_frame` on (becomes the client's `StreamInfo`, protocol.md §5.2). Sent after `start`, after a resize and whenever the picture size changes. The video lane races this message, which is why `first_frame` exists. |
| `cursor` | `event` (`hx.v1.CursorEvent`) | Shape (RGBA, straight alpha, content-hash `id`) or position. The session converts from `hx-capture`'s premultiplied ARGB and clamps cursors larger than 256 × 256. On `start` and `request_idr` it re-sends the current shape (`Control::request_idr` already does). |
| `cancel_frames` | `up_to_frame` u32 | The session wants every not-yet-delivered frame numbered up to this one (serial arithmetic) cancelled in transit. |
| `stats` | `SessionStats` | About 1 per second while streaming. |
| `event` | `kind` (`FATAL`, `DISPLAY_CHANGED`, `CAPTURE_ERROR`, `CLIPBOARD_ERROR`, `RESIZE_REFUSED`), `message` (at most 256) | Something the gateway should log or tell the client. `FATAL` means the pipeline stopped (`Control::fatal_error`). |
| `pong` / `goodbye` | `nonce` / `code`, `message` | As above. |

`SessionStats` (interval values, derived from `hx_session::stats::StatsSnapshot` by subtraction): `interval_ms`, `captures`, `frames`, `keyframes`, `idr_frames`, `bytes`, `max_frame_bytes`, `errors`, `capture_buffer_waits`, `encode_p50_us`, `encode_p95_us`, `total_p95_us`, `cpu_ms`, `target_bitrate_kbps`, `fps_ceiling`, `queue_depth_max`. The gateway uses it for logs, metrics and the admin `Status`; the client protocol has no server-to-client statistics message yet. Decision: protocol.md gets a **numeric-only** `ServerStats` message (a follow-up, §21), built by the gateway from `SessionStats` and its own transport numbers; **no session-supplied text is ever forwarded to a client** (§19.1, rule 3).

## 15. Media lane (`media`)

### 15.1 Video frame

One `kind 0x10` frame per encoded frame. The body is **exactly** protocol.md §6's 20-byte media header followed by the encoded payload, so the gateway validates the header and then forwards header and payload unchanged as the body of the client's frame stream (channel byte `0x05` first on QUIC; the identical bytes inside a `0x05` message on WebSocket). That keeps the path zero-copy friendly: the gateway reads each frame into one pooled buffer (`bytes::Bytes`) and slices it; it never re-encodes or concatenates.

How the session fills the header:

| Header field | Source |
|---|---|
| `version` | `1` |
| `codec` | `EncodedFrame.codec` mapped to the `VideoCodec` number (H.264 = 1) |
| `flags` | bit 0 `KEY` = `EncodedFrame.idr` (an IDR; a gradual-intra-refresh recovery point does **not** set `KEY`, because `KEY` means "decodes without earlier frames"); bit 1 `RESYNC` = this IDR answers a `start`, a `request_idr`, a `cancel_frames` or a gateway cancel. Other bits 0. |
| `frame_number` | a `u32` counter owned by the link layer, incremented for **every** `EncodedFrame` taken from `Pipeline::frames`, including one dropped before it is written, so a gap means "skipped". It does not follow `EncodedFrame::frame_no` (a `u64` that restarts when the encoder is recreated). Wraps modulo 2^32. |
| `capture_timestamp_us` | the frame's capture time on the container's monotonic clock in microseconds (from `EncodedFrame.timing`, `hx_capture::mono_ns` / 1000); the client only uses differences |
| `payload_len` | `EncodedFrame::bytes().len()` |

Width, height and fps are **not** in the header; they travel in `stream_format` (§14.2).

Gateway validation per frame, before the payload is accepted: `length` within the limit; header present; `version == 1`; `codec` equals the negotiated codec; no reserved flag bits; `payload_len == length - 21`; `frame_number` strictly newer (serial arithmetic) than the last accepted on this link, else drop and count; rate limits of §13; and a bounded **bitstream sanity check** (decision 8, §22): the payload begins with an Annex B start code; scanning at most its first 64 KiB, every NAL header has `forbidden_zero_bit` 0 and a valid type (1 to 12, 19 and 21; 13 to 15 (SPS extension, prefix NAL, subset SPS) and 20 (SVC/MVC slice extension) are refused because the session's x264 never emits them and they are unparsed attack surface for a client's decoder); on a `KEY` frame an SPS (type 7) is present and parsed with a small exp-Golomb reader (no allocation, `forbid(unsafe_code)`) and its coded size, after cropping, equals the `stream_format` in force for that frame and does not exceed the 7680 × 4320 pixel cap. Because the lanes race (above), an IDR can arrive before the `stream_format` that announces its size: the frame is parked in a staging queue, which counts against the video pool, for at most 2 s until the format in force for it matches its SPS (only the staging task waits; the media reader keeps draining the lane, §15.2); a mismatch that persists, or a frame with no `stream_format` at all, is a violation. The size of **every** SPS in a frame (at most 8, all equal, over the whole payload, in key and non-key frames alike) must equal the `stream_format` in force; the coded size (macroblocks times 16) is capped before cropping and less than one macroblock of cropping is allowed; only profiles 66, 77, 88 and 100 are accepted; an IDR slice in a frame not flagged `KEY` is a violation. A structural or sanity failure is a link violation (§19.2); a rate overage drops the frame. The gateway does **not** decode the payload and does not alter the header (which is why the header is validated, not rewritten). The client defends itself independently (sandboxed decode with a `max_pixels` limit, fuzzed; 1G/1H).

### 15.2 Backpressure and no unbounded queues

- The gateway **always drains the media lane**: it reads each frame promptly into the video pool of §13 (never smaller than one maximum-size frame). It drains the lane at the speed the session sends it, within the lane's byte budget (§13), and never because of a slow client: a slow client leads to cancels and drops at the gateway (below), not to a paused lane, so the session's writer never blocks behind it and `Pipeline::frames` is never stalled by the network. Only the byte budget can pause reading: not a slow client, and not a key frame waiting for its `stream_format`, which waits in the staging queue (§15.1) while the reader goes on. When the shared pool is full the oldest queued, then the oldest staged, frames are evicted and reported `DROPPED`.
- Toward the client, the gateway limits frames in flight to what the transport window allows. When a frame cannot be sent within the window or a budget of about 100 ms, it is **not queued**: the oldest not-yet-delivered frame streams are reset with `STALE_FRAME` (protocol.md §6.2) and reported as `CANCELLED`, and frames never started are reported as `DROPPED`.
- Because H.264 P-frames depend on earlier frames, any cancel or drop breaks the chain. After one, the gateway discards every frame that is not `KEY` until the next `KEY | RESYNC` frame and sends `request_idr` (limited to 2 per second). This is the "cancel queued frame streams and send a fresh full image" rule of architecture §7.4, with the gateway doing the stream resets and the session producing the fresh image.
- The session's own writer must complete each frame it starts (a half-written frame would desynchronise the lane). A write that makes no progress for 2 s closes the lane (a fault).

### 15.3 Acks and cancels (feedback to the congestion controller)

`frame_feedback.results`: one `FrameResult` per frame with `frame_number` u32, `outcome`, `bytes` u32, `elapsed_us` u32 (from the first write to the client until its ack: `FrameAck.elapsed`) and `queued_us` u32 (time the frame waited in the gateway before its first write).

| `outcome` | From `hx_transport::AckOutcome` | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `DELIVERED` | `Delivered` | The client received the whole frame (stream completion on QUIC, `0x09` ack on WebSocket) |
| `CANCELLED` | `Cancelled` | The gateway reset the stream (`STALE_FRAME`), by its own decision or on `cancel_frames` |
| `REJECTED` | `Rejected(code)` | The client stopped the stream |
| `DROPPED` | (gateway only) | The gateway discarded the frame without starting to send it (window full, no client, resync discard) |

`ConnectionLost` is not sent; the client connection ending is followed by `stop`. Feedback arrives on `ctl`, so a congestion signal is never stuck behind video. The session's controller (1F) uses `elapsed_us`, `queued_us`, the `DELIVERED` rate and the `CANCELLED`/`DROPPED` counts to step its bitrate ladder (S2: settled near 3.5 Mbps at 150 ms RTT, 10 Mbps and 1% loss) and to decide when to send `cancel_frames` and force a `RESYNC` IDR. Feedback for one frame is sent at most once; a result for an unknown frame number is ignored by the session.

### 15.4 Audio (reserved)

`kind 0x11` is the protocol.md §7.2 datagram body (`seq`, `timestamp`, one Opus packet, 10 ms frames) as a media-lane frame. It is not produced in Phase 1: the `AUDIO` feature bit is 0 in `info`, and a `0x11` frame while the feature was not negotiated is a violation. Audio is loss tolerant: the gateway forwards it as a datagram and drops it rather than queueing it.

## 16. Bulk lane (`bulk`)

Clipboard and files use the transfer shape of protocol.md §9.4 (header, data chunks, end, abort) on one lane, so many transfers share one low-priority connection and cannot delay anything else.

- **IDs**: the gateway allocates odd ids and the session even ids; an id already in use is a violation. `TransferOpen.channel` is `CLIPBOARD` (protocol channel `0x07`) or `FILE` (`0x08`); its `header` is the protocol `TransferHeader` (`mime`, `name`, `size`) under the same validation (printable-ASCII `mime`, the file-name rules of protocol.md §3.1, empty `name` for the clipboard). The gateway applies those rules to every header the session sends **before** it opens a client stream.
- **Size**: the receiver counts bytes; fewer or more than `size`, or a clipboard over 64 MiB, aborts that transfer (`TRANSFER_ERROR`) and the lane continues. The file limit is `max_file_bytes` (default 4 GiB, configurable lower) in both directions, as protocol.md §5.6 defines it; gatewayd refuses a header announcing more than the limit before reading any body (on the client side that is `TRANSFER_REJECTED`, protocol.md §5.6), while a body that does not match its announced size stays `TRANSFER_ERROR`.
- **Flow control**: a byte stream gives per-lane, not per-transfer, flow control. The gateway reads a transfer's data only while it holds less than 1 MiB unread for it and 4 MiB for the link, so a stalled client stream pauses the bulk lane and never `ctl` or `media`. One slow transfer can delay other transfers; accepted.
- **Direction semantics**: there is no request message; both directions are pushes. The side whose selection changed opens a `CLIPBOARD` transfer (the session when a local application copies text or a PNG; the gateway when the client's clipboard changes) and the receiver makes it the local selection (the session holds the body in memory, at most 64 MiB, and offers it to local applications). When a local application copies files (`text/uri-list`) the session pushes each regular file, at most 16 per copy, as a `FILE` transfer; the gateway applies policy and may refuse each with `TRANSFER_REJECTED`.
- **Policy**: the gateway enforces the device's clipboard and file settings in both directions (drops the data and aborts), and also tells the session through `StreamConfig` so that it does not read the selection at all. Clipboard transfers from the session are limited to 4 per second.
- **Names are hints.** The session writes received files into a directory of its own choosing (`/var/lib/hx-session/received`, one of the unit's two writable paths, §21 item 3; linked as `~/Received`), picks its own file name (`name`, then `name.1`, `name.2`, ... when it exists), never overwrites (`O_EXCL`), never follows symlinks (`O_NOFOLLOW`), and creates the file with mode `0600`. The gateway never treats a name as a path, and forwards the session's names to the client only after the protocol.md name validation, as data shown in the transfer dialog and never as UI text (§19.1, rule 3).

## 17. Priorities and backpressure

The requirement: **input never waits behind video**, and video is dropped or cancelled, never queued without bound.

1. **Lanes separate sizes.** Video (up to `max_frame_bytes`, 4 MiB by default), cursor shapes and transfers each have their own connection or direction; `ctl` G → S carries only messages of at most 16 KiB.
2. **The gateway's writer for `ctl` G → S** is a single task with queues drained in strict order: **P0** `input` events (ordered, bounded at 4 096; gatewayd first rate limits what a client may send to 2 000 input events per second with a burst of 500; a burst is paced (flow control slows the client) and a client more than one second behind its allowance, one that sends faster than 2 000 a second for good, is closed with `PROTOCOL_ERROR`), then the single-slot **`mouse`** (latest wins: a newer motion replaces an unsent one, absolute and relative merged as in protocol.md §7.1, `MouseCoalescer`); **P1** `frame_feedback`, `request_idr`, `reconfigure`, `start`/`stop`, `ping`; **P2** `transport_stats`, `client_stats`, `goodbye`. A key or button always goes out before pending motion that arrived after it. **If the queues back up because the session does not read** (a write on `ctl` makes no progress for 2 s, or the P0 queue fills while the client is within its rate limit), that is a **link fault** (§19.2), not a client `PROTOCOL_ERROR`: the client is not at fault and is sent `Goodbye(INTERNAL_ERROR)` as for any link fault. Button events carry absolute coordinates, so a coalesced motion cannot misplace a click.
3. **The session's reader for `ctl` G → S** runs on its own thread, independent of the encode pipeline, and injects input with the XTest backend straight from it. The encoder threads cannot delay input.
4. **The gateway's reader for `ctl` S → G** parses cursor, `stream_format`, `cancel_frames` and `stats` in a task separate from the media reader and the bulk reader. Cursor positions coalesce to the newest within 1 ms; a shape is never overtaken by a later position (the protocol.md ordering rule).
5. **Video**: drained promptly, bounded window, cancel or drop with feedback, resync by IDR (§15.2). **Bulk**: lowest priority; the gateway yields after every 256 KiB so it cannot monopolise the runtime.

## 18. One active client per desktop and takeover

v1 has one active client per desktop. The session link knows clients only through `epoch` (§14.1); all policy sits in gatewayd.

- A second client that authenticates (pinned key and PIN passed) **and asks to view the desktop** (its first `ViewConfig`, protocol.md 5.9: authenticating alone claims nothing, so that a device can log in only to revoke another one) while another is active **takes over**, subject to a per-server policy `takeover` ∈ `allow` (default) or `deny` (the newcomer is answered `BUSY`). Takeovers are **rate limited**: at most 3 per 10 minutes per desktop, after which the newcomer is answered `BUSY` (so two devices cannot fight each other, or a stolen device keep kicking the owner off). A device replacing its **own** session (the same device id) is exempt from both the policy and the limit **only if the old connection is dead**, that is, it does not answer a ping within 1 s (a client that lost its network is not locked out until the connection times out). If the old connection is alive the ordinary policy and limit apply, so a copied key plus PIN cannot displace the owner at will, not even under `allow`. The default is `allow` because a user switching devices must not be locked out by their own stale session; both clients have passed authentication, and the displaced one is told.
- Takeover sequence in gatewayd, in this order: (1) stop forwarding the old client's input and send `input{release_all}` to the session; (2) cancel the old client's in-flight frames (`CANCELLED`) and abort its transfers (`TransferAbort`); (3) tell the old client it was displaced: `Goodbye` with the `TAKEN_OVER` close code and the new device's sanitized name and device id (protocol.md §5.7; a client that negotiated minor 0 gets `BUSY` with a fixed text instead), so that the displaced client's UI can offer a **"revoke that device"** action (a stolen-then-used device is the likely reason to be displaced); the event is also written to privd's audit log (`RecordEvent` `TAKEOVER`); (4) increment `epoch` and send `start{epoch, config}` for the new client, then expect `stream_format` with the same `epoch` and an IDR `RESYNC` frame. Frame numbers keep counting, so the new client's reorder window starts clean.
- The session resets its congestion controller on `start`, discards feedback tagged for earlier frames and re-sends the cursor shape. Clipboard and transfers are per client and are not carried across. Concretely, a `start` whose `epoch` differs from the current one (a takeover) makes the session withdraw its clipboard offer and forget the received-files batch offered to the displaced device (`~/Received` keeps the files themselves); the new client sees only what is copied or received after its `start`.
- Only the current `epoch` may send input to the session: gatewayd forwards nothing from a client that is not the active one.
- When the last client leaves, gatewayd sends `stop`, keeps the link for 30 s (so a reconnect is instant), then closes `bulk`, `media` and `ctl`. The next authorized client redials.

## 19. Untrusted input and fault handling

### 19.1 Rules gatewayd follows for every session-supplied byte

1. Lengths are checked against §13 **before** any allocation; buffers come from fixed pools; no allocation is sized by a session-supplied value beyond those limits.
2. All integers are range-checked (frame numbers, sizes, `payload_len == length - 21`, coordinates, counters); statistics use saturating arithmetic.
3. **Session text is never shown in the client UI.** Strings are length limited and sanitized (control and ANSI characters removed) and go only to logs and the admin API; a client receives event *kinds and numeric codes*, never a session-supplied string (`info.software`, `event.message` and `goodbye.message` are log-only). The one exception is a file name in a transfer header, which has passed the protocol.md validation and is shown as data in the transfer dialog, never as a label or message. Session-supplied text is never used as a path, command or URL.
4. Cursor, transfer-header and file-name content gets the same validation as when a client sends it (protocol.md §3.1, §5.4, §5.5), before any of it is forwarded to a client.
5. Unknown protobuf fields and `oneof` variants are ignored and counted; undecodable bodies are violations.
6. Rates are enforced per message class (§13); the gateway never spends unbounded CPU or memory because the session is chatty.
7. The gateway never sends anything secret to the session, and never derives behaviour from where the session says it is, who it says it is, or what version string it claims, beyond the protocol version already negotiated.
8. No descriptors: every lane is read with `recvmsg`, and any control message or `MSG_CTRUNC` is a violation (the rule of §3).
9. The link parser (framer, message decoding, header, Annex B/SPS and transfer validation) lives in a module or crate that is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, with fuzz targets for 1H: the lane framer, `SessionToGateway` decoding, media-header validation, the Annex B/SPS check, `TransferOpen` validation and cursor-shape validation. Running the relay in a separate, even more restricted process is considered later (§22, Deferred).

### 19.2 Faults and recovery

A **violation** (malformed frame or preamble, forbidden kind, oversize length, structural media-header error, a byte on an unused direction, more frames per second than the §13 frame-count limits allow) or a sustained rate or byte-budget overage (3 consecutive seconds) or a ctl write stall (§17) closes all three lanes (a session `goodbye` closes them as well, and **counts as a fault** with the same backoff: an owner's own close does not), increments a per-link fault counter, logs a rate-limited warning with the sanitized reason, sends the client `Goodbye(INTERNAL_ERROR)` if one is connected, and leaves gatewayd otherwise unaffected. Redialling uses exponential backoff 1, 2, 4, … up to 30 s. After 5 faults within 10 minutes gatewayd asks privd for `RestartSession` (class A, so rate limited by privd); after 3 restarts in an hour it stops redialling quickly and reports the desktop as `session_unavailable` in status, so the app can say so (a bad session package or malware, the user's call). Requests for `InstallSession` (an outdated or incompatible session, or a session that closes right after the preamble three dials in a row on a desktop whose session worked before) share that budget: three per hour, then `session_unavailable`. **There is a way back without an operator:** while `session_unavailable`, gatewayd retries once every 10 minutes (a single dial with the full validation of this section), and also immediately after privd reports the desktop restarted or the session package updated; the first clean handshake clears the state. A violation never crashes gatewayd: parse errors are values.

## 20. Mapping

### 20.1 To the client protocol (protocol.md)

| Client protocol (protocol.md) | Session link |
|---|---|
| Control: `Hello`, `HelloAck`, `Refuse`, `pairing`, `auth`, `Ping`/`Pong` | Terminate at the gateway; the session sees none of it |
| `ViewConfig` (§5.2) | The gateway validates, applies device policy (bandwidth cap, clipboard, files) and sends `start` or `reconfigure` with a `StreamConfig` |
| `StreamInfo` | Built from `stream_format`, with its `first_frame` unchanged |
| `RequestKeyframe` | `request_idr` |
| `ClientStats` | `client_stats` (forwarded) |
| `Goodbye` | `goodbye` / `stop` |
| Input `0x02` (§5.3) | `input` (validated, coordinates clamped to the current display, scancode at most `0xE7`, valid buttons) |
| Mouse `0x03` (§7.1) | `mouse` (coalesced) |
| Cursor `0x04` (§5.4) | `cursor`, validated, positions coalesced |
| Video `0x05` (§6) | `media` kind `0x10`, header forwarded unchanged |
| Frame acks and `STALE_FRAME` (§6.2) | `frame_feedback` (G → S), `cancel_frames` (S → G) |
| Audio `0x06` (§7.2) | `media` kind `0x11` (reserved) |
| Clipboard `0x07`, Files `0x08` (§5.5, §9.4) | `bulk` lane |

### 20.2 To the capture and encode pipeline (what 1F implements)

`apps/hx-session` currently exposes the protocol-independent media path in `hx_session::pipeline`. The link layer sits on top of it and uses only this API:

| Link | `hx_session` API |
|---|---|
| `start` | `Pipeline::start_x11(PipelineConfig { encoder.bitrate_kbps / fps, max_fps, .. })`, or resume a warm pipeline; the first frame follows `Control::request_idr()` |
| `stop` | `Pipeline::shutdown()` (or park it; CPU must reach about zero within 1 s) |
| `reconfigure.bitrate_cap_kbps` | The 1F congestion controller's ceiling; the rate it picks goes to `Control::set_bitrate_kbps` (100 to 100 000, no encoder reopen) |
| `reconfigure.max_fps` | `Control::set_max_fps` (1 to 240) |
| `reconfigure.width/height/scale` | `Control::request_resize(Size)`; a refusal appears in `StatsSnapshot::errors` and becomes `event RESIZE_REFUSED`; the resulting size is announced by `stream_format` |
| `request_idr` | `Control::request_idr()` |
| `frame_feedback`, `transport_stats` | Inputs of the controller (1F), not of the pipeline |
| `cancel_frames` (S → G) | Produced by the controller; followed by an IDR request to itself |
| video | `Pipeline::frames()` (`EncodedFrame`: `codec`, `keyframe`, `idr`, `timing`, `data`), turned into the §15.1 header by the link layer; the pooled buffer returns to the encoder when the frame has been written |
| `cursor` | `Pipeline::cursor_events()` (`CursorEvent::Shape(Arc<CursorShape>)`, `Position`), converted from premultiplied ARGB `u32` to straight RGBA bytes |
| `stats` | `Control::stats()` → `StatsSnapshot`, differenced into intervals |
| `event FATAL` | `Control::fatal_error()` |
| `input`, `mouse` | The XTest injector of 1F; not part of the pipeline |
| clipboard, files | The clipboard and file components of 1F |

## 21. Implementation notes and follow-ups

Not part of the contract; for the people implementing it. Each follow-up names its owner.

1. **`hx-ipc` crate (1C).** One new internal crate holds the framing (§4, §13), the `hx.privd.v1`, `hx.gatewayd_admin.v1` (protobuf identifiers cannot contain a hyphen) and `hx.link.v1` messages and helpers. It depends on `hx-proto` for the shared `hx.v1` types (`InputEvent`, `CursorEvent`, `TransferHeader`, `ClientStats`, `VideoCodec`), and splits the privd side and the link side by Cargo feature, so that `hx-session` does not compile the privd messages (and nothing pulls in `quinn`). **Status:** the crate exists (`crates/hx-ipc`; features `privd` and `link`, `hx-session` enables only `link`), architecture §3 and tech-stack.md are updated, and it adds only `zeroize` and `rustix` (`net`: `recvmsg`, `SO_PEERCRED`). `mlock`, `PR_SET_DUMPABLE` and `openat2` belong to privd itself, the HPKE implementation arrives with the Phase 2 sealed form (only the proto fields are reserved), and the H.264 Annex B / SPS check of §15.1 belongs with the gateway's media path.
2. **`hx-provision` follow-ups (1D):** (a) a device-add call on the Incus client for the proxy device (§6.11); (b) a standalone "check current password, set password" entry point on the post stage's stdin-file mechanism, shielded against cancellation (§4, §6.9); (c) an API that exposes the Incus background operation so privd can cancel it and wait (§4); (d) setting limits on a live instance including the root disk size (§6.8); (e) recipe vars for `settings` (hostname, timezone, locale, keyboard; §6.5); (f) privd uses the existing `Catalog::embedded()` / `embedded_source()` (recipes compiled in, §6.5), with a CI check that the embedded set equals `recipes/`; **and** `hx_provision::Secret` must not be `Clone` and must zeroize on drop, including copies made by `Secrets` (today `Secret` and `Secrets` derive `Clone`); (g) the Docker disable path and the host-network-only fallback (§6.9); (h) teardown helpers that delete only marked objects (§6.13); (i) `ExecSpec` has no user field, which matters only if `hx-session` ever becomes a user unit (§6.11). Until they exist the matching commands return `UNIMPLEMENTED`, or privd uses `Client` methods directly. *Status: (a) to (h) are implemented in `hx-provision` (APIs and caller rules in its README, "Caller obligations for `hx-privd`") (the "Gap in hx-provision" notes in §6.9 and §6.11 are removed); (i) is unchanged.*
3. **Recipes follow-up (1D):** rename the recipe unit that runs `xfce4-session` (`hx-session.service` today) to `hx-xsession.service`; add the in-container `hx-session.socket` and `hx-session.service` (system units, `User=<uid>`, §11) and the root-owned `/run/hx-session` directory. **Conditions on the `hx-session.service` unit**, all to be tested inside an unprivileged container:
   - Explicit `Environment=DISPLAY=:0` and `XAUTHORITY=/run/hx-xorg/Xauthority` (there is no PAM session); a later PipeWire audio path needs `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/<uid>` and ordering after `user@<uid>.service`.
   - Hardening that is valid in an unprivileged container: `NoNewPrivileges=yes`, an empty `CapabilityBoundingSet=`, `RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=read-only` with `ReadWritePaths=` only the received-files directory and the clipboard staging directory `/var/lib/hx-session/clipboard` (not `/run/user/<uid>`, which `ProtectSystem=strict` makes read-only), `LockPersonality=yes`, `SystemCallFilter=@system-service`, `TasksMax`, `MemoryMax`.
   - **Not** `PrivateNetwork=yes` (it breaks Xorg's abstract socket), and **not** `PrivateTmp=yes` unless `/tmp/.X11-unix` stays reachable.
   - `KillMode=control-group`, and the daemon never launches user-facing programs (§11).
   - Restart policy that does not trip start-rate limits quickly, since `RestartSession` runs `reset-failed` first (§6.11).
   - *Done and verified in an unprivileged isolated-idmap Incus 6.0.0 container (`crates/hx-provision/tests/incus_real_session.rs`).* Two facts the verification added. **Incus writes `/run/systemd/system/service.d/zzz-lxc-service.conf` at every boot**, which forces `NoNewPrivileges`, `ProtectKernelTunables` and `ProtectControlGroups` (and others) off for every service and sorts after a unit's own settings, so the recipe ships `hx-session.service.d/zzzz-hx-hardening.conf` that turns `NoNewPrivileges` back on (the process then runs with `NoNewPrivs: 1`). `ProtectKernelTunables` and `ProtectControlGroups` are **not usable**: they work on Ubuntu 24.04 but fail the service with `226/NAMESPACE` on Debian 12 in an unprivileged container, which is why Incus disables them. The daemon installs at `/usr/lib/hx-session/hx-session` and the password helper at `/usr/lib/hx-session/hx-pwcheck` (`hx_provision::session`); the writable paths are `/var/lib/hx-session/received` and `/var/lib/hx-session/clipboard` (linked as `~/Received`), because a path under `/home/<user>` would move when the account is renamed. Incus's drop-in holds only seven settings (ProcSubset, ProtectProc, ProtectControlGroups=no, ProtectKernelTunables=no, NoNewPrivileges=no, an empty LoadCredential, PrivateNetwork=no); none loosens the other hardening of `hx-session.service`, and the real test asserts that. On Incus 6.0.0 a `PATCH` of a device replaces the whole device (no stale option survives); `add_session_link` still replaces with a conditional `PUT` so that it does not depend on that.
4. **`protocol.md` follow-ups (owner of 1A; for the 1C implementation round, not edited here):** a `TAKEN_OVER` close code (§18); a numeric-only `ServerStats` message for the stats overlay (§14.2); a maximum file size, default 4 GiB, enforced in both directions (§13, §16); a client "revoke that device" action available after a takeover (§18).
5. **1B:** the verifier format for PIN and Linux password (§5.3), the admin-socket pairing-token semantics (§9) and the hook that delivers privd's recipient public key through the authenticated pairing exchange (§5.2, one-liner path only).
6. **1E (installer):** it is the `Admin` caller for `BeginSetup`, `SetupHost`, `CreateDesktop`, `EnrolOwnerKey` and the admin-socket verifier and token commands; it performs the package, unit, credential, rescue-helper and port-rule stages itself (§5.1); in the SSH path it receives the password and PIN on stdin (§5.4) so gatewayd never sees them; **Gate 1 applies to the installer's own argv** (§5.2). The one-liner (`--pair`) ends after the pairing token is installed; the post-pairing setup (password, PIN, `CreateDesktop` from the app) is specified in protocol.md §5.10 and §5.5 above (1G), and until it is implemented the one-liner cannot finish a desktop. Design in architecture §5.
7. **Resumed installs.** The installer's resume (architecture §5.3) re-calls idempotent commands; the app resends the password after a gatewayd restart.
8. **Version coupling.** gatewayd, privd, the CLI and the in-container `hx-session` ship from one release. The link tolerates a minor mismatch only; after an update gatewayd calls `InstallSession` for any desktop reporting an older major.
9. **`hx-privd` status (1C).** Implemented in `apps/hx-agent/src/privd` (its README lists the on-disk state, the command coverage and the deviations). Not in this release, answering `UNIMPLEMENTED`: `GetRecipientKey` and every sealed-secret command, including any slot marked `sealed` (HPKE is Phase 2), `PREBUILT` images and `image_file` (the fetcher downloads manifests and agent releases only, and there is no image signature scheme yet), `Uninstall` (until the 1E work lands; it is specified in §6.13 in its narrowed form), `UpgradeIncus` and `HOST_NETWORK_ONLY` Docker. **Differences from the text above, and what is still open:**
   - **The pepper (§2, §5.3) weakens the contract until it is sealed.** §2 puts it in `/etc/hx/creds/pepper.cred`, encrypted with `systemd-creds` (TPM-backed where available). privd takes it from the systemd credential `pepper` when its unit has one (`LoadCredentialEncrypted=pepper:/etc/hx/creds/pepper.cred`, which the shipped unit does) and otherwise from a root-only plaintext file `/var/lib/hx/pepper.key`. On a host with a TPM, running on the fallback means a copied disk yields the pepper and the stored verifiers can be attacked offline, which is exactly what §5.3 is meant to prevent. Generating the credential at install (and migrating `pepper.key` into it) is therefore **a 1E release blocker**: the installer's `credentials` stage does both, with the migration steps and the done-check in architecture §5.7 (the privd README has the manual form).
   - **`InstallSession` has no minisign check of its own.** It trusts a root-owned file in `/usr/lib/hx/current/session/<distro>/` (one read, owner, mode, link count and size checked), which is part of the agent release that `ApplyUpdate` or the installer verified (§6.12); there is no package in a release yet (`CreateDesktop` then warns).
   - **Self-update is implemented** (§6.12, "Decisions of the implementation"): `CheckUpdate`, `ApplyUpdate`, `RollbackUpdate`, the sandboxed fetcher and its unit. **Gaps:** the build embeds no signing key until 1E provides `release-keys/*.pub` (updates answer `FAILED_PRECONDITION`); a new privd binary that cannot start at all is not rolled back by privd (the `OnFailure=` rescue helper of §6.12 covers it, once the installer ships it); unit files are not part of a release (the installer writes them, architecture §5.7); there are no per-update `ErrorDetail` values (the messages say why); the health probes, the fetcher unit and `systemctl` were tested against fakes and a local HTTPS server, not on a host with real systemd.
   - **Partly open:** the account-policy self-checks (§3) now also read the live groups of every Gateway peer (`SO_PEERGROUPS`, one more small `unsafe` block, in `peergroups.rs`), refuse a second passwd entry on the gateway uid and a POSIX ACL on the Incus socket, but they do not walk the ACLs or capabilities of the directories above the sockets; the `CURRENT_PASSWORD` check needs the `hx-pwcheck` helper (§6.9), which neither exists yet nor is in a packaged `hx-session`, so outside the setup window it fails closed (`INTERNAL`) for now; the post stage of `CreateDesktop` is shielded from cancel (a cancel waits for it, bounded by its own timeouts), but the build stage is not.
   - **Unchanged by design, no contract effect:** host addresses are enumerated with `ip -j address show` (fixed argv, 4 MiB cap, an unparseable or empty list is an error) instead of a netlink socket; `/etc/hx/privd.toml` is read for `[update] channel` only (a private, root-owned file; §2 lists more keys than are read today), and the desktop cap is the compiled-in 1; `SetLimits` uses the configured disk size as its floor because `hx-provision` reports no disk usage; the sysctl drop-in is applied with `sysctl -p <that file>`, not `sysctl --system`.
10. **Implementation status of the session link (1C).** Both ends and the relay between the client protocol and the link are implemented: the session end in `apps/hx-session/src/link` (socket activation by `sd_listen_fds`, three lanes, supersede, the runtime of §20.2), the gateway end in `apps/hx-agent/src/link` (`LinkClient`, `LinkEvents`, `ReconnectPolicy`, `LinkKeeper`: the dial on demand with the 30 s keep, which was called `DesktopLink` until it clashed with the gateway's trait of that name), and the relay in `apps/hx-agent/src/gateway/relay` (`RelayLink`, the gateway's `DesktopLink`: video, cursor, input, feedback, statistics, transfers and takeover per client, §20.1). They are tested against each other over a real socket with the real pipeline under Xvfb (`tests/link_session.rs`, and `tests/relay_e2e.rs` from a real client through the real gateway), and the link client against a fake hostile session. The relay's rules: the event pump never waits for a client; frames are bounded after they leave the link's pool (4 queued, 16 in flight, with byte bounds); a client that cannot keep up has its frames cancelled and gets one IDR; only the current epoch's input is forwarded; session text is never forwarded; `RestartSession` and `InstallSession` are additionally limited by the gateway (3 an hour, a minute apart). Files a client sends are offered to desktop applications as a file-list clipboard (`text/uri-list` and the GNOME/XFCE copied-files target) pointing into the received directory, so they can be pasted in the file manager (`ClipboardManager::offer_received_files`; files that arrive within 3 s of each other are one paste). Known gaps: `StreamConfig.scale_percent` is validated but has no session-side effect yet; `audio_enabled` is ignored (audio is not negotiated); a SIGTERM stops the daemon without the orderly `goodbye` (a signal handler needs a crate or `unsafe`, left to packaging).

## 22. Decisions and deferred items

### Decisions

Answers to the open questions of v0.1, taken after the security review.

1. **The device registry stays in gatewayd** (§2). PIN and Linux-password verifiers are `HMAC(pepper, Argon2id(secret))` with the HMAC computed by privd. It exposes **no MAC oracle**: `PepperVerify` returns only yes or no and counts failures globally per purpose (PIN 10/h and 50/day, PASSWORD 5/h and 20/day, then locked until an Admin `ResetLockout`), and `PepperEnrol` returns a MAC only to set a verifier (Gateway: 5/day after setup) (§5.3, §6.15). A stolen registry can therefore be guessed only online, through failure-capped verification. The `PASSWORD` cap can be used as a lockout lever by an internet attacker once web login exists (accepted; §5.3). Registry changes are recorded in privd's audit log (`RecordEvent`) and shown to all paired devices. The residual risk of a memory-resident gatewayd is stated in §1.2.
2. **Phase 1 accepts the plain Linux password passing through gatewayd** (one-liner path). Phase 2 secrets that a client supplies (Drive token, recovery key) use the sealed, owner-authenticated, nonce-bound form of §5.2, whose fields are reserved now; the backup key is generated on the server and never travels (§2). Conditions: the current-password check (§6.9) runs a fixed-argv helper shipped in privd's verified `hx-session` package (`hx-pwcheck`, dynamically linked against `libcrypt1`) and trusts only its exit status; the current password travels the same swept stdin-file path as `chpasswd`; and the failure caps of decision 1 apply.
3. **The setup window stays for the one-liner path** (2 hours by default, extendable by Admin only; §5.1). In the SSH install path the password and Drive token go over SSH to the root installer (an `Admin` caller), which writes the verifiers through the admin socket, so gatewayd never sees them.
4. **Takeover default is `allow`** (§18): the displaced client gets a `TAKEN_OVER` code and a "revoke that device" action; takeovers are rate limited (3 per 10 minutes) and audited. The `TAKEN_OVER` close code is a protocol.md follow-up (§21, item 4).
5. **A numeric-only `ServerStats` message is added to protocol.md** (follow-up, §21 item 4); session text is never forwarded to a client (§19.1, rule 3).
6. **Docker enable**: Gateway only while the setup window is open; Admin-only afterwards (`hx-agent docker enable`); `DisableDocker` stays open to Gateway (§5.1, §6.9, §7 rule 5).
7. **File size**: default maximum 4 GiB, configurable lower, enforced in both directions; a protocol.md follow-up (§21 item 4).
8. **Hostile video**: both layers. gatewayd checks Annex B structure and, on key frames, the SPS dimensions (§15.1); the client decodes in a sandbox with a `max_pixels` limit and is fuzzed (1G/1H).
9. **Schemas live in a new internal `hx-ipc` crate** that depends on `hx-proto` for the shared `hx.v1` types, with the privd and link modules split by feature (§21 item 1). Architecture §3 and tech-stack.md are updated when the crate is added in 1C.
10. **Proxy-device semantics** (`uid`, `gid`, `mode`, `security.uid`/`security.gid` under an isolated idmap; symlink and replacement test; restart and reboot; throughput) **are verified on real Incus as the first step of 1C implementation** (§11). There is no fallback to a root-identity connect: a failure reopens the design.
11. **Single desktop in v1.** Multi-desktop isolation, the device-to-desktop mapping and per-desktop link directories are deferred.
12. **Fetcher rules are contract-level now** (§6.12: dynamic user, no capabilities, no `AF_UNIX`, timeouts, size quotas, read-once verified copy, strict version regex, monotonic manifest timestamp, safe extraction); the sandbox details belong to 1C and 1D.
13. **Unit naming.** The streaming daemon is `hx-session.service` (a system unit running as the desktop user, started through `hx-session.socket`, §11); the recipe unit that runs `xfce4-session` is renamed (for example `hx-xsession.service`), a 1D/recipes follow-up (§21 item 3).
14. **The one-liner carries no token and uses `--pair`** (owner 1E and the wizard): the installer prompts for it on `/dev/tty` with hidden input and hands it to gatewayd as the `CreatePairingToken` secret frame (§9); `--token` in argv is only for unattended use and is documented as weaker (`sudo` logs argv to the auth log and journal, shells keep history; §5.2). Since 2026-11-22 (maintainer decision at Gate 1) it is refused (exit 2) unless the explicit flag `--token-on-command-line` is also given, for `hx-agent install` and `hx-agent pairing token` alike, so a token never reaches argv by accident.
15. **Uninstall is split** (v0.4, §6.13): privd removes only what it created; `hx-agent uninstall` does the rest, because privd's sandbox cannot write unit directories, traverse gatewayd's state directory or outlive its own unit.
16. **The installer owns units and host files privd cannot write** (v0.4, §5.1, §6.4): `hx-host-firewall.service`, `hx-docker-user.service`, credentials, the rescue helper and the port rule are installed by `hx-agent install`; privd writes `/etc/hx/host-firewall.nft` and the sysctl file only.
17. **The public port is socket-activated** (v0.4, §6.12): `hx-gatewayd-public.socket` with `FileDescriptorName=public`, so gatewayd needs no capability to bind 443.
18. **Release artifacts** (v0.4, §6.12): ustar-only tarball, canonical one-line manifest, both release keys required, the signing key in a protected GitHub environment.
19. **Ubuntu 22.04 is not a supported host** (v0.4, architecture §4.1): systemd 249 has no `systemd-creds`.
20. **Post-pairing setup for the one-liner path** (1G, maintainer-approved 2026-11-22; protocol 1.3). Setup runs on a new, reconnectable connection type `INTENT_SETUP` (feature `SETUP`), not on the pairing connection (30 s budget, no features, single-use token) and not on a normal PIN session (5-minute idle close, would mix setup authority into every session). It is admitted only for the **bound setup device** while the window is open and privd says `SETUP_OPEN`; a connection without a PIN is key-only and can only set one (§5.5). One gatewayd-owned job on a dedicated privd lane, independent of the client connection (reconnect and converge after a gatewayd restart); the job finishes setup itself; `start` needs at least 50 minutes of window; **nothing in privd's contract widens** (§5.1), apart from Admin's `FinishSetup` in `UNINITIALIZED` moving to `READY` when a ready desktop exists. Secrets as in §5.3. Accepted: a Docker retry after `SELF_TEST_FAILED` during setup only through the Admin CLI afterwards; `hx-agent install --pair` and `--token` refuse desktop-only options (the app wizard is the only source); `SetupStart` fields 3 to 9 stay reserved for the Phase 2 sealed Drive token; 50 minutes as the minimum window.
21. **Prebuilt desktop images** (1D, maintainer-approved 2026-11-22): a **separate image key pair** (current and next) in `apps/hx-agent/image-keys/`, the GitHub environment `image-signing` restricted to `main` with no per-run approval (a leaked image key must not also sign agent releases); the keys stay `UNPROVISIONED` until the maintainer supplies them, and the release gate refuses a release meanwhile; compatibility bound by `recipe_digest`, not by date; a second hardened fetcher with a chunked hand-off through an 80 MiB tmpfs (no 5 GiB tmpfs, no loop quota disk, no unverified streaming into Incus); the 1E box "both image paths" closes on the offline `--image-file` cells plus privd's online integration tests, with an online canary cell once real keys exist; the newest 4 `images-*` releases are kept (§6.5, §6.12).
22. **Pair a new device from the app** (1.3, maintainer-approved 2026-11-22): a PIN-authenticated, active device may request a short-lived pairing code over the protocol (protocol.md §5.9) instead of the user running `hx-agent pairing code` on the server; rate-limited, one live code, audited as `DEVICE_PAIRING_CODE_ISSUED` (§6.15, §9). A device paired with it is ordinary and never a setup device.
23. **Reinstate after a PIN lockout with the account password** (1.3, maintainer-approved "Pair again" screen, 2026-11-22; protocol.md §11.7): only a device revoked **by the PIN lockout** (the registry now records `revoked_by`: `LOCKOUT`, `DEVICE`, `OPERATOR`, `SELF`) and less than 30 days ago may reinstate itself, on an `INTENT_REINSTATE` connection with its own key, by proving the account password through privd `PepperVerify(PASSWORD)` (privd's global PASSWORD budget plus 3 per device per hour). Any other revoked or unknown key gets the uniform refusal. Reinstating sets the device `active` and resets its PIN counter but authorizes no session: the device logs in afterwards with its existing PIN. Audited as `DEVICE_REINSTATED` with cause `PASSWORD`. A device revoked by its owner, another device or the operator stays revoked (only `hx-agent devices reinstate` can bring it back).
24. **Server management from the app** (1.3, maintainer-approved 2026-11-22; protocol.md §5.11): a PIN-authenticated, active device may read an overview and change the PIN and password, restart the desktop, turn Docker off, set limits and update the agent through `ServerMgmt`. gatewayd uses only commands the Gateway role already has (§5.6): privd's contract does not widen. Turning Docker **on** stays Admin-only (the app shows `sudo hx-agent docker enable`). The current PIN is verified on the login's failure counter; the current password by privd's `CURRENT_PASSWORD` check. Every mutation is audited with the requesting device.

### Deferred

- **privd-backed signing oracle** for the identity key (a rustls custom `SigningKey` that asks privd for each handshake signature), the target design that takes key impersonation out of a gatewayd compromise (§2.1).
- **A separate, more restricted relay process** for the session link parser, if the in-process parser plus `forbid(unsafe_code)` and fuzzing proves insufficient (§19.1).
- **Owner-key-signed Docker enable** requested from the app after setup (§5.1).
- **Multi-desktop** isolation and per-desktop link directories (decision 11).
- `HOST_NETWORK_ONLY` Docker mode, `UpgradeIncus`, and the Phase 2 commands, which stay named and `UNIMPLEMENTED` (§6.9, §6.13, §6.14).
- The pairing-exchange hook that delivers privd's recipient key without SSH (1B).
- An augmented PAKE (SPAKE2+) for pairing, so that a stolen server-side verifier cannot be used to pair (longer-term option for 1B).

---

# Part 3: the repository link

Status: draft v0.8, revised after review (2026-11-27; the review fixes are in §25 queue and budget bounds, §26 admission and cleanup, §29 create-only snapshots, §31 TOO_LARGE, §35 records and teardown; v0.8 settles privd's job runner for W5a: the steps and their failures §26, the relay buffer cap §25, the Drive session failure event §29, the guard timer and every timer §32, privd's progress text §33, the backup key reader §34, the on-disk records and crash recovery §35, the effects of a failure §31; v0.7 settled the Drive helper for W4; v0.6 settled the worker's start sequence, exit codes, error mapping and package layout for workstream W3), written before any Phase 2 code ("Contracts before consumers"). It specifies what runs between the backup worker, `hx-privd` and the Drive helper, and how a backup or restore job is run, supervised and cleaned up. The decisions behind it are recorded in §36; the commands that start jobs (§6.14) stay reserved until their code lands. Architecture background: [architecture.md](architecture.md) §8.5 and §9.

## 23. Purpose, topology and trust

Phase 2 jobs (backup, restore, check, prune, key management, repository initialisation) run `rustic_core` in the **backup worker**. The worker reads or writes the desktop's files, but it never reaches Google Drive: every repository operation is a request on the **repository link** to `hx-privd`, which checks it against a per-job policy (§30) and forwards it over a second link to the **Drive helper**, the only process that holds the Drive token and speaks HTTPS.

```
worker (helper container, no network)
   ⇄ [Incus proxy device: unix ↔ unix byte pipe]
   ⇄ hx-privd (relay + policy)
   ⇄ [/run/hx/drive.sock]
   ⇄ Drive helper (systemd unit: holds the Drive token, the only process that speaks HTTPS)
```

privd cannot be the Drive endpoint itself: its unit has `IPAddressDeny=any`, and root privd never speaks HTTP (§6.12). privd is also not bypassed by a direct worker-to-Drive-helper pipe, for three reasons. The worker parses hostile desktop data and is the process most likely to be compromised. privd enforces the per-job operation allowlist and verifies SHA-256(body) = id on every write, so a compromised `BACKUP` worker can add objects but cannot overwrite or delete packs, snapshots, keys or the repository config (this is the anti-ransomware property). And a direct pipe would need `AF_UNIX` in the Drive helper's sandbox, or descriptor passing through Incus, which the proxy device cannot do. The cost is two memory copies at up to about 25 MB/s; privd streams in chunks of at most 1 MiB and never buffers a whole pack (it runs under `mlockall`, §5.2).

| Party | Holds | Trust |
|---|---|---|
| Backup worker | The repository password for this job (secret slot 0, §34); the snapshot read-only (backup) or the restore target (restore) | Low. It parses container-controlled data; assume that a file in the desktop can compromise it. |
| Drive helper | The Drive refresh token (`LoadCredentialEncrypted=drive-token`, §2) | Low to medium. It parses Google's responses. It never sees the repository password. |
| `hx-privd` | The backup key (`LoadCredentialEncrypted=backup-key`, §2), the policy and the job state | The deputy. It never speaks HTTP and never interprets repository content. |

**Goals.** A compromised worker cannot obtain the Drive token, reach any network, read anything but its own job's snapshot, or destroy existing backups in a `BACKUP`, `RESTORE`, `CHECK`, `SNAPSHOTS` or `RESTORE_PLAN` job. A compromised Drive helper cannot decrypt backups. A compromised gatewayd gains nothing here: Part 3 has no gateway-facing surface beyond the reserved commands of §6.14.

## 24. Transport

**The helper container.** For every job privd creates one **ephemeral, empty Incus container**, named `hx-bkw-<8 hex digits of the job id>`, with `ephemeral: true`, no profiles, no image (`init --empty`), on the hx storage pool, root disk 256 MiB, and **no network interface**. privd sets exactly the configuration below and nothing else (§7, rule 4); every value is a constant or is computed from validated names.

| Config key | Value |
|---|---|
| `security.idmap.isolated` | `true` (a fresh host uid range per job) |
| `security.privileged` | `false` |
| `security.nesting` | `false` |
| `security.guestapi` | `false` (no `/dev/incus`) |
| `security.syscalls.deny_compat` | `true` |
| `limits.memory` / `limits.memory.swap` | `1GiB` / `false` |
| `limits.cpu` | `2` |
| `limits.cpu.priority` / `limits.disk.priority` | `0` / `0` (lowest priority; constants) |
| `limits.processes` | `64` |
| `boot.autostart` | `false` |
| `user.hx.kind` / `user.hx.job` | `backup-worker` / the job id |

The helper is created with **one** `POST /1.0/instances` that carries the config and all devices below, `"source": {"type": "none"}` (what `init --empty` sends), `"ephemeral": true` and an **explicit empty `"profiles": []`** (leaving the field out applies the `default` profile, which has a NIC). The container's only content is the static musl `hx-backup-worker` binary, pushed as `/sbin/init` (mode 0755, uid 0) from `/usr/lib/hx/<running version>/` while the helper is stopped, and the empty directories `/sbin`, `/dev`, `/proc`, `/sys`, `/tmp`, `/run` and `/src` or `/dst`. The file API does not create parent directories (only the `incus` CLI does), so `hx-provision` creates `/sbin` itself. After creation `hx-provision` reads the helper back and requires no profile, exactly the spec's devices (no NIC) and every config key; a helper that fails this is deleted, **except** that one whose `user.hx.kind` or `user.hx.job` is missing or wrong is not provably ours and is left in place: privd reports an error naming the instance (operator cleanup) and never deletes it. LXC mounts `proc`, `sys` and `dev`; the worker is PID 1 and uid 0 of the helper's own user namespace, which has no capabilities in the initial namespace.

**Devices.** privd adds exactly these devices, through `hx-provision`'s typed constructors (`BackupHelperSpec`, `SnapshotMount`, `TargetMount`, `RepoLink`; §7, rule 4):

| Device | Used by | Definition |
|---|---|---|
| `root` | all jobs | The pool's root disk, 256 MiB. |
| `src` | `BACKUP` | A disk device with `source=/var/lib/incus/storage-pools/<pool>/containers-snapshots/<desktop>/<snapshot>/rootfs`, `path=/src`, `readonly=true`, `shift=true`. The source **must be the snapshot's `rootfs` subdirectory**: the snapshot's top directory is `d--x------` owned by the desktop's host root id, which a helper with a different isolated map sees as 65534 and cannot enter. Through `rootfs` the worker sees the container's exact ids, readable `/etc/shadow` and `security.capability` extended attributes, and every write gives `EROFS`. |
| `dst` | `RESTORE` | A writable disk device with `source=/var/lib/incus/storage-pools/<pool>/containers/<target>/rootfs`, `path=/dst`, `shift=true`. The target is an instance that privd created with `init --empty` and **never starts before the write**.
