It is built from the desktop by an **allowlist**, not a denylist: its config is exactly the keys privd sets on desktops (`security.privileged`, `security.guestapi`, `security.idmap.isolated`, `boot.autostart`, `limits.processes`, `limits.cpu`, `limits.memory`, and the Docker add-on's three keys), its profiles are only the hx desktop profile and, if the desktop has it, the compat profile, and its devices are the `root` disk on the pool and nothing else (the desktop's own `hx-link` proxy device is simply not copied: started beside the desktop it would take over the desktop's host socket). A desktop that carries anything else (`raw.*`, `security.idmap.base` or `size`, `linux.*`, another device, a NIC of its own, `security.privileged` true, `security.idmap.isolated` not true) is refused with `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Volatile keys are not copied, and `boot.autostart` is forced to `false`. The desktop's other plain `user.hx.*` keys (username, recipe, created, `docker_ack`, and so on) are copied. The target is marked as what it is: `user.hx.kind=restore-target`, `user.hx.job=<job id>` and `user.hx.state=restoring` (never the desktop's values for these three), and a source that already has a `user.hx.kind` is refused; until the restore is verified and promoted (architecture 9.8) it is never treated as a desktop, and deleting it requires that kind (and the job id when known). Its isolated map is **not** the desktop's: Incus gives it a range of its own (a copied `volatile.idmap.*` would give two instances one host range), and because the rootfs is stored unshifted the range does not matter for what the worker writes. The worker writes container ids; at the target's first start Incus applies the target's own map. |
| `repo` | all jobs | A proxy device with `bind=host`, `listen=unix:/run/hx/backup/<job>.sock` (uid 0, gid 0, mode 0600), `connect=unix:/run/hx-worker/repo.sock`. Keep both paths short: a long `listen` path is refused by `bind` (the `sun_path` limit of 107 bytes), and the production path is a fixed constant (`/run/hx/backup/` and 32 hex digits, pinned by a test); the length check applies to the test-only constructor. No `security.uid` or `security.gid` is set: the connect side then runs as the helper's own uid 0, which owns the worker's 0700 directory (verified). Incus does **not** remove the host socket when the helper is deleted (verified), so privd removes `/run/hx/backup/<job>.sock` itself (§26, step 5). |

Jobs that only touch the repository (§28: `INIT`, `SNAPSHOTS`, `RESTORE_PLAN`, `FORGET_PRUNE`, `CHECK`, `KEYS`) get `root` and `repo` only; in particular a prune helper has no `/src`, so only repository content can reach it. `/run/hx/backup` is root 0700, created from `systemd-tmpfiles` (`d /run/hx/backup 0700 root root -`, §2); privd opens it with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` and requires owner root and mode 0700, refusing the job otherwise. **The storage paths above assume Incus's `default` project** (a non-default project keeps its volumes in `<project>_<name>` directories); privd uses the default project only. **There is no cache device in v1**: the worker runs `rustic` with no cache (`no_cache`), and a cache volume is added later only if measured index reads on the nightly Drive test are slow (§36, M3).

**Why this mechanism is correct (verified on Incus 6.0.0, kernel 6.8, a btrfs loop pool).** On this Incus the container's rootfs is stored **unshifted** and mounted with an idmapped mount (`volatile.last_state.idmap='[]'` after start, `idmapped` in the root mountinfo; snapshots also carry `last_state.idmap='[]'`). A `shift=true` disk device on that `rootfs` therefore gives a helper with a different map the container's own ids, in both directions: ids 1234:4321 and 1000:1000, a v2 file capability, a user extended attribute and a fifo written through `/dst` were all correct inside the target after it started. This depends on Incus internals, so **privd checks before each job**, and the checks are the only way to build a mount (`SnapshotMount::checked`, `TargetMount::checked`; there is no public unchecked constructor). For every job: `kernel_features.idmapped_mounts` is `"true"` and the pool driver is btrfs. For a backup also: the desktop is a container named `hx-<desktop id>`, carries the hash-xcreen marker and no `user.hx.kind`, has its root disk on the pool, and its `volatile.last_state.idmap` is `"[]"`; the snapshot belongs to that desktop, is an `hx-backup-...` snapshot and has `volatile.last_state.idmap` `"[]"`; every name in the source path is derived from those objects. For a restore: the target is a container named `hx-restore-...` with `user.hx.kind=restore-target`, its status is `Stopped`, it has **never started** (`last_used_at` is zero, there is no `volatile.last_state.power` and no `volatile.idmap.current`), its `volatile.last_state.idmap` is exactly `"[]"` (what a never-started empty instance has on Incus 6.0.0, observed), and its root disk is on the pool. Any failure is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` and the job never runs (architecture §15).

**Worker end of the link.** The worker creates `/run/hx-worker/` (0700) and `repo.sock`, accepts **exactly one** connection and closes the listener. privd connects to the proxy device's host socket, retrying every 200 ms for at most 30 s after the helper starts. A lost connection ends the job; there is no reconnect.

**No half-close.** Observed on Incus 6.0.0: when the host side calls `shutdown(SHUT_WR)`, the forwarder closes the whole connection and the reply is lost. Neither side of any link in Part 3 therefore calls `shutdown(SHUT_WR)` or relies on it. The end of a stream is the `END` flag of a data frame (§25), and the end of a session is `Bye` (§27).

**Drive link.** privd connects to `/run/hx/drive.sock`, a unix socket owned by `hx-drive.socket` (`ListenStream=/run/hx/drive.sock`, `SocketUser=root`, `SocketMode=0600`, `Accept=no`), which activates `hx-drive.service`. That unit runs `hx-backup-worker` with `HX_BACKUP_ROLE=drive` (§6.12) and has the fetcher's hardening: a dynamic user; no capabilities; `RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_INET AF_INET6` (the activated `AF_UNIX` descriptor is inherited, so the unit needs no `AF_UNIX` of its own); the fetcher's `IPAddressDeny`/`IPAddressAllow` and resolver drop-in; the same `SystemCallFilter`; `LoadCredentialEncrypted=drive-token:/etc/hx/creds/drive-token.cred` (privd never loads the token); `MemoryMax=512M`. The binary itself restricts the hosts it will connect to, `www.googleapis.com` and `oauth2.googleapis.com` (how: §29, "The Drive helper's implementation", c). The Drive helper serves **one connection at a time** (a second connection waits in the socket's backlog), checks `SO_PEERCRED` for uid 0 on every accepted connection and closes one that is not, and exits 60 s after the last connection closes. It reads `LISTEN_FDS` and `LISTEN_PID` and serves descriptor 3 (which must be a listening `AF_UNIX` `SOCK_STREAM` socket); it never binds a socket of its own in production. The unit files are `apps/hx-agent/systemd/hx-drive.socket` and `hx-drive.service`, installed with the other units but **not enabled** by the installer: the Drive-connect flow (workstream W5) enables the socket once a token exists.

**No descriptors.** Neither link passes file descriptors. Both ends read with `recvmsg` and treat any control message or `MSG_CTRUNC` as a protocol violation (the rule of §3).

**The worker's sandbox.** The helper's isolation (own isolated user namespace, no capabilities in the initial namespace, the default LXC seccomp profile plus `deny_compat`, AppArmor, cgroup limits, no network interface) is the outer layer. As PID 1 the worker then hardens itself in **three steps**, because the capability set depends on the job kind and the kind is known only from `Job`:

1. **Start-up (before any peer byte is read).** Install the signal dispositions (below); create `/run/hx-worker` (0700) and the listener `repo.sock` (bound, then `chmod 0600`, inside the 0700 directory); accept the one connection (waiting at most 60 s); close the listener and unlink `repo.sock`; set `PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS`, `PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0` and `RLIMIT_CORE=0`.
2. **Handshake, unfiltered.** `Hello`, `HelloAck`, `Job` and the secret frames are processed by the main thread alone, with the full capability set of the helper's root and no seccomp filter. Its only input is privd's frames, which `hx_ipc::repo` validates before anything is allocated or used; the desktop's files are not touched yet and no other thread exists. (Accepted: a bug in the codec at this point runs with the helper's capabilities, in a container with no network and nothing but the repository link and the mounted source or target.)
3. **After the last secret has been delivered, before the first thread starts and before `/src`, `/dst` or `rustic_core` are touched:** drop capabilities to the table below, then install the `seccompiler` allowlist for the job kind. Only then does the worker start its reader, writer and progress threads and run the job. A failure in this step ends the worker with exit code 70 and no `JobResult`.

| Job kind | Capabilities kept |
|---|---|
| `BACKUP` | `CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH` only |
| `RESTORE` | `CAP_CHOWN`, `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, `CAP_FOWNER`, `CAP_FSETID`, `CAP_SETFCAP`, `CAP_MKNOD` |
| every repository-only kind | none |

**Dropping capabilities** means: every capability outside the table is removed from the bounding set; the permitted and effective sets are exactly the table's (never more than the process already had); the inheritable set is empty and the ambient set is cleared; the result is read back with `capget` and the worker exits with 70 if it differs. With `NO_NEW_PRIVS` set and `execve` unavailable, nothing can regain a capability.

**The seccomp allowlist** is built for the job kind (one allowlist program per kind plus two small programs, `clone3` answers `ENOSYS` and the network family answers `EPERM`; the three are installed with `SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_TSYNC`, and threads started later inherit them). A syscall outside it kills the process (`SIGSYS`); a production build has no log mode. The one exception is the network family (`socket`, `socketpair`, `connect`, `bind`, `listen`), which a second program answers with `EPERM` instead of a kill, because glibc's name-service lookups (a user name for a snapshot) try a socket and cope with a refusal; nothing is gained by a refused call. It has no `socket`, `connect`, `bind`, `listen`, `execve`, `execveat`, `fork`, `vfork`, `ptrace`, `mount`, `setns`, `unshare`, `chroot`, `bpf` or `process_vm_*`; `clone` only with thread flags and none of the `CLONE_NEW*` flags (`clone3` answers `ENOSYS`); `prctl` only `PR_SET_NAME`, `PR_GET_NAME` and `PR_SET_DUMPABLE` with 0; `prlimit64` only without a new limit (it can read, never raise). Outside `RESTORE` it also has no call that writes the file system: `openat` and `open` only without `O_WRONLY`, `O_RDWR`, `O_CREAT`, `O_TRUNC` and `O_TMPFILE`, and none of `mkdir`, `symlink`, `link`, `mknod`, `rename`, `unlink`, `rmdir`, `chmod`, `chown`, `utimensat`, `truncate`, `fallocate` or the `*xattr` writes (`setxattr`, `lsetxattr`, `fsetxattr`, `removexattr` and their variants). `RESTORE` adds exactly those, and still has none of the denied calls above. Reading extended attributes is allowed everywhere (a backup records them).

**PID 1.** `SIGCHLD` is set to ignore (the kernel then reaps any orphan, so the worker needs neither `wait4` nor a reaper thread); `SIGPIPE` is ignored (Rust's default); a `SIGTERM` handler calls `_exit(143)`, because PID 1 of a PID namespace ignores a signal that has no handler. The worker never spawns a process.

**Protocol violation.** On any violation (a frame the codec or `LinkState` refuses, a message in the wrong state) the worker writes one line naming the rule to stderr (the rule text of `LinkError` never carries peer data), sends **nothing** (no `JobResult`, no `Bye`), closes the link and exits with code 72. A lost connection or 60 s of silence from privd (§32) exits with 73. privd treats a worker that exits without `JobResult` as a failed job (`WORKER_PROTOCOL` when it saw a violation of its own, otherwise a failure of the helper).

**Exit codes of `hx-backup-worker`.** The code says how the session ended; the outcome of the job is in `JobResult`, never in the code.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | The session ended in order: one `JobResult` (successful or failed) was sent, and `Bye` too, except that `Bye` is skipped when downloads were still running after the 10 s discard wait (§32). |
| 64 | The role fails closed: `HX_BACKUP_ROLE` has a value other than `worker` or `drive`, or the process was given arguments (it takes none), or, in a build without the `test-hooks` feature, any `HX_WORKER_*` variable is set (a release binary must not honour test overrides). |
| 65 | Withdrawn (v0.7): it meant "the Drive role is not built yet". Never reused. |
| 70 | The sandbox could not be set up (`NO_NEW_PRIVS`, dumpable, capabilities, seccomp). The job never started. |
| 71 | The listener could not be created, or no connection arrived within 60 s. |
| 72 | Protocol violation (above). |
| 73 | The link was lost or privd was silent for 60 s. |
| 74 | Internal failure that is not a job failure (a panic outside the job runner). A panic *inside* the job is a failed `JobResult` chosen as in §31 ("Panics"), not an exit code. |
| 75 | Drive role only: the OAuth client file is missing or invalid (§29, "The Drive helper's implementation", a). Nothing is served. |
| 143 | Terminated by `SIGTERM`. |

The Drive role reuses the table with these meanings: `0` the helper exited in order, 60 s after the last connection closed; `64` as above; `70` the early hardening failed; `71` there is no usable activated socket (`LISTEN_FDS` is not 1, `LISTEN_PID` is not this process, descriptor 3 is not a listening `AF_UNIX` `SOCK_STREAM` socket), or `accept` failed for good; `74` an internal failure outside a connection (the runtime could not start, a panic outside a connection); `75`; `143`. The codes `72` and `73` are **not** exit codes of the Drive role: the helper serves many sessions, so a protocol violation, a lost link or 60 s of silence ends that one connection (one line naming the rule on stderr, nothing sent) and the helper goes on to wait for the next, or for its idle exit.

## 25. Framing and limits

privd's framing of §4 applies (`length` u32 LE, `kind` u8, body; `length` is checked before anything is allocated), with three kinds:

| Kind | Body | Maximum `length` | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| `1` Message | one protobuf `RepoFrame` (§27) | 65 536 | both |
| `2` Secret | `request_id` u64 (0 for the job) ‖ `slot` u8 ‖ secret bytes (§5.2, §34) | 8 192 | privd → worker only |
| `3` Data | `stream_id` u64 LE ‖ `flags` u8 (bit 0 `END`, all other bits 0) ‖ payload of 0 to 1 048 576 bytes | 1 048 586 | both |

A data stream's `stream_id` is the `req_id` of the request it belongs to. Streams may interleave; the bytes within one stream are ordered. A data frame for an unknown or finished stream, a stream that exceeds its announced size, and an `END` before `size` bytes have arrived are protocol violations.

**Stream rules** (settled with the codec). A stream carries exactly the announced `size` bytes and ends with a frame whose `END` flag is set. A frame without `END` carries at least one byte; the `END` frame may be empty, so a zero-size object is one empty `END` frame. An upload (`Write`) flows requester to responder, and the responder answers `Done` only after `END`; a download (`ReadBegin`) flows responder to requester. An `Error` answering a `Write` **aborts** the upload but does not finish the request: the sender stops sending payload and, **unless it has already sent the stream's `END`** (the `Error` and the `END` crossed on the wire, in which case the `Error` finishes the request), sends an `END` frame (it may be empty) right away, on either link (privd sends the empty `END` to the Drive helper after an `Error` too, even if all `size` bytes were already sent, unless its `END` was already sent); the receiver discards the stream's data frames up to and including that `END` (never more than `size` bytes in all); only then is the request finished and its write slot free. A data frame after that `END` is a protocol violation. `ReadBegin` is the complete answer to a read, so a failure of the source in mid-stream is signalled by ending the session, never by a second response. A request is outstanding until its last response (`Done`, `Error`, `QuotaResult`, the `ListPage` with `last`), except that a download stays outstanding until the `END` of its stream and an aborted upload until the sender's `END`. `req_id` must be unique among the outstanding requests of a link (a sender numbers its requests upwards from 1 and never reuses a number). The count limits of this section are upper bounds: every message must also fit its 65 536-byte frame (about 600 snapshots fit a `JobResult`; the excludes of a `Job` share the frame with the rest of the spec), and a sender whose message does not fit fails the job instead of sending it.

| Limit | Value |
|---|---|
| Outstanding repository requests | worker link: 8, of which at most 4 writes. Drive link: 10, of which at most 4 writes (8 relayed from the worker plus 2 for privd's own `Quota` and `SetUploadRate`) |
| Object size | at most 96 MiB (packs are capped at 64 MiB by the repository config, plus one 8 MiB chunk) |
| Size caps per type | `CONFIG` 64 KiB, `KEY` 64 KiB, `SNAPSHOT` 4 MiB, `INDEX` and `PACK` 96 MiB. They bound `Write` sizes, `ReadBegin` sizes and `ListPage` entry sizes (repositories are created by hx with these pack limits, and privd never relays a larger object) |
| `ListPage` | at most 1 024 entries per page, 1 000 000 in total; a listing of `CONFIG` has at most 1 entry |
| Repeated fields | counted on the wire before decoding (§8, "Memory") |
| Buffering in privd | at most 2 data frames per stream; privd reads the next chunk only after it has written the previous one. Under `mlockall(MCL_FUTURE)` a frame buffer is locked memory as soon as it is allocated, so privd's worst case per job is about one partial frame plus 2 frames per stream × (4 writes + downloads), roughly 10 MiB; privd caps the total of relay buffers across jobs at **16 MiB: 16 frame slots of 1 MiB**. **Each job reserves 4 slots when it starts** (two readers, each holding one frame it is reading and one in the channel to the loop), so a job never waits for another job's slots, and a fifth concurrent job is refused (`BUSY`, nothing is created); within a job a reader takes one of its slots before it reads a frame; a kind-1 frame (at most 64 KiB, and the channel to the loop holds at most 16) gives its slot back as soon as it is decoded, and a data frame keeps it until it has been written to the other link or dropped. **Every queue of privd's is bounded.** The control queue of a link (small frames: requests, answers, `Ping`, `Cancel`) holds at most **1.25 MiB** (the 64 KiB at which the other link's reader stops, plus the 17 frames of 64 KiB that its channel and its decoder can still deliver, rounded up: honest traffic never reaches it), and at most one `Pong` is pending (a newer one replaces it, so a `Ping` flood costs nothing); a peer that lets the 1.25 MiB fill by not reading is dead: the job fails `SILENT` for **the link whose peer does not read**, at once and not after 45 s. The data queue of a link holds at most 4 frames and 2 MiB. The reader of one link waits while the other link's queue holds 4 data frames, 2 MiB, or 64 KiB of control frames, so a slow peer slows what feeds it instead of growing a queue. A reader that waits for a slot or for the other link keeps the silence clock of its own link fresh: a peer that privd is not reading is not silent, and the peer that is not reading is the one that fails (more than 8 outstanding requests of a worker is a violation at once). **The bounds, stated**: per job, 4 MiB of data frames (its 4 slots), 2 MiB in the two kind-1 channels (2 × 16 × 64 KiB), 2.5 MiB of control queues (2 × 1.25 MiB) and two frames being decoded, about **9 MiB per job and 36 MiB for the four jobs the budget admits**; the data frames of all jobs are the 16 MiB above. Q-H5 stays open as a measurement (§36) |
| Relaying | privd re-encodes every kind-1 frame it relays from the decoded, validated message and never forwards a raw body (unknown fields would be a covert channel) |

A protocol violation makes privd stop the helper and the Drive session, fail the job with `WORKER_PROTOCOL` and audit it. **How privd relays (v0.8).** A request of the worker is checked with `hx_ipc::repo::check_job_op` (§30) before anything is forwarded; `Deny` is `POLICY_DENIED`; `AllowIfAbsent` (`INIT` writing `CONFIG`) is allowed only if privd itself saw an answer to a `List` of `CONFIG` of this job with no entry, and is `POLICY_DENIED` otherwise (the Drive helper refuses to overwrite a config too, §29). privd gives each forwarded request its own `req_id` on the Drive link (counting up from 2; 1 is `OpenRepo`) and rewrites the id on the answer. Every `Write` except of `CONFIG` is hashed as it streams (SHA-256 over the payload bytes) and **privd forwards the worker's final frame, the one with `END`, only after the digest has been compared with `id`**, so that the Drive helper never receives the `END` of an object whose hash is wrong (it commits an upload at `END`); on a mismatch privd closes the Drive session without `END` (the helper drops an upload that never ended), answers the worker `INTEGRITY` and fails the job. A `Write` whose size is not that of its stream is a violation. Frames are always re-encoded from the decoded message and the payloads are relayed without copying (§25). A response, data frame or event that `LinkState` accepts for a request that privd has already answered itself is dropped by the tracker's `discard` flag. A `Write` of a `PACK` or `INDEX` larger than 72 MiB (the Drive helper's cap, §29, "Memory", which hx-made repositories never exceed) is refused by privd with `TOO_LARGE` before it is forwarded: the helper is killed, the job fails `TOO_LARGE`, and the back-off of §31 applies. **Requests are checked against the policy first, in every state**: a request that arrives while the job is being cancelled or after the Drive session was lost is refused `POLICY_DENIED` (audited, backed off) when the policy forbids it, and only then answered `CANCELLED` or `UNAVAILABLE`. When the forwarding of a request fails because the Drive link is gone, the worker is answered `UNAVAILABLE`. After a `Write` of `CONFIG` has been forwarded, privd forgets that it saw the repository without a config, so a second write needs a new empty listing.

## 26. Session and job lifecycle

1. privd checks that the job may start ("Admission", below), takes the repository's job slot and writes the job record (§35), opens its Drive session (`Hello`, `OpenRepo`; §29) so that a revoked token, a lease held elsewhere or an unreachable Drive helper fails the job before a snapshot exists, creates the snapshot (backup jobs), creates and starts the helper container, and connects to it (§24).
2. privd sends `Hello` {`major`, `minor`, `job_id` (16 random bytes)} within 5 s of connecting; the worker answers `HelloAck` {`major`, `minor`, `software`}. A different `major` ends the session. The helper must reach `HelloAck` within 30 s of its start.
3. privd sends `Job` {`job_id`, `kind`, `spec`, `secret_slots`} and then one kind-2 frame per slot. The worker validates the `Job` with the same rules as privd (`hx_ipc::repo::decode`) and hardens itself when the last secret has arrived (§24).
4. The worker runs the job, sending `RepoRequest` and `Progress` frames, and ends it with **exactly one** `JobResult`, then `Bye`, then exits. `JobResult` and the worker's `Bye` are protocol violations while any request is outstanding, with one exception: a failed `JobResult` (`ok` false, `CANCELLED` included) is allowed while the only outstanding requests are downloads whose `ReadBegin` was already sent, whose remaining data frames are still accepted and discarded up to their `END` (an upload or an unanswered request still blocks any `JobResult`, and `Bye` always needs nothing outstanding; after a failed `JobResult` privd may end the session by stopping the helper, which is ephemeral, without waiting for the downloads): a cancelled worker waits for the answers of its outstanding requests (privd answers them, for example `CANCELLED`) and closes aborted uploads with `END` before it sends `JobResult{CANCELLED}`. For a successful `BACKUP`, `snapshot_id` must equal the id of a `Write` of type `SNAPSHOT` of this job that was committed (its `Done` was sent). The helper stops and, being ephemeral, is deleted.
5. privd verifies that the helper is gone (and deletes it if it is not), removes the job's `/run/hx/backup/<job>.sock` (Incus leaves the socket file behind), deletes the snapshot, releases the slot and clears the record. Every part of this cleanup is attempted whatever failed before it ("Cleanup", below).

On the Drive link privd opens a session of its own for the job, starting with `Hello` and `OpenRepo` (§29), and closes it (`Bye`) when the job ends.

**Admission (v0.8).** `start` refuses, before it takes the slot or touches Incus or the Drive link: while crash recovery has not finished (§35); while an uninstall has begun; for a desktop privd does not know; for a writing job (`INIT`, `BACKUP`, `FORGET_PRUNE`, `KEYS`) while a back-off runs (§31, "Effects"); for a `BACKUP` while `needs_attention=BACKUP_TAKEN_OVER` is set for the desktop; for a `BACKUP` when the pool has less than 15% or 2 GiB free (the rule of `CreateSnapshot`; architecture §9.6, "server disk too full"); when the job needs the host's backup key as slot 0 and it cannot be read (§34); while the desktop's slot is held or the relay budget has no 4 slots left (`BUSY`: nothing is queued, the scheduler skips the run, §35); when the encoded `Job` does not fit its frame (a `FORGET_PRUNE` names at most 1 800 ids per job, the oldest first, and the rest go in a later prune; excludes that do not fit refuse the job: `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, before any snapshot or helper exists); and when a `RESTORE` that is not `resumable` finds a kept restore target (`resume.json`, §35). **The slot is taken before anything else is changed**, and a leftover job record of the desktop is cleaned (§35) while the slot is held, so that two admissions cannot clean and start at once. When `/etc/hx/creds/drive-token.cred` does not exist, privd sets `needs_attention=DRIVE_RECONNECT` itself and starts nothing (§29 b: systemd would not start the Drive unit for a missing credential file, and a job would only see `UNAVAILABLE`). A refused job leaves no record, no slot and nothing in Incus.

**Steps and what happens when one fails (v0.8).** Every failure ends in the cleanup below, which a success ends in too. A failure becomes the job's **failure** (the names are the `Failure` values of the internal API: `PRECONDITION`, `SNAPSHOT_FAILED`, `HELPER_FAILED`, `WORKER_PROTOCOL`, `DRIVE_PROTOCOL`, `WORKER_FAILED` (with the `WorkerError`), `REPO` (with the `ErrorClass`), `CANCELLED`, `DEADLINE`, `SILENT`, `GUARD`, `POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE`, `UNAVAILABLE`, `INTERNAL`), the audit log gets one line with the job id, the kind and the failure name (never peer data), and the effects of §31 apply.

| # | Step | On failure |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Take the slot (in memory, per desktop) and write `job.json` before anything else exists (§35) | the record cannot be written: `INTERNAL`; the slot is released |
| 2 | Open the Drive session: connect to `/run/hx/drive.sock` (retrying every 200 ms for at most 5 s), `Hello`, `HelloAck` within 30 s, then `OpenRepo` {`create` = the job is `INIT`; `takeover` = what the caller said, false for every v1 caller but the restore onto a new server and the re-claim} | no socket, no `HelloAck`, end of stream: `UNAVAILABLE`; `OpenRepo` answered `Error`: `REPO` with that class and its effects (§31); a `takeover` that succeeded records a back-off of `TAKEOVER_QUIET_PERIOD` for writing jobs (§29) |
| 3 | `BACKUP`: take the snapshot `hx-backup-<UTC stamp>-<4 hex>` (the desktop lock is held for this call only) | `SNAPSHOT_FAILED`; nothing else exists yet. A cancel or deadline that arrives while the lock is awaited, or before the snapshot is taken, is `CANCELLED` or `DEADLINE`, not `SNAPSHOT_FAILED` |
| 4 | The host checks of §24, create the helper (a `RESTORE` first creates its target with `hx-provision`'s `RestoreTargetSpec`), push the worker, start it | `PRECONDITION` (a check of §24 failed: nothing was created) or `HELPER_FAILED` (an Incus call failed). An instance that is not provably ours is never deleted (§24) |
| 5 | Connect to the proxy's host socket (every 200 ms, at most 30 s after the start), `Hello`, `HelloAck` (within 30 s of the start), `Job`, the secret frames. The Incus proxy accepts before the worker listens and then closes: a connection that ends before `HelloAck` is dropped and privd connects again, inside the same 30 s (each attempt is bounded by the time left to `HelloAck`, not by a fresh 30 s). The writer thread of a dropped connection never touches the queue of the next one | no connection or no `HelloAck` in time: `HELPER_FAILED`; a `HelloAck` that breaks a rule: `WORKER_PROTOCOL` |
| 6 | Relay until `JobResult` and `Bye` | §31 and §32 |
| 7 | Cleanup | below |

**Cleanup (v0.8; step 5 of the lifecycle).** In this order, each part attempted whatever failed before it: (a) close the worker link, and the Drive session (`Bye` first whenever the session is healthy, a cancelled or failed job included, and not after a failure without grace); (b) wait at most 10 s for the helper to be gone (it exits with PID 1 and Incus deletes it), and force-delete it if it is still there; (c) unlink `/run/hx/backup/<job>.sock`, beneath its directory and without following a link; (d) delete the snapshot; (e) a `RESTORE` whose `resumable` flag is set keeps its target and records it (§35), any other restore deletes its target; (f) clear `job.json`; (g) release the slot. When (b), (c) or (d) fail, privd audits it, **keeps `job.json`** and releases the slot in memory only: the leftover is removed by crash recovery for that desktop, which the next admission runs first (§35).

## 27. Messages

`RepoFrame` is a protobuf `oneof` carried in kind-1 frames; it is `hx.repo.v1` in `hx-ipc` (§36). Exactly one variant is set, else the frame is invalid; unknown fields are ignored and field numbers are never reused (§4).

| Variant | Direction | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `Hello` / `HelloAck` | privd → worker / back | Version handshake, §26. |
| `Job` | privd → worker | `job_id`; `kind` ∈ `INIT`, `BACKUP`, `SNAPSHOTS`, `RESTORE_PLAN`, `RESTORE`, `FORGET_PRUNE`, `CHECK`, `KEYS`; `spec` (a `oneof`, §28); `secret_slots` (at most 2). |
| `RepoRequest` | worker → privd (and privd → Drive helper, with privd's own `req_id`) | `req_id` u64 (non-zero, unique per link); `op` (a `oneof`): `List`{`type`}, `ReadFull`{`type`, `id`}, `ReadPartial`{`type`, `id`, `offset` u32, `length` u32}, `Write`{`type`, `id`, `size` u32} (then data frames), `Remove`{`type`, `id`}, `Create`{}. |
| `RepoResponse` | privd → worker (and Drive helper → privd) | `req_id`; `result` (a `oneof`): `ListPage`{`entries` [{`id` 32 bytes, `size` u32}], `last` bool}, `ReadBegin`{`size`} (then data frames), `Done`{}, `Error`{`class`, `retry_after_ms`}. |
| `Progress` | worker → privd | `phase` (enum), `files_done`, `files_total`, `bytes_done`, `bytes_total` (0 = unknown); numbers only; at most 2 per second. |
| `JobResult` | worker → privd | `ok`; `error` (`WorkerError`, §31); counters (`files_new`, `files_changed`, `files_unmodified`, `bytes_processed`, `bytes_added`, and `entries_unreadable`, `BACKUP`: source entries skipped because they could not be read); `snapshot_id` (32 bytes, `BACKUP`); `key_ids` (`INIT`, `KEYS`); `manifest` (`RESTORE_PLAN`; privd validates it as untrusted); `snapshots` (`SNAPSHOTS`, at most 4 096: {`id`, `time`, `tags`, `manifest_digest`, `size`}). |
| `Cancel`{} | privd → worker | Stop at the next safe point (§32). |
| `Ping`{`nonce`} / `Pong`{`nonce`} | both | Liveness (§32). |
| `Bye`{} | both | Orderly end of the session. |

`type` ∈ `CONFIG`, `KEY`, `SNAPSHOT`, `INDEX`, `PACK`; `id` is 32 bytes (all zero for `CONFIG`). **No message carries a path, a Drive name or id, a URL, a credential or free text from the worker.**

**Shapes fixed by the codec.** The job-level `Cancel`{} keeps its name; the Drive link's cancel of one request is the separate variant `CancelRequest`{`req_id`} (§29). `secret_slots` entries are {`slot` 0 or 1, `kind` ∈ `REPO_PASSWORD` (slot 0), `NEW_KEY_PASSWORD` (slot 1), `length` 16 to 1 024}, declared in slot order, and the kind-2 frames follow in that order with `request_id` 0; `INIT` declares slots 0 and 1, `KEYS` declares slot 1 only when `add`, every other kind declares slot 0 only. A `SNAPSHOTS` job carries the empty `SnapshotsSpec`. `ListPage` may be repeated for one `req_id` until the page with `last`; a page before the last one has at least one entry. A `RepoRequest` for `OpenRepo`, `Quota` and `SetUploadRate` is answered like any other (`Done`, or `QuotaResult` for `Quota`, or `Error`); `OpenRepo` must be the first request of the Drive session. Errors from the Drive helper never use `POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE` or `UNAVAILABLE`, which are privd's own (§31). `HelloAck.software` is 1 to 64 bytes of `[A-Za-z0-9._+-]`. `Progress.phase` takes the values of `repo.proto` (repair index, scan and upload, write snapshot, plan, download and write, fix-ups, check, forget, prune, init, keys). `JobResult`: `ok` and `error` agree (`ok` means no `error`; a failure carries no snapshot, manifest or snapshot list, and carries `key_ids` only for a failed `KEYS`: the keys it had added before it failed); `snapshot_id` is set exactly for a successful `BACKUP`, `manifest` exactly for a successful `RESTORE_PLAN` (at most 61 440 bytes, so that it fits the frame; `hx-backup` writes manifests of up to 64 KiB, and a larger one is reported as `ENGINE`), `snapshots` only for `SNAPSHOTS`, `key_ids` (at most 4) for a successful `INIT` and `KEYS` and for a failed `KEYS`, `entries_unreadable` only for a successful `BACKUP`. A snapshot entry's `tags` (here and in the `BACKUP` spec) are at most 4 strings of 1 to 64 bytes from `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`; `manifest_digest` is the SHA-256 of the manifest (all zero when the snapshot has none). `Event.until_ms` is a Unix time in milliseconds (0 = unknown; unbounded in the codec, and privd clamps it to now + 26 h). `RepoError.retry_after_ms` is at most 86 400 000 (24 h).

## 28. Job specs

privd validates the spec before sending it; the worker validates it again.

Bounds beyond the table: the text fields of the manifest input (`distro` id, version and pretty name, `desktop`, `preset`, `agent version`) are at most 128 bytes without control or format characters (Unicode Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp; `hx_backup::manifest` strips exactly that set from guest text); the container configuration has at most 256 entries, keys of at most 128 bytes and values of at most 1 024 bytes (the `hx_backup::manifest` limits) and no key starting with `volatile.`; at most 256 excludes, each non-empty, at most 1 024 bytes (`hx_backup::rules::MAX_PATTERN_BYTES`) and free of control and format characters; `KEYS.remove_key_ids` must not repeat an id (as `forget`). The `INIT` and `FORGET_PRUNE` constants (`compression`, pack size limits, `instant`) must have exactly the values of the table.

| Kind | Spec | Secret slots | Devices |
|---|---|---|---|
| `INIT` | `compression` = 3, `datapack_size_limit` = 64 MiB, `treepack_size_limit` = 16 MiB | 0: the host key (the backup key); 1: the recovery key | `repo` |
| `BACKUP` | The manifest input: distro, desktop, preset, container configuration without `volatile.*`, agent version, backup format version. The user's excludes (at most 256, each at most 4 096 bytes, in the `hx_backup::rules` syntax). `docker_mode` ∈ `COMPLETE`, `LIGHT` and `docker_enabled` (bool: the manifest records the Docker mode only when it is true). Tags: the `hx-backup-…` snapshot name and the schedule kind. A `BACKUP` job always runs `repair index` first, after removing index files that cannot be read; the replaced index files are removed only once the new one is written. | 0: the repository password | `repo`, `src` |
| `SNAPSHOTS` | none | 0 | `repo` |
| `RESTORE_PLAN` | `snapshot_id` | 0 | `repo` |
| `RESTORE` | `snapshot_id`; `verify_existing` (resume an interrupted restore) | 0 | `repo`, `dst` |
| `FORGET_PRUNE` | `forget`: the snapshot ids that privd chose from the retention rules and the floor of the two newest successful backups (at most 4 096), taken **only from the snapshots privd recorded as successful `BACKUP` results** (§36 decision 10); `instant` = true. The job first removes index files that cannot be read, rebuilds the index from the pack headers (`repair index --read-all-packs`, new index written before the old files go) and verifies every copy of a blob stored in more than one pack; it fails the job with `WorkerError::INTEGRITY` (§31), deleting nothing, if a pack file is in no index afterwards or a copy does not verify. privd raises the owner alert and does not retry in a loop (§36 decision 10) | 0 | `repo` |
| `CHECK` | `read_data_percent` 0 to 100 (0 = metadata only) | 0 | `repo` |
| `KEYS` | `add` (bool); `remove_key_ids` (at most 4, named by privd) | 0: a key that opens the repository; 1: the new key (when `add`) | `repo` |

**What the worker returns** (settled with the worker, workstream W3). `JobResult` fields per kind:

| Kind | Result |
|---|---|
| `INIT` | The repository must not exist: the worker lists `CONFIG` first and answers `REPOSITORY_EXISTS` when there is one, without writing anything. It creates the repository (`Create`, then `CONFIG` and the key file for slot 0, the host key), adds slot 1 as a second key, and returns `key_ids` = [id of the slot-0 key, id of the slot-1 key], both 32 bytes. |
| `BACKUP` | `snapshot_id`; `files_new`, `files_changed`, `files_unmodified`; `bytes_processed` = bytes of all files read; `bytes_added` = bytes added to the repository **as stored** (compressed and encrypted); `entries_unreadable`. Rule discovery that fails (budget, an unreadable directory) leaves its paths in the backup, protected paths stay protected, and each failed step is added to the count. Entries that cannot be read (permission, a path longer than `PATH_MAX`, entries beyond the scan budget) are **skipped and counted, never fatal**: a backup whose snapshot was written is `ok` with its `snapshot_id` even when `entries_unreadable` is above 0 (any desktop process could otherwise make every backup fail). privd (W5) reports it as a successful backup with a warning count. `SOURCE_UNREADABLE` is only for a source that cannot be read at all (`/src` missing or its root unreadable). |
| `SNAPSHOTS` | One entry per snapshot, oldest first, at most 4 096 and only as many as fit the frame; when the list does not fit, the job fails with `ENGINE` (§25). `tags` are the snapshot's tags without the engine's constant `hx-backup` tag and without any tag outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, at most 4; `manifest_digest` is the SHA-256 of the bytes `RESTORE_PLAN` would return for that snapshot (all zero when it has no valid manifest); `size` is the manifest's `source_sizes.bytes` (0 when none). |
| `RESTORE_PLAN` | `manifest` = the compact JSON of `hx_backup::manifest::Manifest` with `rustic_snapshot_id` filled in (privd, v0.8, parses it as untrusted input with `Manifest::from_slice` and requires `rustic_snapshot_id` to be the requested snapshot; a manifest that fails either is a failed job, `WORKER_FAILED(ENGINE)`, and is audited); a manifest over 61 440 bytes, or a snapshot without a valid manifest, is `ENGINE`. An unknown `snapshot_id` is `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND`. |
| `RESTORE` | Restores into `/dst` with numeric ids and verifies owner and mode against the snapshot afterwards (a mismatch, a missing capability or a write error is `TARGET_UNWRITABLE`). v1 always verifies the content of files already present (`verify_existing` is accepted; a stale file is never trusted on its size and time). |
| `FORGET_PRUNE` | Deletes **exactly** the snapshots named in `forget` that exist (an id that is already gone is skipped, so a retried job is idempotent), then prunes with `instant` delete. |
| `CHECK` | `read_data_percent` 0 reads metadata only, 100 reads all data. A repository in which the check finds errors ends `CANCELLED` if `Cancel` was received, `REPO` if the link recorded a repository error, else `ENGINE` in v1 (the findings go to the console log at debug level); a distinct code can be added later without breaking the contract. |
| `KEYS` | With `add`, adds slot 1 as a key and returns its id in `key_ids`; then removes each of `remove_key_ids` (a failure to remove, including the refusal to remove the key in use, is `ENGINE`). Add comes before remove, so the opening key is never at risk; a **failed** `KEYS` job reports in `key_ids` the key it had already added, so that privd can record or remove it. |

## 29. Drive link: privd-only operations

The Drive link uses the same framing, `Hello`, `RepoRequest` and `RepoResponse` as §25 to §27, plus the operations below, which only privd may send. The worker's link has none of them. `OpenRepo`, `Quota` and `SetUploadRate` are operations of `RepoRequest` (each with privd's own `req_id`, answered as in §27); `QuotaResult` is a `RepoResponse` result; `CancelRequest` and `Event` are top-level `RepoFrame` variants. The variants valid on the Drive link are exactly `Hello` (carrying the job id), `HelloAck`, `RepoRequest`, `RepoResponse`, `CancelRequest`, `Event`, `Ping`, `Pong` and `Bye`; `Job`, `Progress`, `JobResult`, `Cancel` and secrets never appear on it.

| Message | Direction | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `OpenRepo`{`folder`, `create`, `server_id`, `takeover`} | privd → Drive helper | Names the repository folder (a privd constant per server and desktop, in v0.8 `hx-<first 8 hex digits of the server id>-<desktop id>`, which a restore onto a new server replaces with the folder of the repository it restores from; the worker never learns it; 1 to 128 bytes of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` starting with a letter or digit). `server_id` is the 16 raw bytes. It must be the first request of a session. Lease handling is described below. |
| `Quota`{} → `QuotaResult`{`limit`, `usage`, `usage_in_trash`} | privd → Drive helper and back | From Drive's `about.storageQuota`, which counts the trash (architecture §9.7). The runner of W5a sends none; the quota check before a run and the 50% upload cap while a client is connected (`SetUploadRate`) are W5b (§36). |
| `SetUploadRate`{`bytes_per_s`} | privd → Drive helper | 0 = unlimited. privd sets the 50% cap while a client is connected (architecture §9.5). |
| `CancelRequest`{`req_id`} | privd → Drive helper | Abandon one request (the request is still answered, normally with `CANCELLED`). A download whose `ReadBegin` was already sent is not answered again: its stream runs to its `END`. |
| `Event`{`kind` ∈ `PAUSED_DAILY_LIMIT`, `RESUMED`, `RETRYING`, `SESSION_FAILED`; `until_ms`; `error_class`} | Drive helper → privd, unsolicited | Status. `SESSION_FAILED` (v0.8) says why the helper is about to close the link, below. |

**The Drive helper owns** per-request retries (1 s doubling to 64 s, equal jitter, `Retry-After` honoured, at most 8 retries); the daily-limit gate shared by all requests of the session (a pause of one hour, then a probe, at most 26 pauses); a no-progress timer on every attempt and one deadline per request (below); the lazy creation of the `data/xx` folders; permanent deletion (`DELETE files/{id}`; it never calls `files.emptyTrash`; folders are deleted leaves first; architecture §9.7); and the lease. This is S4's `HxDrive` and retrier, ported to raw objects; the classifier reads the status and the Drive `reason` that the HTTP layer saw, where S4 matched OpenDAL's error text. **privd owns** job deadlines, the operation policy (§30), the hash check of every write, cancellation, the upload-rate setting, the persisted "daily limit hit at T" time, progress and the audit log.

**The lease.** Two servers must not write one repository. A lease object `hx-lease.json` sits in the repository's Drive folder, outside rustic's file types; only the Drive helper reads and writes it, and the worker never sees it. Content: `{format: 1, server_id, generation, taken_at, hostname_hint}`, where `server_id` is 16 random bytes in hex (privd creates it once and keeps it in `/var/lib/hx/backup/server-id`), `generation` is a u64, and `hostname_hint` is at most 64 bytes, sanitized.

- `OpenRepo` with **no lease** in the folder writes one, generation 1. A lease that names **this** server is accepted.
- A lease that names **another** server, with `takeover=false`, is answered with the error `LEASE_HELD` (§31). privd then fails the job and sets `needs_attention=BACKUP_TAKEN_OVER` ("This backup is now used by another server"). Scheduled backups stop until the user re-claims the backup, which is an explicit, owner-authorized action (planned with Phase 2 parts 2C and 2D).
- `takeover=true` is sent only by the restore-onto-a-new-server flow and by that explicit re-claim. It writes the lease with `generation` + 1.
- The Drive helper **re-reads the lease before the first write and before every `Remove`**, and may cache it for at most 60 s. A server that has lost the lease gets `LEASE_HELD` and its job ends.
- After a takeover the new owner starts **no write job for 2 minutes**, longer than the cache window, so that an old owner's in-flight prune cannot delete packs that the new owner reuses. Concurrent appends from an old owner's in-flight backup are harmless, because objects are content-addressed; only removals are dangerous, and those are fenced by the re-read.
- The Drive helper refuses a `Write` of `CONFIG` when a config object already exists (defence in depth for `INIT`'s "only if absent", §30), **and a `Write` of `SNAPSHOT` when that snapshot already exists** (v0.8; the check is made before the first attempt, so the helper's own retries still overwrite), both with `PERMISSION`. A worker therefore cannot re-write a snapshot it did not create and report it as its own. privd adds its own half: it notes the snapshot ids that the worker listed or read in the job, and a successful `BACKUP` whose `snapshot_id` was one of those ids **before the job wrote that snapshot** is **not recorded** (§35); the audit line says why (the snapshot was listed or read before its write). Reading one's own snapshot after writing it does not count.
- Drive offers no compare-and-swap for this object, so a small window remains. It is accepted and documented: it takes two takeovers racing each other, which means a user doing two restores at once.

**The Drive helper's implementation (v0.7, settled for W4).** The code is `apps/hx-backup-worker/src/drive/`. These points were open or contradictory in v0.6; each is now fixed.

- **a. The OAuth client.** There is one fixed client for every install (architecture §5.4). Its `client_id` and `client_secret` are read once at start from the root-owned, world-readable file `/usr/lib/hx/current/drive-client.json` (`{"client_id": "...", "client_secret": "..."}`, at most 4 KiB, both strings 1 to 256 bytes of printable ASCII, no other key). **No real value is committed to the repository or generated**, and the secret of an installed-app client is not confidential (Google's own wording), which is why it is a plain file and not a credential. **The file is not shipped yet:** the release workflow does not write it and privd's update extractor does not list it, so today an install has no client file and the role exits 75. Shipping it (a release step that writes it from a repository variable, and the extractor's file list) is an open item for W5 (§36). A missing, unreadable, oversized or malformed file makes the Drive role print one line naming the file (never its content) and exit **75** before it reads the token or serves a connection (fail closed). Tests use files with fake values.
- **b. The `drive-token` credential.** `LoadCredentialEncrypted=drive-token:/etc/hx/creds/drive-token.cred` puts the decrypted bytes at `$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY/drive-token`. Format v1: a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly one key, `{"refresh_token": "..."}` (at most 2 048 bytes in all; the value 16 to 1 024 bytes of `[A-Za-z0-9._~/+=-]`; an unknown key is refused so that a future format cannot be half read). The file is read once per session (so a replaced credential is picked up by the next job), into a zeroizing buffer and a zeroizing string, never logged, never put in an error. This is as far as zeroization goes: `serde_json`'s intermediate copies and OpenDAL's own copy of the token (the operator's options and its signer, which build the token request) are ordinary heap strings that are freed, not wiped. The token request is sent as a form body, never as a query string: OpenDAL builds it with the token, the client id and the secret in the query, and the HTTP layer moves that query into the body, percent-encoded (the helper hands OpenDAL percent-encoded values, so a token with `+` or `/` arrives intact), and strips the query from every URI it attaches to a response, so no error carries a URI with a secret. **An absent, unreadable or malformed credential does not end the process**: the helper still answers `Hello`, and then answers every `OpenRepo` with `AUTH_REVOKED` (there is no token to refresh and the remedy is the one for a revoked token: connect Drive again), so privd gets a defined class instead of a closed socket. (If systemd cannot load the credential at all, the unit does not start and privd sees `UNAVAILABLE`; privd therefore checks that `/etc/hx/creds/drive-token.cred` exists before it starts a job and sets `DRIVE_RECONNECT` itself when it does not: W5.)
- **c. The host restriction.** Every HTTP request of the helper passes one function that refuses anything but `https`, the default port, no user information and exactly `www.googleapis.com` or `oauth2.googleapis.com`. The HTTP client is `hyper-util`'s pooled HTTP/1.1 client on a hand-written connector, so it has no redirect following (a 3xx is an error of class `INTERNAL` and is not retried), no proxy support (the environment's `HTTP_PROXY` and friends are not read) and no HTTP/2 to turn off. The connector checks the host again, resolves it itself and drops every address that is not global unicast (the unit's `IPAddressDeny` is the second layer), and speaks TLS 1.3 only through `rustls` with the `ring` provider (no `tls12`) and the platform's root store (`rustls-native-certs`, as the update fetcher). `reqwest` was rejected: its rustls stack turns on `rustls`'s `tls12` feature, which `deny.toml` bans, and it brings a certificate bundle under a licence outside the allowlist. OpenDAL's own retry layer is not installed.
- **d. The test seam (`test-hooks` only).** In a build without the feature none of this code exists and any `HX_WORKER_*` variable is refused (exit 64): `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_SOCKET` (bind and serve this path instead of the activated descriptor), `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_API_ADDR` (`host:port`: after the host check, requests to either Google host are sent to this address, and the certificate must be valid for that IP), `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_CA` (a PEM file of additional trusted roots), `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_CLIENT` (the client file), `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_PEER_UID` (the uid `SO_PEERCRED` must show instead of 0) and `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_IDLE_MS` (the idle exit). All start with `HX_WORKER_DRIVE_`, which the release workflow greps for in the shipped binary. The library API takes the same values as a typed `DriveConfig`, and the retry timing as a scalable `Timing`, so tests need no clock tricks.
- **e. Exit codes.** Table in §24: 0, 64, 70, 71, 74, 75 and 143; code 65 is withdrawn.
- **f. No seccomp filter of its own.** The unit's `SystemCallFilter` applies, and the binary sets `NO_NEW_PRIVS`, `PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0` and `RLIMIT_CORE=0` before it reads any peer byte or touches the token (the worker's `early_hardening`). A second allowlist for a process that runs tokio, `rustls` and a musl resolver would have to track their syscalls across versions and would turn a missed call into a `SIGSYS` kill in production, for a small gain over the unit's filter plus `RestrictAddressFamilies`, no capabilities and a dynamic user. W6 (the security review) may reopen it.
- **g. The upload-rate cap.** One token bucket shared by all concurrent uploads of a session, measured on the bytes of upload request bodies as they are written to the connection (chunks of at most 64 KiB, each released at the time the rate allows; no initial burst). `SetUploadRate{bytes_per_s}` replaces the rate for chunks not yet released, including a chunk that is already waiting (its wait is worked out again when the rate changes, so lifting the cap releases it at once); 0 is unlimited, and a nonzero rate below 2 KiB/s is raised to 2 KiB/s (a smaller one would make a single chunk wait for hours). The rate is per session and starts unlimited. Reads are not limited.
- **Where the repository lives in Drive.** `OpenRepo.folder` is a folder of that name directly under the owner's My Drive root (with `drive.file` the app sees only what it created). `create=true` creates it if absent; `create=false` and no such folder is answered `NOT_FOUND`. `Create`{} creates `keys`, `snapshots`, `index` and `data` below it (never the `data/xx` folders, which appear when a pack is first written to them); it counts as a write for the lease. Objects are `config`, `keys/<hex>`, `snapshots/<hex>`, `index/<hex>` and `data/<first two hex digits>/<hex>`, as restic and rustic lay them out.
- **Listings** are collected in full before the first `ListPage` is sent; a failure of any page of the Drive listing (after the retries) is a single `Error`, never a truncated list followed by an error. The helper counts entries while Drive pages (at most 100 000 in all, under the codec's 1 000 000, for memory) and counts the requests of one listing (at most 4 096); an endless or repeating `nextPageToken` therefore ends in `INTERNAL`, not in memory. There is no timer on the listing as a whole, only the no-progress timer of each request (below), so a long listing is never cut off for being long. Names that are not an id of the type, and folders, are skipped; an object larger than its type's cap is a listing failure (`INTERNAL`, logged with the status only). A `List` of `CONFIG` is a `stat` of `config` and answers one entry, or `last` with none.
- **Reads** are streamed in ranged chunks of at most 4 MiB. A `ReadFull` of a `CONFIG`, `KEY` or `SNAPSHOT` (at most 4 MiB), and a `ReadPartial` of at most 4 MiB, is one read done before `ReadBegin`, so its failure is an ordinary `Error`. A `ReadFull` of an `INDEX` or `PACK`, and a longer `ReadPartial`, first `stat`s the object (a size over the type's cap, or a range past the end, is `INTERNAL`), then reads the first chunk, then sends `ReadBegin{size}` and the data, one chunk at a time, each retried as a whole. A failure after `ReadBegin` cannot be an answer (§25): the helper closes the connection, and only the writer does it, **between two frames**, so privd sees a clean end of stream and never a half frame. **On the Drive link an end of stream at a frame boundary, with no `Bye`, is the loss of the session, not a protocol violation** (§32); only a frame that is cut short is a violation. Before it closes the link the helper sends `Event{SESSION_FAILED, error_class}` with the class of the failed attempt (a gate exhausted, a revoked token; v0.8, decision 12), so that privd learns what failed. After `ReadBegin` only the no-progress timer applies to the stream (below), not the request deadline, because the request has been answered. A `ReadPartial` returns exactly `length` bytes or fails with `INTERNAL`; a short answer means the index is wrong and is not retried. The size of a body is enforced as it arrives: a response longer than the chunk (or, for anything but a media read, 4 MiB) aborts the read with `INTERNAL`, whatever its `Content-Length` says.
- **The session failure event (v0.8, W5-2 decided: adopted).** `Event.kind = SESSION_FAILED` and `Event.error_class` (field 3 of `hx.repo.v1.Event`, an `ErrorClass`) are added to `repo.proto`. The helper sends the event on its control queue immediately before it queues the close ("Reads", above), so privd reads it before the end of stream. `error_class` is set exactly for `SESSION_FAILED`: it must not be `UNSPECIFIED` or one of privd's own classes (`POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE`, `UNAVAILABLE`, §31), and it must be 0 for every other kind; `hx_ipc::repo` refuses an event that breaks either rule. The class is that of the failed attempt, `INTERNAL` where the source gave no better. privd treats the event as the loss of the Drive session (§32) with that class: the end of stream that follows is not a violation and is not audited as one, and the failure of the job takes the class (so `AUTH_REVOKED` sets `DRIVE_RECONNECT` even when the failure was met in the middle of a download). An end of stream with no such event before it is `UNAVAILABLE` (a crashed helper). The worker never sees the class: it sees `UNAVAILABLE` for its outstanding requests.
- **Memory.** Uploads are held whole until their `END` (a retry needs the body); the link allows 4 at once and cannot be slowed without silencing `Ping`. The helper therefore caps a `Write` of a `PACK` or an `INDEX` at **72 MiB** (§28: a pack is at most 64 MiB plus one 8 MiB chunk, a tree pack 16 MiB; the codec's per-type ceiling of 96 MiB in §25 is a bound for any peer, not what repositories made by hx contain): a larger `Write` is answered `INTERNAL` at once, its data frames are dropped as they arrive, nothing is buffered. Reads hold one chunk (4 MiB) at a time from a 32 MiB budget, and a chunk's permit is kept until its frames are queued; at most 16 frames of 1 MiB wait in the outgoing queue. The sum, against `MemoryMax=512M`: uploads 4 × 72 = 288 MiB; read chunks 32; queued frames 16; up to 10 frame builders of 1 MiB (one per running download) 10; the decoder's frame 1; the relay channels and hyper's buffers of up to 10 responses about 10; a listing of 100 000 entries about 25; runtime, TLS and stacks about 30. Total about 412 MiB, a margin of about 100 MiB, and a compromised worker cannot raise it, because the cap is enforced here and not only by privd.
- **Permanent delete.** OpenDAL's `gdrive` service deletes by moving to the trash (S4, architecture §9.7). The helper's HTTP layer rewrites exactly that request (`PATCH /drive/v3/files/{id}` with the body `{"trashed":true}`, and, when the request carries OpenDAL's operation marker, only if it is `Delete`) into `DELETE /drive/v3/files/{id}` and presents the 204 as the 200 OpenDAL expects. `emptyTrash` is never called. A `Remove` acts on **files only**: the helper `stat`s the path first and refuses a folder with `INTERNAL`, because a `DELETE` of a folder deletes its subtree. A test asserts that no trashing request reaches Drive. **Folders:** there is no message that deletes a folder in v1, so no path of a session does; the helper has the leaves-first tree delete (`DELETE` of every file and folder, deepest first) ready and tested for the disconnect flow, which needs its own `RepoRequest` operation (reserved, §6.14).
- **Retry budget.** Besides the 8 retries, **a request** (not each Drive call of it: a `Write` makes several, an `OpenRepo` several more) is given up after 30 minutes of its own time. **Time the request spends at the daily-limit gate and time its upload spends waiting for the upload cap do not count**: a capped upload is healthy for as long as it progresses. One deadline is carried through every call of the request, and an attempt is capped at what remains of it. After `ReadBegin` a download has no deadline, only the no-progress timer. There is no total-size timeout on an attempt (four 64 MiB uploads at a 50% cap on a slow uplink take hours and are healthy): an attempt fails when it makes **no progress** for 60 s, that is, when no request byte is written, no response byte is read and no header arrives for 60 s (a chunk waiting for the upload cap counts as progress while it waits, in slices of at most 1 s, and the cap is never below 2 KiB/s). Connecting tries each address in turn, a failed or timed-out TCP connection or TLS handshake included, with one 5 s bound per address for the TCP connection and the TLS handshake together, so one blackholed address cannot use up an attempt. After the last retry the error class is the one of the last attempt; `RATE_LIMITED` carries the last `Retry-After` (or the last backoff delay) in `retry_after_ms`. A `401` from the Drive API is first treated as an expired access token: the helper builds a fresh operator (a new token exchange) and repeats the request once; only a second `401`, or `invalid_grant` from the token endpoint (at once), is `AUTH_REVOKED`.
- **The daily-limit gate** counts pauses per session, not per request: a request that meets `dailyLimitExceeded` while the gate is already closed (or was closed after its attempt began) waits at the gate and does not start another pause. When a pause ends, **one** request probes: the others keep waiting until the probe has a **response that is not `dailyLimitExceeded`** (its headers are enough: a success reopens the gate for everyone at once, even if the transfer goes on for a long time; another `dailyLimitExceeded` starts the next pause). Only a response of the **Drive API** counts as the probe's answer (a 2xx or 3xx, or a 404 or 412); an answer of the token endpoint never does (the probe's first request after a pause is usually a token refresh), nor does a 5xx, a 429, a 401 or another 403. A token exchange of the helper's own (for `Quota` or the folder check) is never a probe and never reopens the gate. `Event{PAUSED_DAILY_LIMIT, until_ms}` is sent when a pause starts and `Event{RESUMED}` when the gate reopens. When the 27th pause would be needed the gate is exhausted: every waiting request and every later one fails with `DAILY_LIMIT` without another attempt. `Event{RETRYING, until_ms}` is sent with each retry delay, best effort (dropped when the link's queue is full).
- **Cancellation** abandons the work of a request at its next await (a backoff, the gate, a Drive call) and answers `CANCELLED`; for a `Write` whose `END` has not arrived yet it answers at once and the upload is closed as in §25. `CancelRequest` for a request that is not outstanding is ignored. A download whose `ReadBegin` is sent runs to its `END`.
- **Hostile answers.** Google's answers are untrusted. Every response body is counted as it is read and the read is aborted (and the relay task and its connection dropped) when it exceeds its ceiling: the range or the type's cap for a media read, 4 MiB for anything else, 64 KiB for an error body, which is read up to that size and then dropped with its connection, not drained. A `Content-Length` over the ceiling fails at once. The requests of one attempt are counted (4 096) as well.
- **Control replies.** `HelloAck`, `Pong` and the answer to a cancelled upload go through a queue of their own that the writer empties before the data queue, so a link whose data frames are backed up (privd slow to read) still answers `Ping` and `Cancel`. The reader never waits for the data queue.
- **Stopping.** The unit says `SuccessExitStatus=143`: the helper keeps the `SIGTERM` handler of the worker (exit 143, §24), and systemd must not mark a stopped unit as failed for it.
- **A panic** in the work of one request is answered `INTERNAL` (detail on stderr, nothing from the peer or from Google in it) and the session goes on; a panic anywhere else ends the helper with 74.
- **The lease, completed.** `taken_at` is Unix seconds; `hostname_hint` is the host's name (`gethostname`; `ProtectHostname=yes` only stops the helper from changing it) with every byte outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` replaced by `-`, cut to 64 bytes, `unknown` when empty (v0.6 named the field but not its source); `server_id` is lower-case hex. A lease object larger than 4 KiB, or that does not parse as `format` 1, is **unreadable**: `OpenRepo` without `takeover` answers `INTERNAL` (detail in the log without content), with `takeover` it replaces the object with generation 1. `Create`, `Write` and `Remove` are the mutating requests. The first mutating request of a session always reads the lease from Drive; every `Remove` reads it from Drive again; the other mutating requests accept a lease read at most 60 s earlier (`LEASE_CACHE_MAX`). When any of these checks finds a lease that does not name this server (another `server_id`, or this server with a different generation than the helper holds), the request is answered `LEASE_HELD` and so is every later mutating request of the session; reads go on. A failure to read the lease is that request's `Error` with the class of the failure. An `OpenRepo` that failed leaves the session without a repository, and every later repository request is answered `INTERNAL`.

## 30. Policy

privd checks every request from the worker against the job kind before forwarding it. `—` means not allowed.

| Job kind | List | Read | Write | Remove | Create |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `INIT` | all types | `CONFIG`, `KEY` | `CONFIG` (only if absent), `KEY` | — | yes |
| `BACKUP` | all | all | `PACK`, `INDEX`, `SNAPSHOT` | `INDEX` only | — |
| `SNAPSHOTS`, `RESTORE_PLAN`, `RESTORE`, `CHECK` | all | all | — | — | — |
| `FORGET_PRUNE` | all | all | `PACK`, `INDEX` | `PACK`, `INDEX`; `SNAPSHOT` only for the ids in the spec | — |
| `KEYS` | all | all | `KEY` | `KEY` only for the ids in the spec | — |

On every `Write` except `CONFIG`, privd computes SHA-256 over the streamed body and commits the upload (sends `END` to the Drive helper) **only if the digest equals `id`**; otherwise it aborts the upload and answers `INTEGRITY`. A denied operation is answered `POLICY_DENIED`, is audited and fails the job. (The `Create` column is the reading of §36, Q-C1.)

**What this table does not protect (Q-S1, settled in §36 decision 10).** The policy stops a `BACKUP` worker from removing packs and snapshots, but it holds the password, so it can write validly encrypted `INDEX` files and `PACK` files that lie, and privd's hash check proves only that an id is the SHA-256 of its bytes. The table is therefore **not** what keeps a later prune safe: that is the `FORGET_PRUNE` job's own verification of the index before it plans (§28). `INDEX` removal stays allowed for `BACKUP`, because `repair index` needs it to heal an index that lists packs that no longer exist (after an interrupted prune), and because every forging attack that matters works without removing any index. privd must never treat the worker's index, its listings of snapshots or the snapshot ids it reports as evidence for a deletion.

## 31. Errors

`RepoError.class`, as seen by privd and relayed to the worker. privd classifies a failed job by the errors it relayed itself, never by the worker's claim.

| Class | Meaning and handling |
|---|---|
| `NOT_FOUND` | The object is absent. An empty `List` of `CONFIG` means there is no repository; this is normal flow, not a failure. |
| `TRANSIENT` | Network errors, 5xx, 408, 405, after the Drive helper's retries. Fail the run and reschedule. |
| `RATE_LIMITED` | 429 or a 403 rate-limit reply, after retries. Fail the run and reschedule. |
| `DAILY_LIMIT` | The daily-limit gate gave up after about a day. Fail the run; start no upload job for one hour. |
| `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` | Drive is full: the handling of architecture §9.7. |
| `AUTH_REVOKED` | 401 or `invalid_grant`. Set `needs_attention=DRIVE_RECONNECT` ("Action needed: reconnect Google Drive"). |
| `PERMISSION` | Any other 403, for example `appNotAuthorizedToFile`. Fail the run and audit it. |
| `LEASE_HELD` | Another server owns the repository (§29). Set `needs_attention=BACKUP_TAKEN_OVER`; scheduled backups stop until the user re-claims the backup. |
| `POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE` | privd itself refused the request (§25, §30). Kill the helper, fail the job, audit it and back off. |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | The Drive helper is unreachable. Fail the run and reschedule. |
| `CANCELLED` | The job was cancelled (§32). |
| `INTERNAL` | A bug; detail goes to the journal only. |

`WorkerError` in `JobResult`: `ENGINE`, `SOURCE_UNREADABLE`, `WRONG_PASSWORD`, `NO_REPOSITORY`, `REPOSITORY_EXISTS`, `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND`, `TARGET_UNWRITABLE`, `CANCELLED`, `REPO` (a relayed repository error; privd uses its own record), and `INTEGRITY` (added with Q-S1; **the worker's own refusal**: the engine's index verification before a prune failed (§36 decision 10: a pack in no index, a pack that lists a blob twice or mixes blob types, a blob whose entries have different types, or a copy of a duplicated blob that does not verify), and nothing was deleted; `FORGET_PRUNE` only; it is distinct from privd's relay error class of the same name in the table above, and privd alerts the owner and does not retry it in a loop). **No free text crosses the link.** Detail goes to the worker's console log, which privd never parses: the `WorkerError` is logged at warn; engine error text, paths of the user's files and check findings only at debug. privd's own failure reason for a job is `WORKER_PROTOCOL` for a protocol violation (§25) and otherwise one of the classes above.

**Effects of a failure on privd's state (v0.8).** privd classifies a failed job by what it relayed itself: the job's **repository class** is the class of the first `Error` it relayed whose class is neither `NOT_FOUND` nor `CANCELLED`, or that of a `SESSION_FAILED` event (§29); a `JobResult` of `REPO` takes it (and `INTERNAL` when there is none); privd's own refusals (`POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE`, `UNAVAILABLE`) win over relayed ones. These effects apply whenever privd relayed such an error, **also when a cancel or a deadline is what ended the job** (the class is the repository's state, not the job's fate), and a successful `JobResult` that had been accepted wins over a failure that follows it (a lost link, silence, a violation after the result): the job succeeded, its backup is recorded, and the outcome says so; the failure that followed is audited all the same (`backup job <id>: a failure after the accepted result was ignored: <name>`). Then:

| Class or failure | Effect |
|---|---|
| `AUTH_REVOKED` | `needs_attention=DRIVE_RECONNECT` (host-wide; cleared by `SetBackupCredentials`, W5c), audit |
| `LEASE_HELD` | `needs_attention=BACKUP_TAKEN_OVER` for the desktop (cleared by the re-claim, 2C), audit |
| `PERMISSION` | audit: the first `Error` of this class in a job, naming the operation and the object type (never an id, a name or peer data), and the count of the rest in the job's final audit line; a refused `Write` of `CONFIG` is the case that matters (an app not authorized for the folder) |
| `DAILY_LIMIT` | back-off of 1 hour for writing jobs (§35, `backoff.json`) |
| `POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE` (a `Write` of a `PACK` or `INDEX` over 72 MiB, §25) | the helper is killed, the Drive session closed without `Bye`, audit (job id, kind, class), back-off of 1 hour for writing jobs; `INTEGRITY` also raises the owner alert |
| `JobResult` `WorkerError::INTEGRITY` (the engine's own refusal before a prune) | audit, the owner alert, back-off of 1 hour for writing jobs; never retried in a loop |
| `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, `TRANSIENT`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `UNAVAILABLE` | none beyond the failure (the scheduler reschedules, the drive-full prune is 2B) |

The **owner alert** goes through privd's internal alert hook (`AlertSink`: the alert names the kind, the desktop and the job, and carries no peer data). The default sink writes an audit line `alert: integrity ...`; the real ntfy and webhook alerts (architecture §9.9) are 2B and replace the sink without touching the runner. `needs_attention` gains two wire values with this version (additive, `privd.proto` `NeedsAttention`): `DRIVE_RECONNECT` = 8 and `BACKUP_TAKEN_OVER` = 9; gatewayd forwards only the values 1 to 7 to clients until the client protocol learns them (W5b).

**Panics.** `rustic_core` 0.14 panics on some damaged or forged repository data (a corrupt pack in a restore, a forged size hint in an index, in any job that loads the index). The worker runs every job kind inside `catch_unwind`; a panic ends the job with a failed `JobResult` (`CANCELLED` or `REPO` when the link's record says so, else `ENGINE`), never a crash without a result.

**How the worker chooses a `WorkerError`** (the first rule that matches). (1) The link failed (violation, lost, silent): no `JobResult`; see §24. (2) `Cancel` was received and the engine stopped: `CANCELLED`. (3) The engine's error is a failure of the repository or of reading it (rustic's own error, a snapshot lookup, a missing manifest, a closed engine) **and** the link backend recorded a `RepoError` of a class other than `NOT_FOUND` during the job: `REPO` (the class is for privd's record only; it never crosses the link). An engine integrity failure (the prune's index verification) is tested the same way first, because that verification reads the repository: `CANCELLED` if `Cancel` was received, `REPO` if a repository error was recorded, and only otherwise `INTEGRITY`. (4) By the engine error: a wrong key, or a key that is not valid text, is `WRONG_PASSWORD`; no repository where one is required, `NO_REPOSITORY`; a repository where `INIT` found one, `REPOSITORY_EXISTS`; an unknown snapshot, `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND`; a local file-system error that stops the read of the source itself, `SOURCE_UNREADABLE`, and on the restore target, or a missing capability, or a verification mismatch, `TARGET_UNWRITABLE`; an engine integrity failure, `INTEGRITY`; anything else, `ENGINE`. `TARGET_UNWRITABLE` is chosen only for local I/O errors (rustic's input/output kind, with no link failure in the error's cause chain; damaged repository data met during a restore without a link failure is `ENGINE`): a restore that fails because a repository read failed follows the rules above (`REPO`, or `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND` or `ENGINE`). Content-write failures that `rustic_core` turns into panics (a full disk in the middle of a file) stay `ENGINE` in v1; privd's own pool monitoring detects a full disk. `INTEGRITY` comes from the prune's index verification (`Engine::prune`, §36 decision 10). A `NOT_FOUND` answer is normal flow in the engine (an empty `CONFIG` listing) and never selects `REPO` by itself.

## 32. Liveness, timeouts and cancellation

- privd sends `Ping` every 15 s on both links (`Ping` and `Pong` are valid from the moment `Hello` has been sent or received, also before `HelloAck`). A peer silent for 45 s is dead: privd force-stops the helper and closes the Drive session. "Silent" means that no byte at all has been read from the link for 45 s (a frame that arrives slowly keeps it alive); the clock starts when the link is connected. The worker exits after 60 s without anything from privd. **All timers of this section run on a monotonic clock** (privd's `Monotonic`, injectable for tests): a step of the wall clock changes none of them. The loop looks at the clock every 100 ms; when it finds that more than half the silence timeout (22.5 s) has passed since its last look (the host was suspended or starved), the silence baselines and the ping schedule of both links restart, because privd could not have read from either link: it never calls a peer silent for time in which it was not listening.
- The worker sets **no per-request timeout**. privd's guard timer for a Drive request is **not** a total time from the request: it is built from the helper's own rules (§29, "Retry budget"), and it is implemented as follows (v0.8). privd keeps, for every outstanding Drive request, the time of its last **sign of life**: privd sending the request and each data frame of an upload, a `ListPage`, the `ReadBegin` and each data frame of a download, an `Event` of any kind (it restarts every outstanding request), and the answer. A request is **stale** when more than `DRIVE_GUARD` = 31 minutes (the helper's 30-minute request budget plus one minute) have passed since the later of its last sign of life and the end of the current daily-limit pause (`PAUSED_DAILY_LIMIT.until_ms`, clamped to now + 26 h, until `RESUMED` or until that time). There is no total: a request that shows a sign of life every 30 minutes lives for ever, and a **download that has begun** (`ReadBegin` sent) and an **upload whose `END` has been forwarded** have no guard at all: the first is covered by the helper's 60 s no-progress timer, the second is a capped upload, healthy for as long as the helper answers `Ping`, and the helper's own request deadline and the job deadline are their backstops. The silence rule above (45 s) is the only fixed liveness timer. A stale request is the loss of the Drive session (below) with the failure `GUARD` and the class `UNAVAILABLE`.
- **End of stream on the Drive link.** The Drive helper closes the link without `Bye` when a download fails after `ReadBegin` (§29), after `Event{SESSION_FAILED}`. It does so between two frames, so privd's decoder ends cleanly (`Ok(None)` at a frame boundary): privd treats that as the loss of the Drive session, **not** as a protocol violation, and does not audit it as one. A frame cut short in the middle is a violation (`DRIVE_PROTOCOL`, audited). **The loss of the Drive session** (v0.8): every outstanding relayed request that has not begun a download is answered `UNAVAILABLE` (a download that has begun cannot be answered: its worker is cancelled), every later request of the worker is answered `UNAVAILABLE` at once, `Cancel` is sent to the worker and its grace of 30 s applies (below); the job fails with the class of the `SESSION_FAILED` event, or `UNAVAILABLE` (a `GUARD` failure for a stale request). The same end of stream on the **worker** link is not a violation either (a cut frame is, `WORKER_PROTOCOL`): without a `JobResult` it is `HELPER_FAILED`, and after one it is the normal end of a worker that skipped `Bye` (§24).
- Job deadlines:

| Job kind | Deadline |
|---|---|
| `INIT` | 30 min |
| `KEYS`, `SNAPSHOTS`, `RESTORE_PLAN` | 10 min |
| `CHECK`, `FORGET_PRUNE` | 6 h |
| `RESTORE` | 12 h |
| `BACKUP` | 30 h (it must outlast the daily-limit gate) |

- **Cancel.** privd sends `Cancel`; the worker starts no new writes and answers `JobResult{CANCELLED}` within 30 s (also during a restore or check, whose in-flight downloads do not hold it up: §26). Otherwise privd force-stops the helper (ephemeral, so it is deleted), verifies that it is gone, and closes the Drive session. A killed backup leaves orphan packs, which the next `repair index` reuses, and no snapshot file, because rustic writes the snapshot last. **How privd cancels (v0.8).** A cancel (a caller's, or a deadline) sends `Cancel` once and `CancelRequest` for every outstanding Drive request that has not begun a download (so that the helper answers them `CANCELLED` at once), keeps relaying (an aborted upload is closed as in §25), and starts the 30 s grace. A `JobResult` of any kind ends the grace; without one after 30 s privd force-stops the helper.
