The outcome is `CANCELLED`, or `DEADLINE` when the job deadline fired, whatever the worker's result says, **except** that a successful `JobResult` that was already on its way wins (the job finished before it saw `Cancel`) and the job succeeds. A link silent for 45 s, a protocol violation on either link and a refusal of privd's own (`POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE`) get **no grace**: the helper is force-stopped at once and the Drive session is closed without `Bye`. The job deadlines count from step 1 of §26.
- **What the worker does on `Cancel`.** It raises the engine's cancel token and from that moment sends **no new request of any kind** (a call into the backend fails at once, which is what stops a running `rustic_core` call, which cannot otherwise be interrupted). A `Write` whose request is already sent runs to its `END` (a stream cannot end early), or is aborted by privd's `Error` and closed with `END` (§25); every other outstanding request is waited for. When only started downloads are left it sends the failed `JobResult{CANCELLED}` (§26), then waits at most 10 s for those downloads to reach their `END` (their data is discarded) and sends `Bye` once nothing is outstanding; if downloads are still running after 10 s it exits without `Bye` (privd stops the helper, §26). The same applies to every failed `JobResult` that is sent while downloads are outstanding. A job that had already finished successfully when `Cancel` arrived reports its success.

## 33. Progress

The worker sends `Progress` {`phase`, `files_done`, `files_total`, `bytes_done`, `bytes_total`} at most twice per second, numbers only, never a path or text. privd adds its own relay counters and turns the result into §4 `Progress` frames for the caller of `BackupStatus` or `RestoreStatus`, at most 10 per second:

- `stage` is `BACKUP` or `RESTORE`; `CHECK`, `PRUNE` and `INIT` reuse `BACKUP`.
- `index` and `total` come from privd's fixed phase table below; `step` is privd's own fixed English text for the phase, never worker text; `percent` is computed by privd, 0 to 100, or 255 when unknown.
- The additive fields `bytes_done`, `bytes_total` and `transfer_bytes` (§4) carry the byte counts, because the restore screen shows bytes and never promises a duration (architecture §9.8, item 6).

The worker maps the engine's steps to `Progress.phase` as follows: repair index, `REPAIR_INDEX`; resolving the rules, measuring the source and reading and uploading, `SCAN_AND_UPLOAD` (and `WRITE_SNAPSHOT` from the moment the snapshot object is written); the restore comparison, `PLAN`; writing files, `DOWNLOAD_AND_WRITE`; the owner and mode verification and the mount points, `FIXUPS`; `CHECK` for check and read-data; `FORGET`, `PRUNE`, and `INIT` and `KEYS` for those jobs. `files_*` carry counted items and `bytes_*` carry byte counts of the current step, which restart at 0 when the phase changes. The worker sends the latest numbers at most every 500 ms from a thread of its own, never from an engine thread.

| Job | Phases (index from 1) |
|---|---|
| Backup | 1 snapshot, 2 start worker, 3 repair index, 4 scan and upload, 5 write snapshot, 6 clean up |
| Restore | 1 prepare target, 2 plan, 3 download and write, 4 fix-ups, 5 boot check |

**What privd reports (v0.8).** `step` is one of these fixed English texts, never a byte of worker text: Backup: 1 `Taking a snapshot`, 2 `Starting the backup worker`, 3 `Repairing the index`, 4 `Scanning and uploading`, 5 `Writing the snapshot`, 6 `Cleaning up`; Restore: 1 `Preparing the restore target`, 2 `Planning the restore`, 3 `Downloading and writing files`, 4 `Fixing ownership and modes`, 5 `Checking that the desktop boots` (reported by the promotion step, 2C; the worker never reaches it). Every other kind has its own three phases: 1 `Starting the backup worker`, 2 its work (`Creating the repository` for `INIT`, `Managing keys` for `KEYS`, `Listing snapshots` for `SNAPSHOTS`, `Planning the restore` for `RESTORE_PLAN`, `Forgetting and pruning` for `FORGET_PRUNE`, `Checking the repository` for `CHECK`), 3 `Cleaning up`, reported with the stage `BACKUP`. The worker's phases map as: `REPAIR_INDEX` to Backup 3; `SCAN_AND_UPLOAD` to Backup 4; `WRITE_SNAPSHOT` to Backup 5; `PLAN` to Restore 2; `DOWNLOAD_AND_WRITE` to Restore 3; `FIXUPS` to Restore 4; `CHECK`, `FORGET`, `PRUNE`, `INIT`, `KEYS` to the work phase of their kind (a phase the kind's table does not have is ignored). `percent` is that of the current phase: `bytes_done` over `bytes_total` when the total is known, else `files_done` over `files_total`, else 255, clamped to 100; the phases privd runs itself report 255 while they run and the last frame of a finished job is 100 with `ok`. `bytes_done` and `bytes_total` are the worker's numbers of the current phase; `transfer_bytes` is privd's own count of the object bytes it relayed to the Drive helper (uploads) or from it (downloads) in the job. The three byte fields are added to `privd.proto`'s `Progress` (fields 8, 9, 10) with this version. **At most 10 frames per second per job**: the mapper emits a frame when 100 ms have passed since the last one (the monotonic clock) and holds the newest otherwise; a held frame goes out at the next tick, a phase change and the last frame of a job included (the newest wins; the throttle is strict, so a job's last frame may be dropped when the job ends before the interval has passed, and the outcome, not a frame, is what ends a job). The latest frame is also what `status` returns.

## 34. Secrets on the link

Two kinds of repository secret exist, both carried in kind-2 frames under the rules of §5.2 (`Zeroizing` buffers, never in a protobuf field, argv, environment or log): `REPO_PASSWORD` (slot 0) and `NEW_KEY_PASSWORD` (slot 1), each 16 to 1 024 bytes. The Drive helper never receives a repository secret, and the worker never receives a Google credential.

**Keys.** A repository has **one master key** and two rustic key files for it, so stock `rustic` or `restic` can open it with either password:

| Key file | Password | Held by |
|---|---|---|
| Backup key (`BACKUP_KEY`) | 32 random bytes, base64url, generated on the server by the installer's `credentials` stage (§2, architecture §5.7); never transmitted | privd, from `LoadCredentialEncrypted=backup-key`. It reads it into a `Zeroizing`, mlocked buffer at job start and sends it as the kind-2 frame for slot 0. |
| Recovery key | A second password of at least 128 bits, in a human form (for example Crockford base32 in groups), added with `Repository::add_key` | Generated by privd. An `INIT` or `KEYS` job adds it. privd persists **only its rustic key id** (rotation means adding a new key and removing that id) and never the key. |

**Sealing the recovery key to the owner key.** privd seals the recovery key for the client with HPKE **base mode** to the enrolled owner public key (§5.2), with `info` = `hx-recovery-key/v1` and `aad` = `len(desktop_id)` (u8) ‖ `desktop_id` ‖ the 32-byte rustic key id. The sealed box travels through gatewayd as ciphertext it cannot open or substitute, because gatewayd cannot replace the enrolled owner key (unlike a device key). Enrolment of the owner key is therefore a prerequisite for showing the recovery key (§5.2).

**Restoring onto a new server.** The user's recovery key travels to the new server as the sealed kind `RECOVERY_KEY` (§5.2: owner-authenticated, with a privd-issued nonce). privd passes it to the worker as slot 0 of the `RESTORE_PLAN` and `RESTORE` jobs; then a `KEYS` job (slot 0: the recovery key, slot 1: the new server's backup key) adds the new server's `BACKUP_KEY` to the repository. The restore flow takes over the lease with `takeover=true` (§29).

**What the worker does with a key.** It holds it only for the job: `PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0` (§24), a helper with `limits.memory.swap=false`, an ephemeral container deleted at the end. `rustic` keeps unzeroized internal copies of key material; this is accepted and stated (§36).

**How privd reads the backup key (v0.8).** For a job whose slot 0 is the host's backup key (every job on a server that made the repository; `RESTORE_PLAN`, `RESTORE` and the `KEYS` job on a new server take the recovery key from the caller instead), privd reads `$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY/backup-key` (the file systemd's `LoadCredentialEncrypted=backup-key:/etc/hx/creds/backup-key.cred` makes; the directory is the one `PrivdConfig` accepted for the pepper, §2) at admission, not earlier and not cached: a regular file (not followed through a link) owned by root with no group or other permission, 16 to 1 024 bytes after one trailing newline is removed, no NUL. The bytes go into a `Zeroizing` buffer that is moved into the kind-2 frame and dropped (wiped) as soon as the frame is written. A missing or malformed credential refuses the job with `FAILED_PRECONDITION`; its content is in no log, audit line, error, progress step, argv or environment. The unit's `LoadCredentialEncrypted=backup-key` line is added with the installer's credentials work (W5c); until then the directory has no such file and no job starts.

## 35. Single writer, job record and crash recovery

**One job per repository.** privd holds a per-repository **job slot**: one job of any kind at a time. In v1 a repository is a desktop, so the slot is per desktop id (the in-memory slot of the runner plus `job.json`). The scheduler skips a scheduled run while the slot is held (architecture §9.5). A backup holds the desktop lock only while it creates the snapshot (§8). `DestroyDesktop` and `Uninstall` cancel the active job first and wait for its cleanup (below). Across servers the same guarantee is the lease (§29).

**The job record.** While a job holds the slot, privd keeps `/var/lib/hx/backup/<desktop_id>/job.json` (root, 0600, written atomically: temporary file, `fsync`, `rename`, `fsync` of the directory; the directory 0700). It is written before the helper is created (every name in it is chosen first) and removed only after cleanup (§26, step 5). A file that exists but does not parse is not reset: privd refuses to start jobs for that desktop and audits it, and crash recovery deletes the objects it can name by their markers and leaves the file for an administrator. Exactly these fields, one JSON object:

```json
{"format": 1,
 "job_id": "<32 lower-case hex>",
 "kind": "BACKUP",
 "desktop_id": "main",
 "started_unix": 1800000000,
 "helper": "hx-bkw-1a2b3c4d",
 "snapshot": "hx-backup-20261127T030000Z-ab12",
 "snapshot_id": null,
 "target": null,
 "target_job": null,
 "resumable": false}
```

`kind` is the `JobKind` name of §27 (`INIT`, `BACKUP`, `SNAPSHOTS`, `RESTORE_PLAN`, `RESTORE`, `FORGET_PRUNE`, `CHECK`, `KEYS`); `helper` is always the name derived from `job_id`; `snapshot` is a string for `BACKUP` and null otherwise; `target` is the `hx-restore-...` instance name for `RESTORE` and null otherwise; `snapshot_id` is the 64 hex digits of the snapshot a `RESTORE` restores and null otherwise; `target_job` is the job that **created** the target (the target's `user.hx.job`: this job's id for a new target, the kept record's for a resumed one) and null when there is no target; `resumable` is the caller's flag of a `RESTORE` (false for every other kind): the restore flow (2C) sets it when it will resume or promote the target, and it is what step 4 of crash recovery reads. Unknown fields are refused, so that a future format cannot be half read.

**The completed-backups record.** `/var/lib/hx/backup/<desktop_id>/completed.json` (root, 0600, atomic) is privd's own list of the backups it completed, **the only input of retention** (§36, decision 10; `FORGET_PRUNE`'s `forget` list is built from it and from nothing else, never from a listing of the repository or a worker's report of what exists):

```json
{"format": 1,
 "backups": [
   {"snapshot_id": "<64 lower-case hex>", "job_id": "<32 hex>", "time_unix": 1800000000,
    "files_new": 12, "files_changed": 3, "files_unmodified": 900,
    "bytes_processed": 123456, "bytes_added": 7890, "warnings": 0}
 ]}
```

An entry is added when a `BACKUP` job's `JobResult` is successful, has passed `LinkState` and names a snapshot that the job did not list or read before it wrote it (§29) (its `snapshot_id` is a committed `SNAPSHOT` write of this job), **before** the job's cleanup starts and with `fsync`; `time_unix` is privd's clock at that moment, not the repository's; `warnings` is the job's `entries_unreadable` (a backup with unreadable entries is a success with a warning count, §28). At most 4 096 entries (the `forget` limit); an addition beyond that drops the oldest entry's *record* only (the snapshot stays in the repository and privd never forgets it: unrecorded means untouchable). A crash between the worker's success and the write leaves a snapshot unrecorded, which is the safe direction. `FORGET_PRUNE`'s builder keeps the **floor** (the two newest recorded backups by `time_unix`) and applies the retention rules (architecture §9.7; every 12 h: the latest 14 plus 4 weekly plus 6 monthly; every 24 h: 7 daily plus 4 weekly plus 6 monthly; the buckets are UTC days, ISO weeks starting Monday and UTC months, and a bucket keeps its newest backup) to the recorded entries only; the ids it returns are the recorded entries kept by none of them. An id is removed from the record when a `FORGET_PRUNE` that named it succeeded. A snapshot that is in the repository but not in the record is neither counted in retention nor in the floor and is never forgotten automatically. A record that cannot be read is **never rewritten from nothing**: an update that finds `completed.json` unreadable is skipped and audited (the file stays for an administrator), and only a `FORGET_PRUNE`, which needs the record, refuses to start on it; a backup does not read it at admission.

**Recovery and two kept targets.** If crash recovery finds a `job.json` that names a resumable target and a `resume.json` that names another, only one kept target per desktop is ever recorded: the older one (the `resume.json`'s) is deleted and audited, and the job's is recorded.

**Resumable restores (v0.8).** A target is kept, and `resume.json` written, only when the host reports that the target exists, **also when the job resumed it**: a kept target that has vanished (deleted outside privd) fails the job once, clears `resume.json` and is audited, so a restore never loops on a target that is gone. `resume.json` names the target, the snapshot it was written for and the job that created it. A `RESTORE` with `resumable` set that finds a `resume.json` **for the same snapshot resumes it**: the helper is built on the kept target instead of a new one, `verify_existing` is forced true, and `resume.json` is written again with the same `target`, `snapshot_id` and `job_id`. For a **different snapshot** a resume would mix two snapshots (a restore writes and verifies files, it never removes the ones a different snapshot did not have), so privd first **deletes the kept target and its record** (audited), and starts fresh. A `RESTORE` that is not `resumable` is refused (`BUSY`-class) while a kept target exists: `resume.json` is never silently overwritten and the older target is never abandoned. **A successful resumable restore leaves its target kept and `resume.json` in place**, for the promotion of 2C; until then a desktop with a kept target accepts only resumable restores. The runner's internal `discard_resume(desktop)` (W5b exposes it) deletes the kept target (marker checks as in §24) and then the record, audited, and is refused while the desktop's slot is held. What promotes a kept target is 2C (§36).

**Other records.** `/var/lib/hx/backup/<desktop_id>/resume.json` (0600, atomic; present only while a restore target is kept): `{"format": 1, "job_id": "<32 hex>", "target": "hx-restore-...", "snapshot_id": "<64 hex>", "resumable": true}` (`job_id` is the job that created the target, kept across resumes)`; `/var/lib/hx/backup/<desktop_id>/backoff.json`: `{"format": 1, "until_unix": 1800003600, "reason": "DAILY_LIMIT"}` with `reason` ∈ `DAILY_LIMIT`, `POLICY_DENIED`, `INTEGRITY`, `TOO_LARGE`, `TAKEOVER` (a later `until_unix` replaces an earlier one and an earlier never replaces a later); `/var/lib/hx/backup/server-id` (§29): 32 lower-case hex digits and a newline, created once with `getrandom`, mode 0600.

**Crash recovery at privd start, before any backup command is admitted** (v0.8: the runner refuses `start` until recovery has finished, and recovery is started by `serve` next to the isolation check, in the background; every step is idempotent and a step that fails does not stop the rest. A failure that the host cannot attribute (a listing of helpers or of targets, the stop of the Drive unit, the run directory) keeps the whole runner closed; a failure that belongs to one desktop (its snapshots, its records) blocks **that desktop** only, and the others run. Recovery is repeated with a back-off (30 s, doubling to 15 minutes). It is **surfaced** through the runner's `recovery_state()` (done, the blocked desktops with the reason, the attempts), which W5b shows; there is no `needs_attention` value for it. The audit log gets a line when the set of failures changes and a count when it repeats, never one line per retry):

1. force-delete every instance marked as a backup helper (`user.hx.kind=backup-worker`, an `hx-bkw-<8 hex>` name and a `user.hx.job` whose first 8 digits match the name);
2. remove every `/run/hx/backup/*.sock`;
3. delete every `hx-backup-*` snapshot;
4. keep an `hx-restore-*` target (an instance marked `user.hx.kind=restore-target`; one without that marker is never touched) only if a record says the restore can resume (`resumable: true` in `job.json` or `resume.json`; the worker is then run with `verify_existing`, §28), and delete it otherwise;
5. stop `hx-drive.service`;
6. mark the job interrupted and clear the slot, **only when steps 1 to 4 all completed** (a listing that failed means that nothing is known about what is kept: the records are left exactly as they are and recovery is repeated, so a resumable target and its records survive a failed listing): `job.json` is removed (after the objects it names are gone) and a kept restore target is recorded in `resume.json` (the record must still say `resumable`, and the instance must carry `user.hx.kind=restore-target` and the job id of the record); a `resume.json` that names no instance is removed. The `resume.json` it writes names the job that **created** the target (the record's `target_job`) and the record's `snapshot_id`, so a resumed target keeps its original `user.hx.job`. The report of the run (what was deleted, kept and failed) is audited.

**`DestroyDesktop` and `Uninstall`** (v0.8: both call the runner's `teardown_cleanup` before they touch the instance or the pool, under the locks they already hold) first cancel the active job of the desktop and wait for its cleanup (at most 60 s, then the same force-stop), and then run steps 1 and 4 (helpers and restore targets) and remove the job's sockets, `resume.json` and `job.json`. **`DestroyDesktop` is scoped to its desktop**: it cancels only that desktop's job and deletes only the helpers, targets and sockets that belong to it (named by the desktop's `job.json`, `resume.json` or active job, or carrying one of their job ids in `user.hx.job`); another desktop's job and kept target are left alone, and an object that no record attributes is left for crash recovery. `Uninstall` is host-wide: it cancels every job and removes every helper, target and socket. They delete restore targets whether or not they are resumable (step 4's "keep if resumable" applies to crash recovery only): a leftover helper or target would make the pool "in use" and the teardown fail. `Uninstall` in keep mode leaves the desktop alone and does the same for the objects of jobs. This is what replaces the "stale repository locks" of the S4 design: `rustic_core` has no lock files, so the repository needs none, and what can be stale is the job record (and, across servers, the Drive lease, §29).

## 36. Decisions and open items

### Decisions

Taken by the maintainer and the design review on 2026-11-26.

1. **The recovery key is sealed to the owner key, not the device key** (M1). gatewayd can substitute device keys but cannot substitute the enrolled owner key (§34).
2. **The backup key is generated on the server** (M2): by the installer's `credentials` stage, as `backup-key.cred`, and never transmitted. A Phase 1 host gets it by re-running the installer once. The client-supplied `BACKUP_KEY` kind is withdrawn and its number reserved (§5.2). The recovery key is a second key file for the same master key.
3. **The lease: the new server wins** (M4, §29).
4. **No index cache in v1** (M3): the worker runs `rustic` without a cache, and there is no `cache` device (§24). Run times are measured on the nightly Drive test, and a cache volume is added only if index reads prove slow.
5. **Helper limits are accepted for now** (M5): 1 GiB memory and 2 CPUs for a helper, `MemoryMax=512M` for the Drive helper. See the open items.
6. **The Drive helper is a role of `hx-backup-worker`** (M6), selected by `HX_BACKUP_ROLE=drive`, following the fetcher's `HX_FETCH_TEMPLATE` precedent (§6.12).
7. **privd relays; the worker has no direct path to Drive** (§23), and verifies SHA-256 on every write (§30).
8. **A helper container, not a host child of privd.** Rejected, with reasons: a host child of privd in a user namespace (privd's bounding set is `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, `CAP_NET_RAW` and `CAP_IPC_LOCK` with `RestrictNamespaces=yes`; unshifted storage would need an idmapped mount, which needs `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`; and it would walk a hostile tree from the host); systemd `PrivateUsers` or idmapped `BindPaths` (not available on systemd 252 to 255); the Incus file API or SFTP (slow, loses extended attributes, brings hostile data into privd); Incus backup tarballs (a full copy at each run); a long-lived helper with an image rootfs (S4).
9. **The transport is an Incus proxy device and a unix socket**, not restic's REST protocol (an HTTP server in root privd, or TCP over the shared bridge; no typed errors, job control, secret channel or progress channel; a basic-auth password), not OpenDAL in privd, and not a tmpfs file hand-off.

10. **Q-S1: a prune never trusts the index; `FORGET_PRUNE` verifies it first** (settled with `crates/hx-backup/tests/prune_safety.rs`, on the real `rustic_core` 0.14.0, every attack made through a backend that applies the §30 `BACKUP` policy and privd's SHA-256 write check, every honest run through the `FORGET_PRUNE` policy). Findings, for an attacker that holds the password and can list and read all, write new `PACK`, `INDEX` and `SNAPSHOT` files and remove any `INDEX`:

    | Attack (all within the §30 `BACKUP` policy) | `rustic_core` prune with `instant_delete` | After the fix (`Engine::prune`) |
    |---|---|---|
    | a. remove every `INDEX` | fails ("Tree ID not found in index"), nothing deleted; repository unreadable until a repair | index rebuilt from the pack headers, prune succeeds, check and restores pass |
    | b. forged `INDEX` with bogus blob ids, fewer blobs for a real pack, or a wrong pack size (the earliest-`time` entry for a pack id wins over the real one: `PrunePlan::new` dedup in `prune.rs`) | fails ("blob missing in index files", "pack size does not match": `check` and `check_existing_packs` in `prune.rs`), nothing deleted | healed by the rebuild, prune succeeds |
    | c1. remove every `INDEX`, write one `INDEX` that lists decoy data packs (valid header, corrupt data) carrying the real packs' blobs | **data loss**: the real packs are in no index and `prune_repository` deletes unindexed packs first ("removing unindexed packs" in `prune.rs`); check fails, every restore fails | refused with `INTEGRITY`, nothing deleted, all real packs intact |
    | c2. keep the real indexes, add an `INDEX` that lists decoys whose ids sort after the real packs' | **data loss**: a blob listed twice counts as used in the last pack processed (`PackInfo::from_pack`), so the real pack is unused and deleted | refused |
    | c3. add an `INDEX` that marks each real pack deleted with an early time and no blobs, plus decoys (id order irrelevant) | **data loss** (`decide_marked_pack`: marked, no used blobs, not too young: `Delete`) | refused |
    | c4. write only the decoy `PACK` files; the next honest `BACKUP`'s `repair index` indexes them from their headers | **data loss** | refused |
    | e1. replace a partly used real pack's index entry with swapped blob locations (each a valid ciphertext) | **data loss**: repacking decrypts the wrong ciphertext without error and stores it under the blob id (no hash check), then deletes the old pack; check and restore fail | rebuilt from the headers, prune succeeds, data intact |
    | d1. forged `SNAPSHOT` with a missing tree, dated in the future | prune fails for as long as the snapshot exists (retention keeps the newest); nothing deleted | same: a denial of service until privd forgets that snapshot |
    | d2/d3. forged `SNAPSHOT`s dated in the future | `Engine::forget` counts them in the keep rules and in the two protected "newest" snapshots: two forged snapshots displace all four real ones | same: retention must not be computed from the repository's listing |

    "Data loss" means the surviving snapshots no longer restore. Every case in which prune loses data has the same shape: a decoy pack that **lists the real pack's blobs**, and prune keeping the decoy. The attacker cannot remove or overwrite `PACK` and `SNAPSHOT` files, so when it makes the decoys the real data is still in the repository: only the index and the decoys lie. Mitigations evaluated:

    - **(i) `repair index` before planning.** The default (header scan only for unindexed packs, entries with a matching size trusted) does **not** close c1 to c3 or the "fewer blobs" variant: the decoy entry has the right size and a header scan of a pack that exists finds a valid header. `--read-all-packs` reads the header of every pack (one listing and one small ranged read per pack; 2 250 bytes in 12 reads against 1 266 bytes in 4 reads for prune alone on an 8-pack, 4.7 MB test repository). It rebuilds the index from what the packs say, which closes a, b and e1, and turns c1 to c4 into duplicates that (iii) catches. It is `restic repair index --read-all-packs`: the format does not change. rustic removes every old index file *before* it reads the headers, so a kill or one failed header read leaves the repository without an index; the engine therefore holds the removals back through its backend wrapper, writes the new index first, reads the header of a pack it missed once more, and removes the old files only when the new index covers every pack file (tests `m1_*`).
    - **(ii) Never delete packs that are in no index.** `rustic_core` has no option for it (instant delete always removes them). The engine checks, after the rebuild, that every pack file is in some index, and after the plan that `packs_unref` is zero, and refuses otherwise (this also stops a pack whose header read failed from being deleted as unreferenced). Cost: one listing. Without `instant_delete` prune only marks packs and deletes them 23 hours later (tested: the files are still on disk and check fails): that buys a day for someone to notice, but nothing reads the data in that day and §28 needs instant deletion to free Drive space.
    - **(iii) Verify every copy of a blob that is stored in two packs.** This closes c1 to c4: a decoy must list real blobs to displace a real pack, so after the header rebuild it always shows up as a duplicate. Reading all data does not settle it: `check --read-data` reads only the one copy per blob that the index lookup picks (in `check.rs` the packs come from `index_be.into_index()` filtered by what `check_trees` used), while prune keeps the copy in the highest pack id, so with a decoy index check passes on some runs and fails on others (both observed). The engine reads every index entry of such a blob itself (ranged reads of those blobs only, adjacent ones merged), decrypts it with the repository key, decompresses it with the output bounded by the entry's declared length (a zstd bomb cannot grow) and compares SHA-256 with the blob id; it refuses naming the pack unless every entry verifies. A pack that lists the same blob twice is refused outright: a decoy that listed each real blob twice, a verbatim copy first and junk second, passed a check of one entry per pack while prune kept the junk entry and deleted the real pack (test `xs1_*`). Honest duplicates (an interrupted prune after repacking leaves every repacked blob in two packs, and tree packs are repacked on most prunes) verify and the prune cleans them up (tests `h2_*`). Cost: no pack reads on a repository without duplicates; otherwise the duplicated blobs, read from Drive in full, at most the larger of 2 GiB and a quarter of the bytes of all pack files (else the prune is refused and the owner is alerted): a repository of 400 GB can make one prune read up to 100 GB. Memory: about 100 bytes per blob while the index is scanned (a 1 TB repository with 128 KiB blobs: roughly 800 MB, which M5 must cover; see below).
    - **(iv) Restrict `BACKUP`'s `INDEX` removal (a policy change).** Rejected: b2, b3, c2, c3 and e1 write only new files and c4 writes only packs (the tests assert that no index is removed), so it closes nothing, and it breaks `repair index` after an interrupted prune (tested: a backup under a policy without `INDEX` removal fails when an index lists a missing pack). §30 and `hx_ipc::repo::policy` are unchanged.
    - **(v) Verify pack data against the index before deleting.** `rustic_core` 0.14.0 exposes no per-pack read (`check` has no pack-id subset and its reads follow the lookup), so the engine does it for the duplicated blobs with the cipher and zstd crates `rustic_core` already depends on (now direct dependencies of the `engine` feature; `docs/tech-stack.md`). This is mitigation (iii).

    **Decision:** `Engine::prune`, the `FORGET_PRUNE` implementation, removes index files that cannot be read, rebuilds the index from the pack headers (new index written before the old files are removed), refuses with `WorkerError::INTEGRITY` (nothing deleted) if a pack file is in no index afterwards or if any copy of a blob stored in two packs does not verify, and only then plans and prunes with `instant_delete`. No policy change. privd's side: the `forget` ids come from privd's own record of successful `BACKUP` results (the job record's `snapshot_id`), never from a listing of the repository. A snapshot that privd did not record is neither counted in retention nor in the floor of the two newest, and is not forgotten automatically (privd's record can be lost, and forgetting every unknown snapshot would then delete every backup); a `FORGET_PRUNE` that fails because of one, or with `INTEGRITY`, raises an alert for the owner and is not retried in a loop. The worker deletes exactly the ids of the spec (the engine's rule-based `forget` must never be used for this job).

    **The invariant** the engine establishes before a prune plans (`engine/integrity.rs`, tests `xs_*`, `xs1_*`, `xt*_`, `m3_*`, `l7_*`): after the rebuild, no pack mixes blob types or lists a blob twice (rustic's packer never writes either, so a repository written by another tool that does is refused until repaired); every blob with more than one entry has one effective type across all its entries (rustic files an entry under its pack's type, looks blobs up per type, but counts uses by id alone: verbatim copies of a data pack's blobs in a pack typed tree used to pass verification and displace the real pack); and every one of those entries decrypts, decompresses to its declared length and hashes to the blob id. rustic's prune keeps the copy in the pack processed last and its lookup returns any entry of the right type, so a decoy can only win by sharing a blob id with a real pack, and the invariant leaves it no entry that is not a faithful copy of the same type. A blob may be as large as the 96 MiB object cap (§25), since trees are not chunked.

    **Junk index files (H1).** A hash-valid `INDEX` file that cannot be decrypted used to block restore, check, backup and prune for good. Now `BACKUP` and `FORGET_PRUNE` remove such files (they hold no data: the pack headers hold everything; the §30 policy allows both to remove `INDEX`), and read-only jobs (`RESTORE`, `CHECK`, `SNAPSHOTS`, `RESTORE_PLAN`) leave them out of the index listing without removing anything (test `h1_*`). A backend that fails to read the file is an error, not junk. Cost: one read of every index file per job that loads the index.

11. **The Drive helper (W4, v0.7)**: async OpenDAL (`gdrive` only) on a small tokio runtime inside the `drive` role, one hand-written HTTP transport over `hyper-util` and a hand-written TLS connector (rustls with `ring`, TLS 1.3 only) that owns the host check, the permanent-delete rewrite, the upload-rate bucket and the capture of Drive's real status and reason (OpenDAL's own error text does not carry the reason), the client file and the credential formats of §29 a and b, and no seccomp filter of its own (§29 f). The Drive code lives in `apps/hx-backup-worker/src/drive/`, not in a `drive` feature of `hx-backup`: it has no use for rustic, and a feature of a library crate would put OpenDAL one `cargo tree` away from `hx-privd`.

12. **The session failure event: adopted (W5-2, v0.8).** A failure after `ReadBegin` is delivered to privd as `Event{SESSION_FAILED, error_class}` sent before the Drive helper closes the link (§29). Considered and rejected: leaving privd to guess from its own record (it cannot tell a revoked token from a crashed helper, so `DRIVE_RECONNECT` would never be set for a failure in a download and a gate that gave up would be retried at once), and a second `RepoResponse` for a read that has begun (§25 forbids it). The cost is one additive enum value, one field and a rule in the codec.
13. **The guard timer is a staleness timer (v0.8).** privd's guard for a Drive request restarts on every sign of life, has no total, is suspended during a daily-limit pause, does not apply to a download that has begun or an upload whose `END` was forwarded, and is 31 minutes (§32). The only fixed liveness timer is the 45 s silence of a link, with `Ping` every 15 s on both links.
14. **The Drive session is opened before the snapshot (v0.8).** A revoked token, a lease held elsewhere or an unreachable Drive helper then costs no snapshot and no helper; the lifecycle of §26 is otherwise unchanged.
15. **privd keeps its own record of completed backups (v0.8)** and builds `FORGET_PRUNE`'s `forget` list only from it (§35): the floor of two and the retention rules count recorded backups, an unrecorded snapshot is never forgotten, and `INTEGRITY` (the worker's refusal or privd's own hash check) raises the owner alert through an internal hook and a back-off of one hour (§31).
16. **Relay buffers are capped at 16 MiB across jobs (v0.8)** (§25); Q-H5 remains a measurement.
17. **Timers run on a monotonic clock that tests can drive (v0.8)**: every timeout of §26 and §32 is measured with `Monotonic`, not with the wall clock.

### Open items

These are **not decided**; each needs the named measurement or review.

| Item | Question |
|---|---|
| Q-H1 (likely blocking for "Drive token sealed with `systemd-creds`") | privd has no `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` and `/dev/tpmrm0` is normally `tss:tss` 0660, so privd probably cannot run `systemd-creds encrypt` or `decrypt` with the TPM when `SetBackupCredentials` arrives. Options: `SupplementaryGroups=tss` on privd, `--with-key=host`, or a root oneshot sealing unit. Needs the security review; there is no TPM on the development host. |
| Q-H2 | Repeat the helper probes (§24) on Incus 6.0.5 (Zabbly), the distro Incus of Debian 13 and Ubuntu 26.04, and the Debian 12 kernel 6.1: unshifted storage plus an idmapped root mount, a disk-device source under `/var/lib/incus/storage-pools` accepted, the same directory layout, and an ephemeral helper that is deleted automatically when PID 1 exits. |
| Q-H3 | Restore through the helper of POSIX ACLs, hard links, sparse files, `security.*` extended attributes other than capabilities, and Docker `overlay2` data. A user namespace cannot set `trusted.*` attributes. Covered by the Phase 2C restore test. |
| Q-H4 | Does `limits.disk.priority` affect a VPS's block devices? (`io.weight` needs BFQ or `io.cost`.) Measure. |
| Q-H5 | The relay's CPU and memory cost in privd at full upload speed under `mlockall`. The cap on relay buffers across jobs is set (16 MiB, §25, decision 16); the measurement may lower or raise it. |
| M5 | Whether 1 GiB and 2 CPUs per helper and `MemoryMax=512M` for the Drive helper fit the 2 vCPU / 4 GB minimum host. Measure in the Gate 2 run and adjust, including the prune's blob-to-pack map (about 100 bytes per blob, §36 decision 10) on a large repository. |
| Q-S1b | What remains of Q-S1 after decision 10. (1) A decoy pack can still corrupt *future* backups that deduplicate against its blobs: old data can no longer be lost, but new snapshots can be made unrestorable. The periodic `CHECK` with data read and the restore drill are the defence, and a clean `CHECK` does not prove the repository free of decoys (it reads one copy per blob). (2) A forged snapshot with a missing tree, a junk pack with no readable header, or more duplicated blobs than the verification limit makes `FORGET_PRUNE` fail with `INTEGRITY` until the owner acts (a denial of service that fails closed; a remediation job that removes a proven-bad pack is not defined). (3) `rustic_core` 0.14.0 panics inside restore (`restore.rs:657`, an `unwrap` on the blob decryption) when a pack holds corrupt data instead of returning an error, and can panic on forged size hints; the worker must contain panics for every job kind and end the job with a failed `JobResult`. |
| W5-1 | Shipping `drive-client.json` (§29 a): a release workflow step that writes it from a repository variable, an entry in privd's update extractor's file list and in the release layout of §6.12, and the installer's check that it is there before the Drive socket is enabled. Until then no install can run the Drive role. |
| W5-2 | **Decided in v0.8 (decision 12): adopted.** `Event{SESSION_FAILED, error_class}`; implemented in `hx_ipc::repo` and in the Drive helper's `server.rs`. |
| W5b | The client commands of §6.14 (`BackupRun`, `BackupStatus` and the rest) over the runner's internal API; the client protocol learning `DRIVE_RECONNECT` and `BACKUP_TAKEN_OVER` (gatewayd forwards only `NeedsAttention` 1 to 7). |
| M3-R | `DestroyDesktop` deletes only what the desktop's records and active job attribute to it (§35). A helper or target that no record attributes (a crash left no record) is left to crash recovery, which runs at privd start: such an object can make a destroy fail (the pool is "in use") until privd restarts. Accepted for v1: the case needs a crash that also lost its record, and an `Uninstall` removes everything. |
| 2C-R | Promotion of a restored target, the deletion of a kept target that is not resumed, and the restore onto a new server (where there is no desktop to copy the target's definition from). W5a keeps and resumes a target (§35); nothing more. |
| W5b-U | The 50% `SetUploadRate` cap while a client is connected, and the `Quota` check before a run (§29). Also: `MemoryMax=` for `hx-privd.service` is not set, because `mlockall` makes a cap a decision of its own; the queue bounds of §25 are what keep privd's memory finite. |
| W5c | `SetBackupCredentials` (sealed token), the recovery key, the `LoadCredentialEncrypted=backup-key` line of the privd unit, enabling `hx-drive.socket` and shipping `drive-client.json` (W5-1). |
| Q-C1 | Whether the worker's `Create`{} operation is needed at all next to `OpenRepo`{`create`}. Until that is settled §30 permits it for `INIT` only. |

### Accepted risks

- `rustic` keeps unzeroized copies of key material in memory. Mitigations: an ephemeral container, swap off, a non-dumpable process.
- A compromised worker in a `FORGET_PRUNE` job can delete packs. A prune helper has no `/src`, so only repository content reaches it; this is accepted.
- A compromised `BACKUP` worker can make `FORGET_PRUNE` fail until the owner acts (a forged snapshot with a missing tree, a junk pack, a very large number of duplicated blobs) and can write forged snapshots that retention would count if privd took its retention from the repository listing. Both are denials of service that fail closed and raise an alert; neither deletes data (§36 decision 10, Q-S1b).
- The worker is PID 1 and must handle `SIGTERM` and reap children (§24).
- Two takeovers of one repository racing each other can both pass the lease check (§29).
- The design depends on Incus internals: unshifted storage and the storage path layout (architecture §15). The mitigation is the guard before each job (§24) and the real-Incus test of the helper probes.

- A compromised `BACKUP` worker can upload arbitrary plaintext (the repository password, desktop files) as `PACK` objects, readable by anyone with access to the Drive folder, and has covert channels in `Progress` numbers and in request ids, offsets and lengths. "A compromised Drive helper cannot decrypt backups" (§23) assumes the worker is not also compromised.
- A forged snapshot from a compromised `BACKUP` worker restores arbitrary root-owned, setuid or file-capability content into the restored desktop: a desktop user becomes desktop root after a restore.

Open item for Phase 2C: `RESTORE` writes are not confined beneath `/dst` against symlinks in a resumed restore (`rustic_core` uses path-based calls). Evaluate a Landlock ruleset for `RESTORE`; it needs a tech-stack decision and is not implemented.

### Where it lives in code (plan, not contract)

`hx-ipc::repo` (new, beside `hx_ipc::privd` and `hx_ipc::link`) holds the frame policy for kinds 1 to 3 on the existing decoder, the `RepoFrame` schema, the data-frame codec, the limit constants, the error enums and a fuzz target. `hx-backup` keeps its pure modules (`rules`, `manifest`, `policy` for §30, `phases` for §33) in the default features, which privd uses without `rustic`; the `engine` feature (`rustic_core`, worker only) holds the engine and, in `engine::link`, the **link backend**: one connection to privd, a reader thread that demultiplexes responses and data frames to the requests waiting for them, a writer thread, the outstanding-request limits of §25 through `LinkState`, and `ReadBackend` and `WriteBackend` on top of it, so the engine runs unchanged against privd. The Drive helper (OpenDAL `gdrive`) is in the worker package, `apps/hx-backup-worker/src/drive/` (decision 11), not behind a feature of `hx-backup`. privd's `privd/backup/` holds the job state machine, the helper and snapshot calls, the relay, the Drive session, progress mapping, crash recovery, the credentials and the recovery-key handling. **As built in v0.8 (W5a)** the modules of `apps/hx-agent/src/privd/backup/` are `slot` (the job slot, `job.json`, `resume.json`, `backoff.json`), `host` (the Incus and systemd calls behind a trait, over `hx-provision`), `recovery` (crash recovery and the teardown cleanup), `worker_link` and `drive_link` (framed sockets: a reader thread and a writer thread per link, no descriptors), `relay` (the job loop: policy, hash check, id mapping, guard timers, cancel, deadlines), `progress` (the mapping of §33), `completed` (the completed-backups record, retention and the `FORGET_PRUNE` spec builder), `spec` (the job specs), `creds` (the backup key reader, §34) and `runner` (admission, the job task, the internal API that W5b wires to §6.14: start, cancel, observe, status). The recovery-key handling is W5c. The engine takes its backend as a trait object and sets the pack-size limits of §28 at `init` (rustic's default is `u32::MAX`).

**The worker is its own package, `apps/hx-backup-worker`** (library `hx_backup_worker` and the bin `hx-backup-worker`), not a bin of `hx-agent`. Cargo unifies the features of one package across all its bins, so a worker bin inside `hx-agent` would put `rustic_core` into the `hx-agent` library that `hx-privd` and `hx-gatewayd` link, and privd and gatewayd must never contain rustic (§23, decision 6). As a separate package the worker is the only dependent of `hx-backup`'s `engine` feature. The rules that keep it so: `hx-agent` has no dependency on `hx-backup` with `engine`; the release workflow builds each package with its own `cargo build -p` (never `--workspace`, which would unify features across packages); and the test `tests/layout.rs` of the worker package fails when `cargo tree -p hx-agent -e normal` lists `rustic_core`, `rustic_backend` or `hx-backup-worker`. The release layout of §6.12 does not change: the five binaries are still collected side by side. The worker's modules are `role` (dispatch and exit codes), `session` (start-up, the one connection, handshake), `sandbox` (capabilities, seccomp, signals; the only `unsafe` of the package), `job` (the job runner of §28) and `progress`. The role is chosen by `HX_BACKUP_ROLE` alone (§6.12): PID 1 with no argument and no variable is the worker; `drive` is the Drive helper (W4); anything else fails closed with exit code 64. The Drive role's modules are `drive::creds` (client file and token), `drive::retry` (classifier, retrier, daily gate), `drive::transport` (host check, permanent delete, rate bucket), `drive::gdrive` (the repository over OpenDAL), `drive::lease`, `drive::server` (the link session) and `drive::run` (activation, one connection at a time, idle exit).
